# DELIA Reproduction - Software Requirements Specification

**Status: DRAFT** (not ready for dev).

**Revision 3 (2026-09-15):** the user's rulings on GAP-60..80 and a correction to GAP-26 are recorded.
- GAP-60..80 are DECIDED. The rulings are IMPLEMENTATION-NECESSARY-BUT-UNSPECIFIED (user-decided). Where the user said so, they are EXPERIMENT-ONLY instead: GAP-67 (beta = 5), GAP-69 (log reader paused while offline) and GAP-78 (1 s blocks).
- GAP-26 is corrected: exactly the root leader signs a no-signed round state. DEV-05 is withdrawn.
- GAP-61 revises the GAP-15 event-only ruling, and DEV-10 is rewritten.
- New deviations DEV-18..20 are in Section 2.5. Documented limitations LIM-01..05 are in Section 2.7.
- Applying the rulings exposed new conflicts and residuals. They are recorded as GAP-81..89 (Section 25.2) and remain OPEN.
- Some values the user required to be fixed without enumerating them are treated as delegated to the implementer and frozen in one normative test-vector file: the one-byte purpose and signer-role codes, message_type codes, the canonical public-key encoding and the on-disk layout. This reading is noted in GAP-73 and awaits user confirmation.

**Revision 2 (2026-09-15):** the user's rulings on the Section 25 decision sheet are recorded.
- Rulings are classified IMPLEMENTATION-NECESSARY-BUT-UNSPECIFIED (user-decided). None is relabelled PAPER-MANDATED.
- Where a ruling departs from the literal paper, it is listed as a documented deviation (Section 2.5).
- Some rulings conflict with each other or leave residual questions. Those were recorded as new GAP entries (GAP-60 to GAP-80); revision 3 records their rulings.
- A requirement stays draft while any gap it references is OPEN or "DECIDED - CONFLICT PENDING" (legend in Section 2.6).

**Sole authoritative source:** J. Chen, X. Chen, K. He, R. Du, W. Chen, Y. Xiang, "DELIA: Distributed Efficient Log Integrity Audit Based on Hierarchal Multi-Party State Channel," *IEEE TDSC* 19(5):3286-3300, Sept./Oct. 2022, DOI 10.1109/TDSC.2021.3092365. File: `systems/delia/DELIA_Distributed_Efficient_Log_Integrity_Audit_Based_on_Hierarchal_Multi-Party_State_Channel.pdf`. No supplementary material or extended version was supplied.

**Citation convention:** "p. NNNN" is the printed journal page. PDF page index = journal page - 3285 (p. 3289 = PDF page 4). "§" = section, "Alg." = Algorithm, "Eq." = equation, "Fig." / "Table" as printed.

**Reading caveat:** subscripts and Greek letters in the PDF's extracted text are garbled. Symbols in this spec were checked against the rendered page images. Where a value was read from a figure (axis ticks, legends), the requirement says so. Those values should be spot-checked against the printed figure.

---

## Notation used in this spec (paper symbols, ASCII-transliterated)

The brief used Latin substitutes. The paper (Table 1, p. 3288) uses the Greek symbols below, and this spec follows the paper.

| Paper symbol (Table 1) | ASCII in this spec | Brief's symbol | Meaning (Table 1) |
|---|---|---|---|
| n | n | n | number of log servers |
| p_i | p_i | p_i | i-th log server |
| δ_{p_i,ξ} | delta_{pi,xi} | d | ξ-th record of the i-th log server |
| ψ_{p_i,l_i} | psi_{pi,li} | c | LogCacheQueue generated by i-th server in l_i-th batch |
| θ | theta | u | number of log records in a batch |
| ω_{p_i,l_i} | omega_{pi,li} | v | hash chain of logs in l_i-th batch of i-th server (LocalState) |
| G_ν | G_nu | G_n | ν-th global state recording all LocalState in a domain |
| G_ν^{Γ_x} | G_nu^{Gx} | - | ν-th global state with signatures from all participants in Γ_x (signed round state) |
| sid | sid | sid | unique identity of a state channel instance |
| Γ | Gamma / Gx | G (instance) | a state channel instance stored in distributed ledger |
| β | beta | b | number of states in a round of HMSC |
| Δ | Delta | D | number of layers in HMSC |

**Warning on the brief's notation:** the brief writes the GlobalState serial number as `n`, which the paper uses for the number of servers. The paper uses ν for the serial number. This spec always uses `nu` for the serial number and `n` for the server count.

---

## 1. DELIA implementation scope

1.1 **In scope:** a faithful reproduction of the DELIA framework as defined in §2-§4 (pp. 3287-3294):
- Data layer: LogCacheQueue, LocalState, GlobalState (§3.2).
- LSG phase: log serialization and state generation, including Alg. 1 and Alg. 2 (§3.3).
- Audit layer: audit initialization, integrity audit with Alg. 3, and audit closure (§3.4).
- HMSC: the six SCC protocols Init (with federation), Confirm, Update, DisputeResolving (with Alg. 4), OfflineResolving and Close/Disassociate (§4.2-§4.3).
- The experimental configuration of §6, specified separately as EXPERIMENT-ONLY (Section 23).

1.2 **Language and platform mapping:**
- The off-chain algorithms and protocols were written in Java in the prototype (§6.1, p. 3296). They must be written in Go. This is an allowed language substitution only: algorithms, message flows, data structures and asymptotic behavior must not change.
- HMSC is "implemented by a smart contract ... pre-deployed on a distributed ledger" (§4.2, p. 3291). The prototype used Solidity on Ethereum 1.8.1 (§6.1). It must remain a Solidity contract on Ethereum. Re-implementing the contract in Go (for example, as a simulated ledger) is a deviation. It must not happen without explicit user approval (see DELIA-SC-002 and GAP-50).

1.3 **Out of scope:**
- HMF-Audit mechanisms of any kind.
- The MSC baseline [23] used for comparison in §6.2.2. Required only if the user wants Fig. 12 reproduced; see DELIA-EXP-024.
- The analytic results of §5 (Theorems 1-5, Eq. (4)-(5)), except where their proofs state a protocol fact. Those facts are cited individually.
- Related work (§7).
- Every item in Section 26.

## 2. Source-of-truth rules

2.1 The PDF above is the only specification. Recollection of DELIA from other sources is not evidence.

2.2 Every requirement carries exactly one classification:
- **PAPER-MANDATED (PM):** the paper explicitly requires or defines it.
- **PAPER-PERMITS (PP):** the paper gives an example or explicitly allows choices.
- **IMPLEMENTATION-NECESSARY-BUT-UNSPECIFIED (IMPL):** software cannot be built without a choice the paper does not make. Always linked to a GAP.
- **EXPERIMENT-ONLY (EXP):** stated only for the §6 prototype or evaluation.

2.3 **Precedence rules:**
- Where an algorithm listing and the prose disagree, neither is silently preferred. The conflict is recorded as a GAP.
- The one exception is a single figure contradicting an equation that several algorithm listings repeat. The spec then notes the equation as governing and still records the conflict (see GAP-02).
- Where the paper is silent, the requirement is IMPL and a GAP is opened. No default is chosen.
- An inefficient procedure is kept as written: recomputation is not cached, and sequential steps are not parallelized.

2.4 **Internal inconsistencies and ambiguities found in the paper.** These are referenced throughout.

| # | Location | Issue |
|---|---|---|
| INC-01 | Fig. 2 vs Eq. (2), Alg. 1, Alg. 3 (pp. 3288-3289) | Fig. 2 draws the LocalState block as `Hash(.) | omega_{p,l-1} | root_{p,l}` and, for l = 0, `Hash(.) | null | root_{p,0}`. Eq. (2), Alg. 1 and Alg. 3 all use `H(root || omega_{l-1})` and `H(root)` for l = 0. |
| INC-02 | §3.3 stage 2 (p. 3289) vs Eq. (2) | The text says MHTGen "outputs the root value root_{pi,li} of the Merkle hash tree as LocalState". Eq. (2) defines LocalState as a hash over the root, not the root itself. |
| INC-03 | §3.3 stage 2 vs §6.2.1 (p. 3296) | Stage 2 says pj's "local G_{nu-1} is replaced by G_nu". §6.2.1 says "every server needs to store all LocalState and GlobalState data for further verifications", and Fig. 10(b) shows GlobalState storage growing linearly with time. |
| INC-04 | §3.2(3) vs §6.2.1 | GlobalState records "the LocalState of all servers in a domain" (§3.2) versus "all LocalState data from the servers in a state channel instance" (§6.2.1). |
| INC-05 | §3.4 stage 2 and Alg. 3 vs §4.3.3 and §4.3.1 | The auditor obtains the contiguous sequence {G_1..G_nu} "from the distributed ledger". But only signed round states are accepted on-chain, no-signed ones stay off-chain (§4.3.3), and Γ stores only "the latest state" G (§4.3.1). |
| INC-06 | Alg. 3 (p. 3289) | Neither k nor l is ever incremented. l is initialized once, before the outer loop, with no reset shown. The algorithm needs l_i for pi, but GlobalState (Eq. (3)) holds only omega values, not batch numbers. |
| INC-07 | Alg. 4 lines 5-8, 11-16, 19-23 (p. 3293) | Each loop decrements before testing, so G_nu^{Gx} itself is never examined, and a counter can reach -1. The ChildList == null branch has no return if no signed state is found. The child-channel loop (lines 19-23) has no break: as written, it ends with k2 = the smallest serial of any signed state in any child. The prose (p. 3293) says "for every child ... find out its latest signed round state. Among these states, we select the earliest one". |
| INC-08 | §4.3.4 (p. 3293) vs §4.3.3 Rule 1 | A DisputeMsg carrying the checkpoint's signed round state is "valid, if State Update Rules are satisfied". Rule 1 requires nu > nu* (the latest state's serial), and a checkpoint is by construction older than the latest state. |
| INC-09 | §4.3.4 (pp. 3293-3294) | After selecting a checkpoint, "the next state received by Gx must be G_{nu+1}" (nu = checkpoint serial), then "we lose nu - nu* states". Here nu and nu* are not defined consistently with their use in §4.3.2 and §4.3.3. |
| INC-10 | §4.3.5 (p. 3294) | OfflineMsg carries n-1 signatures (S_pi omitted), but pi is not yet in OffList. Rule 3 then demands num - numoff = n signatures. |
| INC-11 | §4.3.5 (p. 3294) | OfflineMsg's tag is printed `OFFLINE:(PK_pi, H(psi_{pi,p1}))`; everywhere else it is `H(psi_{pi,li})`. |
| INC-12 | §4.3.5 (p. 3294) | Cancellation is described both as "After the waiting time, if the participant is not actually offline, it could send ... to request cancelling" and "In the waiting time, if the state channel instance does not receive any cancelling message, it adds ...". |
| INC-13 | §4.3.5 intro vs bullets | The steps are named "(1) offline state update; (2) online state update" but the bullets are titled "Offline State Confirmation" and "Online State Confirmation". |
| INC-14 | §4.3.3 Rule 4 vs its explanation (p. 3292) | Rule 4 is stated as an acceptance condition (child minimum serial < nu, or null). The explanation adds a mutation: "If not, the state stored in Gx's child channels who has a bigger serial number should be set null." It is unclear whether a Rule 4 failure rejects the update or triggers the mutation. |
| INC-15 | §4.3.3 Rule 3 (p. 3292) | numoff counts server addresses in OffList. When ChildList is non-null, num counts child instances. The subtraction `num - numoff` mixes the two units. |
| INC-16 | §4.3.2 (p. 3291) vs Table 1 (p. 3288) | Table 1 defines G_nu^{Gx} as having "signatures from all participants in Gx". §4.3.2 and §4.3.4 say parent-level signed round states carry signatures of child-channel leaders only. |
| INC-17 | §4.3.2 stage 3 (p. 3292) vs §5 Thm. 3 proof (p. 3295) | §4.3.2 says a no-signed round state "only needs the signature of the leader" and that the instance leader "can still conduct beta no-signed round states". Thm. 3's proof says "only the leader of root state channel instance p_rld will sign it". The §4.3.2 approval paragraph has p1 (the root leader) sign. |
| INC-18 | §4.3.2 stage 3 (p. 3292) | The approval condition "nu* < nu + beta" uses nu, which that paragraph never defines. |
| INC-19 | §4.3.2 stage 1 vs Fig. 8 (p. 3292) | Fig. 8 shows p1 as leader of root Γ3 but not of its own child Γ1, whose leader is p2. Γ3's P contains every child participant (§4.3.1), so the minimum-PK rule would pick the same key in Γ3 and in whichever child holds it. The rule does not say whether a parent leader must be one of the child leaders. |
| INC-20 | §4.2 (p. 3291) vs §6.2.2 (p. 3297), Fig. 6 | The SCC "includes six protocols", including Confirm. §6.2.2 lists five on-chain protocols (Init, Update, DisputeResolving, OfflineResolving, Close), and Fig. 6 marks State Confirmation as off-chain. |
| INC-21 | §4.3.1 vs §5 Thm. 4 proof and §6.2.2 | §4.3.1: "The participants could send a message InitMsg". Thm. 4 proof: "each pi should send InitMsg containing all other participants' public keys". §6.2.2: "every server ... has to submit its request". |
| INC-22 | §4.3.3, §4.3.4, §4.3.5 | Participants are identified by public key in P but by "address" in OffList ("number of addresses stored in OffList"), NewLeader ("its address is stored") and OnlineMsg handling ("removes the address"). |
| INC-23 | §4.3.6 (p. 3294) | Closing the root requires "signatures from all participants of root state channel instance", while signed round states at parent level carry child-leader signatures (INC-16). The DisassociateMsg child states are written `(G_{nu_ci}|Close)^{Ga}` in one place and `G_nu^{G1}..G_nu^{Gm}` in the next. |
| INC-24 | §6.2.4 (p. 3298) | The voting-mechanism results say "the offline resolving protocol just involves the servers who have the same leader" and "effectively support our offline resolving protocol". The voting mechanism is defined only in the dispute resolving protocol (§4.3.4). |
| INC-25 | §6.2.1 (p. 3296) vs §6.1 | "15 servers run for 10 hours generating 2,178,000 log records". At the stated rate of up to 2420 items/minute, 2,178,000 records take 900 minutes (15 h). |
| INC-26 | Fig. 11(a) vs Table 2, Fig. 10(a), Fig. 14(a) | Fig. 11(a)'s theta axis reads 50/100/200/300 (from the figure); the other theta sweeps use 50/100/200/500. |
| INC-27 | §3.1 (p. 3288) | HMSC is expanded as "Hierarchal Efficient Multi-party State Channel", elsewhere "Hierarchal Multi-party State Channel". Cosmetic. |
| INC-28 | §2.1 (p. 3287) vs §3.4 stage 2 (p. 3290) | The auditor audits "automatically in cycles" versus "At any time, an external auditor could access the audit phase". |
| INC-29 | §6.1 (p. 3296) | "The algorithms and protocols in DELIA are coded by Java" and servers talk to HMSC "by web3js APIs" (a JavaScript library). The bridge between the two is not described. |

### 2.5 Documented deviations (user-decided)

Each entry is a user ruling that departs from the literal paper text. Deviations are IMPLEMENTATION-NECESSARY-BUT-UNSPECIFIED (user-decided), never paper-mandated. The paper text they depart from is still recorded in the cited requirement.

| DEV | Ruling | Paper text departed from | Affected requirements |
|---|---|---|---|
| DEV-01 | Alg. 3 counters: k++ after each G_k; l++ per reconstructed batch; l not reset per k (single linear pass) | Alg. 3, p. 3289: no increments shown; l initialized once (INC-06) | AUDIT-004..007 (GAP-14) |
| DEV-02 | Alg. 4 implemented per prose and Fig. 9, not the printed listing | Alg. 4 lines 5-8, 11-16, 19-23, p. 3293 (INC-07) | DISPUTE-005..009 (GAP-34) |
| DEV-03 | OfflineMsg: the pending-offline server is excluded from the expected signer set, so n-1 signatures satisfy Rule 3 for OfflineMsg only | §4.3.3 Rule 3, p. 3292; §4.3.5, p. 3294 (INC-10) | OFFLINE-005/006, UPDATE-006 (GAP-42) |
| DEV-04 | DisputeMsg: the State Update Rules are not applied to the checkpoint state. SCC verifies only the checkpoint state's own signatures plus the dispute vote evidence, and the majority-certified result determines the checkpoint (revision 3: GAP-64, GAP-71). Revision 2 skipped only Rule 1. | §4.3.4, p. 3293 ("valid, if State Update Rules are satisfied") (INC-08) | DISPUTE-010, NET-017 (GAP-33, GAP-64, GAP-71) |
| DEV-05 | WITHDRAWN (revision 3). Revision 2 ruling: a no-signed state carried one signature, from the originating instance's leader. The user's correction to GAP-26 makes the root leader the only signer, as §4.3.2 stage 3 and the Thm. 3 proof state. No deviation remains; the row is kept for history. | None now. Revision 2 departed from §4.3.2 stage 3, p. 3292 and the Thm. 3 proof, p. 3295. | CONFIRM-005, 014, 015, 017; NET-012 (GAP-26) |
| DEV-06 | Rule 4 is not a rejection criterion. A valid parent update is accepted, and direct children's stored G with serial > accepted serial is set null; no recursion. | §4.3.3 Rule 4 stated as a condition, p. 3292 (INC-14) | UPDATE-009/010 (GAP-31) |
| DEV-07 | An LSG-verified G_nu is a provisional proposal until HMSC confirmation. A losing proposal is regenerated from the newly accepted state. | §3.3 stage 2, p. 3289 ("its local G_{nu-1} is replaced by G_nu") | LSG-007, 010, 012 (GAP-11) |
| DEV-08 | No separate log collector, log preprocessor or integrity verification components. The log reader feeds LSG; verification is Alg. 2/3 code. | §3.4 stages 1 and 3, p. 3290 | SYS-007, CLOSE-008 (GAP-18) |
| DEV-09 | Audits run periodically with a configured period; on-demand audit is a test/CLI trigger only | §3.4 stage 2, p. 3290 ("At any time, an external auditor could access") | AUDIT-002, SYS-004 (GAP-17) |
| DEV-10 | Revised in revision 3 (GAP-61). The auditor obtains the complete sequential GlobalState history {G_1..G_nu} off-chain from a server. It verifies each no-signed state by its root-leader signature and serial continuity, and cross-checks every signed-round anchor against Ethereum event logs. The revision 2 ruling (only signed anchors, from event logs) is superseded. | §3.4 stage 2, p. 3290 ("the states {G_1, ..., G_nu} from the distributed ledger") (INC-05) | AUDIT-001, 005, 011, 012; SYS-004; NET-032, NET-037 (GAP-15, GAP-61) |
| DEV-11 | Leader rotation skips ineligible participants: those in the finalized on-chain OffList (GAP-79) and those excluded by a dispute vote (GAP-65) | §4.3.2 stage 1, pp. 3291-3292 (rotation over all of P) | CONFIRM-003/004, UPDATE-008 (GAP-22, GAP-60, GAP-65, GAP-79) |
| DEV-12 | OnlineMsg does not require S_pi. The rejoining server syncs from its direct leader rather than "other servers in the domain". | §4.3.5 OnlineMsg tuple (S_p1, ..., S_pn), p. 3294; §3.3 stage 5, p. 3290 | OFFLINE-013, 015 (GAP-44) |
| DEV-13 | GlobalGen applies the Eq. (2) li = 0 branch, which is absent from the Alg. 1 listing | Alg. 1, p. 3289 | ALG1-005 (GAP-07) |
| DEV-14 | Malicious non-leader participants are detected and rejected only; no exclusion protocol is built | §4.3.4, p. 3292 ("easy to be excluded because the leader could detect") | DISPUTE-001 (GAP-39) |
| DEV-15 | Confirm has no contract function | §4.2, p. 3291 (SCC "includes six protocols") (INC-20) | ARCH-001/002, SC-004 (GAP-57) |
| DEV-16 (conditional) | If period tooling (Geth compatible with 1.8.1, period Solidity compiler) cannot run reliably, a newer tool version may be used with contract semantics unchanged. This is labelled an environment substitution, not a protocol deviation. | §6.1, p. 3296 ("Ethereum 1.8.1") | SC-002, SC-006, EXP-001 (GAP-50) |
| DEV-17 | H(psi_{pi,li}) in OfflineMsg is computed over the current active LogCacheQueue, including a partial batch | Eq. (1), p. 3288 defines psi_{pi,li} as exactly theta hashes (a complete batch); §4.3.5, p. 3294 | OFFLINE-003, OFFLINE-005 (GAP-42) |
| DEV-18 | Rule 3 for a parent instance: num - numoff child-leader signatures, plus the parent leader's signature counted separately when the parent leader is not one of those child leaders. A parent signed state may therefore carry num - numoff + 1 signatures. | §4.3.3 Rule 3, p. 3292 ("G_nu^{Gx} contains num - numoff signatures") | UPDATE-003, 006, 008 (GAP-30, GAP-68) |
| DEV-19 | Offline finalization is a separate, permissionless FinalizeOffline transaction sent after the one-block challenge interval. The user classifies this as implementation-necessary: a contract cannot act without a transaction. | §4.3.5, p. 3294 ("In the waiting time, if the state channel instance does not receive any cancelling message, it adds PK_pi and H(psi_{pi,li}) to its own OffList") | OFFLINE-007, 009; SC-004; NET-036 (GAP-43, GAP-63) |
| DEV-20 | Rule 1: nu* = the serial of the latest signed state stored on-chain in the root instance. The user classifies this as the nearest enforceable reading, because SCC cannot see later off-chain no-signed states. | §4.3.3 Rule 1, p. 3292 (nu* is "the serial number of the latest state conducted by the root channel's leader") | UPDATE-004, 005; OFFLINE-010 (GAP-29, GAP-66) |

### 2.6 Gap status legend (used in every "Gap" / "Open gap?" cell)

| Tag | Meaning | Requirement readiness |
|---|---|---|
| (D) | DECIDED by user ruling; no pending conflict | not blocked by this gap |
| (D; residual GAP-nn) | Decided, but a sub-point is still open in GAP-nn | blocked by GAP-nn |
| (CP: GAP-nn, ...) | DECIDED - CONFLICT PENDING; the ruling conflicts with another ruling or with the paper, recorded in GAP-nn | blocked |
| (OPEN) | Not answered by the user | blocked |
| (NND) | No decision needed | not blocked |

### 2.7 Documented limitations (revision 3)

The paper leaves these behaviors undefined, and the user ruled that they stay undefined rather than be invented. Each is recorded here, not fixed.

| LIM | Limitation | Why it is not fixed | Ruling |
|---|---|---|---|
| LIM-01 | A real deployment has no defined way to ingest log records a server generates while offline. The reproduction avoids the case by pausing that server's log reader while it is offline (EXPERIMENT-ONLY). | The paper defines no backlog ingestion, and adding one would invent a protocol. | GAP-69 |
| LIM-02 | State signatures are not bound to sid. A signature made in one instance can be replayed in another instance that confirms the same domain-wide state with the same purpose and signer role. | The paper does not bind state signatures to sid, and GAP-09 keeps sid out of the signed bytes. | GAP-72 |
| LIM-03 | A graceful Close needs signatures from every member of the instance's P, including offline ones, so a single offline participant can prevent it. | §4.3.6 says the root close state carries signatures from all participants. | GAP-76 |
| LIM-04 | Direct nested disassociation is not supported. A parent that itself has a parent must first be detached from that parent. | The paper does not define how an ancestor's ChildList would be repaired. | GAP-76 |
| LIM-05 | An excluded participant can rejoin only if membership is explicitly re-established, and no re-establishment protocol exists. Its GlobalState slot stays frozen. | The paper defines exclusion but no readmission. | GAP-65 |

## 3. System entities

| ID | Requirement | Class | Source | Acceptance criterion | Gap |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| DELIA-SYS-001 | The system has exactly three kinds of entities: (a) servers, each with an LSG module and an HMSC module; (b) an auditor; (c) a distributed ledger maintained by ledger nodes. | PM | §2.1, Fig. 1, p. 3287 | The deployment contains only these entity kinds. No coordinator, broker, database server or cloud provider entity exists. | - |
| DELIA-SYS-002 | All participants in all entities are authenticated and authorized and form an alliance in the same domain. | PM | §2.1, p. 3287 | A non-authorized party cannot participate in LSG broadcasts or HMSC instances. User-decided: servers authenticate with mutual TLS from a private test CA (GAP-48); P comes from experiment config, with one secp256k1 key per participant generated at provisioning (GAP-49). | GAP-48 (D), GAP-49 (D) |
| DELIA-SYS-003 | A server updates audit logs, interacts with other servers in the domain, and runs: LSG (Log Serialization, State Generation) and HMSC (State Confirmation, State Update, Offline-Processing). | PM | §2.1, Fig. 1, p. 3287; §3.3, p. 3289 | Each server process exposes these five functions and no other protocol roles. | - |
| DELIA-SYS-004 | The auditor can check ("censor") all audit logs, local state information and the HMSC instance in the distributed ledger. It performs Integrity Audit and Auditor Forensics (Fig. 1). | PM | §2.1, Fig. 1, p. 3287; §3.4 stage 2, p. 3290 | The auditor can read logs from any server, the complete GlobalState history from a server, and signed-round anchors from Ethereum event logs, which it uses to cross-check that history (GAP-61, DEV-10). User-decided: audits run periodically with a configured period; on-demand is a test/CLI trigger only (DEV-09). The auditor authenticates with a mutual-TLS client certificate (GAP-77). | GAP-15 (D; revised by GAP-61), GAP-61 (CP: GAP-81; residual GAP-83), GAP-17 (D), GAP-77 (D) |
| DELIA-SYS-005 | The distributed ledger pre-deploys the HMSC contract and maintains submitted states in HMSC instances via all ledger nodes. | PM | §2.1, p. 3287; §4.2, p. 3291 | The contract is deployed before any Init. Instance state is readable from any ledger node. | - |
| DELIA-SYS-006 | DELIA is organized in three layers: data (LogCacheQueue, LocalState, GlobalState), network (LSG, HMSC, distributed ledger protocol) and audit (Integrity Audit, Auditor Forensics). | PM | §3.1, Fig. 3, p. 3288 | The module structure maps onto these layers (see Section 21). | - |
| DELIA-SYS-007 | At audit initialization each server enables a log collector, a log preprocessor and an integrity verification module. At audit closure these are closed. | PM | §3.4 stages 1 and 3, p. 3290 | User-decided (DEV-08): no separate collector, preprocessor or verifier components. "Enable" = starting the log reader that feeds LSG; "close" = stopping it, coupled with Init and Close. Verification is the Alg. 2/3 code. | GAP-18 (D) |
| DELIA-SYS-008 | Server roles within an HMSC instance are "participant" and, per round, "leader". Across the hierarchy there are child-instance leaders and a root-instance leader. | PM | §4.1-§4.3.2, pp. 3290-3292, Fig. 7, Fig. 8 | Role assignment follows DELIA-HMSC-CONFIRM-003. No other roles exist. | GAP-23 (D), GAP-68 (CP: GAP-87) |

## 4. Threat model and assumptions

| ID | Requirement | Class | Source | Acceptance criterion | Gap |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| DELIA-THREAT-001 | Network monitoring attack: attackers can eavesdrop on, delete or modify data between log servers and ledger nodes. | PM | §2.2, p. 3287 | Security test: a modified on-chain submission fails signature/rule checks (S1, Thm. 2). | - |
| DELIA-THREAT-002 | Compromise attack: attackers compromise only a minority of servers. Honest set H satisfies abs(H) > n/2. | PM | §2.2, p. 3287; Thm. 3 and Thm. 4, p. 3295 | Test scenarios never assume a compromised majority. | - |
| DELIA-THREAT-003 | Sybil attack: attackers may use multiple forged identities. The defence is identity validation via pre-negotiated P at Init. | PM | §2.2; Thm. 4 proof, p. 3295 | An identity not in P cannot produce an accepted signature for an instance. | - |
| DELIA-THREAT-004 | DoS attack: may be launched at any time after initialization and may block access to the ledger. DoS on minority servers must not stop the system; logs of a DoS-ed server must not be tampered (S4). | PM | §2.2; Thm. 5 and following paragraph, pp. 3295-3296 | With one server unreachable, OfflineResolving proceeds and that server's LocalState stays frozen (DELIA-HMSC-OFFLINE-010). | GAP-41 (D) |
| DELIA-THREAT-005 | Cryptographic algorithms are assumed secure: attackers cannot forge or tamper with signatures without keys. | PM | §2.2, p. 3287 | No requirement relies on anything beyond this assumption. | - |
| DELIA-THREAT-006 | Security goals S1-S4: network monitoring, compromise, Sybil and DoS attackers cannot affect the update of G_nu (signed and no-signed). | PM | §5 "Security Goals", p. 3295 | Tests TEST-23..TEST-28, TEST-32, TEST-36 exercise S1-S4 mechanisms. | - |
| DELIA-THREAT-007 | Design goals: mutual supervision; state stability; low latency independent of participant count absent dispute; offline process. No numeric targets are stated. | PM | §2.3, pp. 3287-3288 | No performance acceptance threshold is derived from the design goals. | - |

## 5. Data structures

### 5.1 LogCacheQueue

| ID | Requirement | Class | Source | Acceptance criterion | Gap |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| DELIA-DATA-001 | Logs are processed in batches of a fixed number theta of records, each treated as a whole. theta is a global parameter, identical on all servers. | PM | §3.2(1), Eq. (1), Table 1, p. 3288 | All servers use one configured theta. A batch completes exactly when its theta-th record is generated. | - |
| DELIA-DATA-002 | LogCacheQueue of server pi for batch li is `psi_{pi,li} = { H(delta_{pi, li*theta+1}), ..., H(delta_{pi, (li+1)*theta}) }`. | PM | Eq. (1), p. 3288 | For li = 0, psi contains the hashes of records 1..theta. For li = 1, records theta+1..2*theta. User-decided: each element is SHA-256 over the record's UTF-8 line bytes excluding line-ending bytes (GAP-03); psi holds exactly theta digests in record order, duplicates kept (GAP-04). | GAP-03 (D), GAP-04 (D) |
| DELIA-DATA-003 | li is a non-negative integer batch number per server, starting at 0. | PM | Eq. (1) text; Eq. (2) li = 0 branch, p. 3288 | The first batch of every server has li = 0. | - |
| DELIA-DATA-004 | Record index xi is per server and starts at 1. | PM | Eq. (1) with li = 0; Alg. 3 input `delta_{pi,1}`, p. 3289 | The first generated record of pi is delta_{pi,1}. | - |
| DELIA-DATA-005 | The paper calls LogCacheQueue a "local hash set, which stores different hash values of each server's log records in a batch". Element ordering (needed as Merkle leaf order) and the handling of two equal hash values (set semantics would drop duplicates) are unspecified. | IMPL (user-decided) | §3.2(1), p. 3288 | Ruling (GAP-04 (a)): psi is an ordered sequence in xi order of exactly theta 32-byte digests; duplicates kept; no deduplication. Test: a batch with two identical log lines has theta elements, with the same digest at both positions. | GAP-04 (D) |
| DELIA-DATA-006 | Every server pj maintains `{psi_{p1,l1}, ..., psi_{pn,ln}}`, the LogCacheQueue of every server in the domain, including its own. | PM | §3.3 stage 1, p. 3289 | After all broadcasts for the current batches, each server holds a psi for every server, and those psi agree across servers. User-decided: domain-wide scope (GAP-10); only current-batch psi is retained (GAP-13). | GAP-10 (D), GAP-13 (D) |
| DELIA-DATA-007 | The value of theta for a deployment is not fixed by the protocol. The experiments use 50/100/200/500 (and 300 in Fig. 11(a)); these are not protocol constants. | IMPL (user-decided) | Eq. (1) ("global parameter"); §6.2, Table 2, Figs. 10, 11, 14 | Ruling (GAP-55): theta is required configuration with no global default. A node started without theta refuses to run. | GAP-55 (D), GAP-67 (D) |

### 5.2 Merkle tree / LocalState

| ID | Requirement | Class | Source | Acceptance criterion | Gap |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| DELIA-DATA-008 | `MHTGen(psi)` takes a LogCacheQueue and outputs root_{pi,li}, the root of the Merkle hash tree constructed from psi. | PM | §3.2(2), p. 3288; §3.3 stage 2, p. 3289; Fig. 2 | MHTGen is deterministic. Changing any element of psi changes the root. Construction per DATA-009 ruling. | GAP-01 (D), GAP-74 (D) |
| DELIA-DATA-009 | Merkle tree internals: leaf values (the psi elements as-is vs re-hashed), leaf order, odd-leaf and non-power-of-two handling, internal-node input layout, domain separation. Fig. 2 only shows a 4-leaf binary example (Hash 0-0, 0-1, 1-0, 1-1 -> Hash 0, Hash 1 -> root). | IMPL (user-decided) | Fig. 2, p. 3288 (example only) | Ruling (GAP-01 (a)): leaves = the psi digests in record order (not re-hashed); parent = SHA-256(left \|\| right) over raw 32-byte digests; at any level with an odd node count the last node is duplicated; no domain separation. Test vector, theta = 3: root = H(H(L1\|\|L2) \|\| H(L3\|\|L3)). theta = 1: root = the single leaf; H(leaf \|\| leaf) is computed only when a node really has two children (GAP-74). | GAP-01 (D), GAP-74 (D) |
| DELIA-DATA-010 | LocalState for li = 0: `omega_{pi,0} = H(root_{pi,0})`. | PM | Eq. (2), p. 3288 | TEST-03. User-decided (GAP-02): H input is the raw 32-byte root. | GAP-02 (D) |
| DELIA-DATA-011 | LocalState for li > 0: `omega_{pi,li} = H(root_{pi,li} \|\| omega_{pi,li-1})`, with root first and the previous LocalState second. | PM | Eq. (2), p. 3288; Alg. 1 and Alg. 3, p. 3289 | TEST-04. User-decided (GAP-02): H input is the raw 64 bytes root \|\| omega_{li-1}; the Eq. (2) order governs over Fig. 2. | GAP-02 (D) |
| DELIA-DATA-012 | LocalState is a hash chain linking LogCacheQueue data in batch order, binding all previous batches of pi. | PM | §3.2(2), p. 3288 | Changing any record in batch b changes omega_{pi,l} for every l >= b. | - |

### 5.3 GlobalState

| ID | Requirement | Class | Source | Acceptance criterion | Gap |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| DELIA-DATA-013 | GlobalState is an array with one LocalState per server: `G_nu = (omega_{p1,l1}, ..., omega_{pn,ln})`, position i holding pi's current LocalState. | PM | §3.2(3), Eq. (3), p. 3289 | A GlobalState has exactly n entries. User-decided: positions follow the P order frozen at Init; no li field is added (GAP-08); the state is domain-wide (GAP-10). An excluded participant's slot keeps its position, frozen at the rollback checkpoint; positions are never deleted or re-indexed (GAP-65). | GAP-08 (D), GAP-10 (D), GAP-65 (D) |
| DELIA-DATA-014 | nu is the GlobalState serial number identifying the snapshot. A newly generated state has serial = previous serial + 1. | PM | Eq. (3) text, p. 3289; Alg. 1 (G_{nu-1} -> G_nu); §4.3.2 stage 3 ("nu' is equal to nu*+1"), p. 3292 | TEST-05: the serial of Alg. 1's output = input serial + 1. User-decided: nu = 0 is G_0, the first generated state is G_1 (GAP-06); nu is bound into the compared and signed encoding (GAP-09). | GAP-06 (D), GAP-09 (D), GAP-73 (D) |
| DELIA-DATA-015 | A new GlobalState is produced when one server's batch fills. It differs from G_{nu-1} only at that server's position. | PM | §3.3 stage 2; Alg. 1, p. 3289 | TEST-05: all positions except i are identical to G_{nu-1}. | - |
| DELIA-DATA-016 | The initial GlobalState, its serial number, and the entry for a server that has not completed batch 0 are unspecified. | IMPL (user-decided) | Not stated (Eq. (3), Alg. 1 assume G_{nu-1} exists) | Ruling (GAP-06): G_0 = n positions, each the all-zero 32-byte digest ("no LocalState yet"), serial nu = 0. The first generated state is G_1. A server's slot stays zero until its first batch. G_0 is not submitted on-chain. Test: at startup every node's latest state is (0, [0x00 x 32] x n) and no transaction is sent for it. The zero digest is only an initialization sentinel (G_0[i] = 0^256), never treated as a LocalState produced from logs; Alg. 3 skips the comparison for a server until its first completed batch (GAP-75). | GAP-06 (D), GAP-75 (D) |
| DELIA-DATA-017 | Whether a GlobalState spans all servers in the domain (§3.2(3)) or the servers of one state channel instance (§6.2.1), and how it relates to the hierarchy, is unresolved (INC-04). | IMPL (user-decided) | §3.2(3), p. 3289; §6.2.1, p. 3296 | Ruling (GAP-10 (a)): one domain-wide G_nu containing every server's LocalState; every instance confirms and propagates that same state. Test: all instances sign byte-identical state encodings for a given nu. An excluded participant's slot stays in the domain-wide layout, frozen at the rollback checkpoint (GAP-65). | GAP-10 (D), GAP-65 (D) |
| DELIA-DATA-018 | Byte encodings are unspecified for: a log record delta before hashing; hash values; the `\|\|` concatenation; GlobalState for comparison, transmission and signing. | IMPL (user-decided) | Not stated | Rulings:<br>- hashes are raw 32-byte SHA-256 digests, not hex (GAP-02)<br>- a record is its UTF-8 line without line-ending bytes, unparsed (GAP-03)<br>- fixed-size values are concatenated as raw bytes; structured objects get one deterministic binary encoding first (GAP-27)<br>- GlobalState = canonical encoding of (nu, ordered entries), with no sid (GAP-09)<br>Layout (GAP-73): GlobalState = uint64 nu (big-endian) \|\| uint32 entry_count (big-endian) \|\| entry_count x 32-byte LocalState in the P order frozen at Init; signed bytes = purpose \|\| signer_role \|\| encoded state, each prefix one byte (GAP-72, GAP-73); public keys use one fixed canonical encoding throughout. | GAP-02 (D), GAP-03 (D), GAP-09 (D), GAP-72 (D), GAP-73 (D) |

There is one broadcast per record, sent immediately, not batched. | PM | §3.3 stage 1, p. 3289; Thm. 3 proof ("should be broadcasted immediately"), p. 3295 | TEST-10: for k records generated, k hash broadcasts are observed, each sent before the (xi+1)-th record's broadcast. | GAP-05 (OPEN) |
| DELIA-LSG-003 | When pj receives `H(delta_{pi,xi})`, it stores the item in its local LogCacheQueue for pi. | PM | §3.3 stage 1, p. 3289 | TEST-01. | GAP-04 (D), GAP-05 (OPEN) |
| DELIA-LSG-004 | pi also stores `H(delta_{pi,xi})` in its own local LogCacheQueue. | PM | §3.3 stage 1, p. 3289 | TEST-01. | - |
| DELIA-LSG-005 | The record-hash broadcast payload does not specify: sender identity, xi, li, or how pj places the hash when messages arrive out of order or are missing. | IMPL | Not stated | Blocked until GAP-05 is decided (not answered by the user). Framing is decided (GAP-47); payload fields and gap handling are not. | GAP-05 (OPEN) |
| DELIA-LSG-006 | State generation is triggered when pi has generated theta records in its li-th batch (batch full). pi then generates a new GlobalState G_nu. | PM | §3.3 stage 2, p. 3289 | No GlobalState is generated for a partial batch. | - |
| DELIA-LSG-007 | pi invokes `MHTGen(psi_{pi,li})` to get root_{pi,li}, invokes Alg. 1 with root_{pi,li} and G_{nu-1} (the latest GlobalState) to get G_nu, then broadcasts `(root_{pi,li}, G_nu)` to the other servers. | PM | §3.3 stage 2, p. 3289 | TEST-09. User-decided (DEV-07): the broadcast G_nu is a provisional proposal until HMSC confirmation. | GAP-07 (D), GAP-09 (D), GAP-11 (D) |
| DELIA-LSG-008 | On receiving `(root_{pi,li}, G_nu)`, pj calls Alg. 2 with its own locally maintained psi_{pi,li} and G_{nu-1}. | PM | §3.3 stage 2, p. 3289 | pj never uses a psi or G_{nu-1} supplied by pi. | - |
| DELIA-LSG-009 | If Alg. 2 returns invalid (0), pj broadcasts a failure notification and terminates this stage. | PM | §3.3 stage 2, p. 3289 | TEST-09: an invalid input makes pj broadcast a failure notification, and pj does not replace G_{nu-1}. User-decided (GAP-12 (a)): the notification carries (sender, pi, nu, reason) and is broadcast to the servers in the domain; recipients only record it; the state-generation attempt is aborted; no dispute is invoked; processing may resume after resync. | GAP-12 (D) |
| DELIA-LSG-010 | If Alg. 2 returns valid (1), pj's local G_{nu-1} is replaced by G_nu. | PM | §3.3 stage 2, p. 3289 | TEST-09: after a valid verification, pj's latest GlobalState = G_nu. User-decided: the adopted G_nu is provisional until HMSC confirmation (GAP-11, DEV-07); G_{nu-1} is kept in history, not discarded (GAP-13). | GAP-11 (D), GAP-13 (D) |
| DELIA-LSG-011 | If Alg. 2 passes, all servers try to reach consensus on G_nu via the HMSC state confirmation protocol (stage 3). | PM | §3.3 stage 3, p. 3289 | A validated G_nu is handed to Confirm (Section 12). | GAP-11 (D) |
| DELIA-LSG-012 | Behavior is unspecified when two servers complete batches concurrently and each derives a different G_nu from the same G_{nu-1}. §4.1 names this "state conflict" and assigns it to HMSC's leader, but the LSG stage defines no rule. | IMPL (user-decided) | §4.1, Fig. 5, p. 3290 (problem stated); §3.3 (no rule) | Ruling (GAP-11 (b), DEV-07): LSG proposals are provisional until HMSC confirmation orders/accepts one. The losing proposer re-runs Alg. 1 against the newly accepted latest G and proposes again; no merge. Test TEST-43. | GAP-11 (D) |

## 7. Algorithm 1 requirements (Global State Generation)

Listing as printed (Alg. 1, p. 3289):
```
procedure GlobalGen(root_{pi,li}, G_{nu-1})
  Parse G_{nu-1} as (omega_{p1,l1}, ..., omega_{pi,li-1}, ..., omega_{pn,ln});
  omega_{pi,li} <- H(root_{pi,li} || omega_{pi,li-1});        > Eq. (2)
  G_nu <- (omega_{p1,l1}, ..., omega_{pi,li}, ..., omega_{pn,ln});   > Eq. (3)
  return G_nu;
```

| ID | Requirement | Class | Source | Acceptance criterion | Gap |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| DELIA-ALG1-001 | GlobalGen takes (root_{pi,li}, G_{nu-1}) and returns G_nu. | PM | Alg. 1, p. 3289 | Function signature matches, plus the position index i (ALG1-006). | GAP-08 (D), GAP-65 (D) |
| DELIA-ALG1-002 | Step 1: parse G_{nu-1} into its n LocalState entries. Entry i is omega_{pi,li-1}. | PM | Alg. 1 line 1 | Parsing uses the GAP-73 layout: uint64 nu, uint32 entry_count, then entry_count 32-byte entries. | GAP-09 (D), GAP-73 (D) |
| DELIA-ALG1-003 | Step 2: compute `omega_{pi,li} = H(root_{pi,li} \|\| omega_{pi,li-1})`. This is computed fresh on every call, with no cache. | PM | Alg. 1 line 2; Eq. (2) | TEST-05. | GAP-02 (D) |
| DELIA-ALG1-004 | Step 3: build G_nu by copying every entry of G_{nu-1} and replacing entry i with omega_{pi,li}. Return G_nu. | PM | Alg. 1 lines 3-4; Eq. (3) | TEST-05. | - |
| DELIA-ALG1-005 | Alg. 1 always chains with omega_{pi,li-1}. It does not show the Eq. (2) li = 0 branch (`H(root_{pi,0})`). Behavior for li = 0 is not stated in the listing. | IMPL (user-decided) | Alg. 1 vs Eq. (2) | Ruling (GAP-07 (a), DEV-13): GlobalGen applies Eq. (2) in full, omega = H(root) when li = 0. The missing branch is treated as an omission in the listing. Test TEST-05b: a server's first batch (zero slot) yields H(root), not H(root \|\| zero digest). | GAP-07 (D) |
| DELIA-ALG1-006 | Alg. 1's inputs do not identify i (which server's position to replace). It must come from context (the generating server), but no parameter is defined. | IMPL (user-decided) | Alg. 1 | Ruling (GAP-08): i is the generating server's index in the P order frozen at Init, supplied by the caller. Test TEST-05. | GAP-08 (D) |

## 8. Algorithm 2 requirements (State Verification)

Listing as printed (Alg. 2, p. 3289; the printed `root'_{pi,lj}` subscript is read as `root'_{pi,li}`):
```
procedure StateVerify(psi_{pi}, root_{pi,li}, G_{nu-1}, G_nu)
  root'_{pi,li} <- MHTGen(psi_{pi});
  if root'_{pi,li} != root_{pi,li} then return 0;        > invalid root
  G'_nu <- GlobalGen(root_{pi,li}, G_{nu-1})
  if G'_nu != G_nu then return 0;                        > invalid global state
  return 1;                                              > valid global state
```

| ID | Requirement | Class | Source | Acceptance criterion | Gap |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| DELIA-ALG2-001 | StateVerify takes (psi_{pi}, root_{pi,li}, G_{nu-1}, G_nu) and returns 0 or 1. | PM | Alg. 2, p. 3289 | Return type is binary. | - |
| DELIA-ALG2-002 | Step 1: recompute root' = MHTGen(psi_{pi}) from the verifier's local psi on every call. No stored root is reused. | PM | Alg. 2 line 1; §3.3 stage 2 ("maintained locally on pj") | Instrumentation shows exactly one MHTGen call per StateVerify call. | GAP-01 (D), GAP-74 (D) |
| DELIA-ALG2-003 | Step 2: if root' != root_{pi,li}, return 0 immediately, before GlobalGen is called. | PM | Alg. 2 line 2 | TEST-07: with a mismatching root, GlobalGen is not invoked and the result is 0. | - |
| DELIA-ALG2-004 | Step 3: compute G'_nu = GlobalGen(root_{pi,li}, G_{nu-1}), using Alg. 1 as the subroutine. | PM | Alg. 2 line 3 | Implemented by calling the Alg. 1 function, not a separate reimplementation. | - |
| DELIA-ALG2-005 | Step 4: if G'_nu != G_nu, return 0. | PM | Alg. 2 line 4 | TEST-08. | GAP-09 (D), GAP-73 (D) |
| DELIA-ALG2-006 | Step 5: otherwise return 1. | PM | Alg. 2 line 5 | TEST-06. | - |
| DELIA-ALG2-007 | What "G'_nu != G_nu" compares is unspecified: the entries only, or also the serial nu, and in which encoding. | IMPL (user-decided) | Not stated | Ruling (GAP-09 (a)): equality compares the canonical encodings of (nu, ordered entries); nu is part of the comparison; sid is not included. Test TEST-08(c): same entries, different nu -> 0. Encodings per GAP-73. | GAP-09 (D), GAP-73 (D) |

## 9. Algorithm 3 / audit requirements (Log Verification)

Listing as printed (Alg. 3, p. 3289). Indentation is as it appears on the rendered page; see GAP-14.
```
procedure LogVerify({delta_{pi,1}, ..., delta_{pi,xi}}, {G_1, ..., G_nu})
  ErrList <- {}                  > batches in which audit log is deleted/forged/tampered
  k <- 1, l <- 0;
  for k <= nu do
    Parse G_k as (omega_{p1,l1}, ..., omega_{pi,li}, ..., omega_{pn,ln});
    for l <= li do
      psi <- {H(delta_{pi,l*theta+1}), ..., H(delta_{pi,(l+1)*theta})};   > Eq. (1)
      root <- MHTGen(psi);
      if l == 0 then omega <- H(root);
      else omega <- H(root || omega);                                    > Eq. (2)
    if omega != omega_{pi,li} then
      ErrList <- ErrList U {li};
    omega <- omega_{pi,li}
  return ErrList;
```

| ID | Requirement | Class | Source | Acceptance criterion | Gap |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| DELIA-AUDIT-001 | To audit pi, the auditor first obtains `{delta_{pi,1}, ..., delta_{pi,xi}}` from pi and the states `{G_1, ..., G_nu}` from the distributed ledger, then calls Alg. 3. | PM | §3.4 stage 2, p. 3290 | The auditor takes logs only from pi. States (GAP-61, DEV-10): the complete sequential GlobalState history, obtained from a server; each no-signed state is verified by its root-leader signature and serial continuity; every signed-round anchor is cross-checked against Ethereum event logs. Which server supplies the history, and what the auditor does when a check fails, are open (GAP-83). | GAP-15 (D), GAP-61 (CP: GAP-81; residual GAP-83) |
| DELIA-AUDIT-002 | An external auditor may start an audit at any time. The system model also describes auditing "automatically in cycles" (INC-28). | PM | §3.4 stage 2, p. 3290; §2.1, p. 3287 | User-decided (GAP-17, DEV-09): periodic audit with a configured period; on-demand trigger for test/CLI only. The auditor authenticates to servers with a mutual-TLS client certificate (GAP-77). | GAP-17 (D), GAP-77 (D) |
| DELIA-AUDIT-003 | LogVerify takes the record sequence of pi and the state sequence, and returns ErrList. | PM | Alg. 3 | Signature matches. | - |
| DELIA-AUDIT-004 | Initialize ErrList to empty, k = 1, l = 0. Per the listing, l is initialized once, before the outer loop. | PM | Alg. 3 lines 1-2 | Code review. User-decided (GAP-14 (a), DEV-01): l is initialized once and not reset per k. | GAP-14 (D) |
| DELIA-AUDIT-005 | The outer loop visits G_k for k from 1 while k <= nu, in ascending order, and parses pi's entry omega_{pi,li} and its batch number li from G_k. | PM | Alg. 3 lines 3-4 | Each retrieved state is visited exactly once, in order. User-decided (DEV-01): k++ after each G_k. li per G_k is derived by replaying the complete history: each time pi's slot changes, pi's completed-batch counter advances (GAP-61). | GAP-08 (D), GAP-14 (D), GAP-61 (CP: GAP-81; residual GAP-83) |
| DELIA-AUDIT-006 | The inner loop, while l <= li: rebuild psi from the auditor's copy of the records per Eq. (1); compute root = MHTGen(psi); set omega = H(root) if l == 0, else omega = H(root \|\| omega). Every record hash and every root is recomputed; nothing is cached. | PM | Alg. 3 lines 5-10 | Instrumentation: one H per record in each rebuilt batch and one MHTGen per inner iteration. User-decided (DEV-01): l++ per reconstructed batch; each batch is rebuilt exactly once (single linear pass). | GAP-14 (D), GAP-16 (D) |
| DELIA-AUDIT-007 | After the inner loop: if omega != omega_{pi,li}, add li to ErrList. Then set omega = omega_{pi,li} unconditionally (the chain resumes from the ledger value). | PM | Alg. 3 lines 11-13 (indentation per rendered page) | TEST-12. User-decided: the comparison follows the printed indentation, after reconstructing up to the current li. While pi's slot in G_k is still the G_0 zero-digest sentinel (pi has no completed batch), no comparison is made and nothing is added to ErrList (GAP-75). | GAP-14 (D), GAP-75 (D) |
| DELIA-AUDIT-008 | Return ErrList. The audit passes if and only if ErrList is empty ("null"). | PM | Alg. 3 line 14; §3.4 stage 2, p. 3290 | TEST-11 (empty on untampered logs). | - |
| DELIA-AUDIT-009 | ErrList contains batch numbers li of batches in which audit logs are deleted, forged or tampered. It carries no other data. | PM | Alg. 3 comment; §3.4 stage 2 | Output type is a set of batch numbers. | - |
| DELIA-AUDIT-010 | The audit is computed sequentially: each LocalState depends on the previous one and "cannot be performed in parallel". There are no Merkle inclusion proofs, multiproofs, binary-search localization or cached roots. | PM | §6.2.3, pp. 3297-3298; Alg. 3 | The audit code path uses a single goroutine and has none of the listed mechanisms. | - |
| DELIA-AUDIT-011 | GlobalState stores only omega values (Eq. (3)). Alg. 3 needs pi's batch number li per G_k, and its source is unspecified. | IMPL (user-decided) | Eq. (3); Alg. 3 | Ruling (GAP-08, GAP-61): li is not added to GlobalState. The auditor replays the complete sequential GlobalState history; whenever pi's slot changes, pi's completed-batch counter advances, which gives li for each G_k. Test TEST-52. | GAP-08 (D), GAP-61 (CP: GAP-81; residual GAP-83) |
| DELIA-AUDIT-012 | Where the contiguous {G_1..G_nu} comes from on the ledger is unspecified: only signed round states go on-chain, and Γ stores only the latest G (INC-05). | IMPL (user-decided) | §4.3.1, §4.3.3 | Ruling (GAP-61, revising GAP-15; DEV-10): servers retain the complete sequential GlobalState history off-chain (GAP-13) and the auditor obtains it from a server. No-signed states are verified by their root-leader signatures and serial continuity. Every signed-round anchor is cross-checked against Ethereum event logs. Γ.G stays "latest state" only; there is no on-chain history array. Tests TEST-45 (anchors from events) and TEST-52. | GAP-15 (D), GAP-61 (CP: GAP-81; residual GAP-83) |
| DELIA-AUDIT-013 | Unspecified: records past the last complete batch; records deleted so that Eq. (1) indices no longer exist; extra (forged) records that shift indices. | IMPL (user-decided) | Not stated | Ruling (GAP-16 (a)): a missing, altered or inserted record fails the affected batch (its li goes into ErrList) and the audit continues; a trailing partial batch is ignored. Test TEST-13. In the reproduction a server generates no records while offline, because its log reader is paused (EXPERIMENT-ONLY; GAP-69, LIM-01). Records of batches whose states were lost in a dispute rollback are open (GAP-84). | GAP-16 (D), GAP-69 (D), GAP-84 (OPEN) |

## 10. HMSC architecture

Instance representation (§4.3.1, p. 3291): `Γ := { sid, P, G, OffList, NewLeader, Parent, ChildList }`.

| Field | Meaning (paper) | Initial value (paper) | Mutated by (paper) | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| sid | unique identity of the instance | ordinary instance: not stated (GAP-20, OPEN); parent instance: "randomly generated" (user-decided, GAP-62: a 256-bit pseudo-random unique opaque identifier generated by SCC at federation activation) | never stated as mutated | §4.3.1, p. 3291 |
| P | set of all participants' public keys | ordinary: from InitMsg; parent: set of participants' public keys in child channels | not stated. User-decided: the loser of a dispute vote is removed from active P (GAP-36); the slot keeps its position, frozen at the rollback checkpoint (GAP-65) | §4.3.1 |
| G | latest state in the instance | not stated. User-decided: the current domain state at creation, G_0 if created before G_1 (GAP-20 partial ruling); stated for "a new instance"; for a parent, G = the current accepted domain GlobalState at federation (GAP-80) | Update stores the accepted signed round state; rollback to the checkpoint (GAP-38); a direct child's G is set null by a parent update with a lower serial (GAP-31) | §4.3.1; §4.3.3 |
| OffList | list of offline servers; holds PK_pi and H(psi_{pi,li}) per offline server | null at initialization | OfflineResolving adds (PK_pi, H(psi_{pi,li})); OnlineMsg removes | §4.3.1; §4.3.5, p. 3294 |
| NewLeader | reserved field for dispute resolving; holds new leader's address | null at initialization | DisputeResolving sets it; reset to null after dispute resolved | §4.3.1; §4.3.4, pp. 3293-3294 |
| Parent | identity of parent instance | null at initialization; a parent instance's own Parent is null | not stated for children upon federation. User-decided: each child's Parent = sid_a when federation activates (GAP-80); each direct child's Parent is reset to null on disassociation (GAP-45). | §4.3.1 |
| ChildList | identity list of all child instances | null at initialization; parent: includes sid_1..sid_m | FederateMsg (at creation of parent) | §4.3.1 |

| ID | Requirement | Class | Source | Acceptance criterion | Gap |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| DELIA-HMSC-ARCH-001 | HMSC is implemented by a smart contract pre-deployed on the distributed ledger. The state channel contract (SCC) comprises six protocols: Init, Confirm, Update, DisputeResolving, Close, OfflineResolving. | PM | §4.2, Fig. 6, p. 3291 | Each protocol is traceable to contract functions and/or off-chain modules per ARCH-002. User-decided (GAP-57 (a), DEV-15): Confirm has no contract function. | GAP-57 (D) |
| DELIA-HMSC-ARCH-002 | Init, Update, DisputeResolving, OfflineResolving and Close execute on Ethereum. State confirmation (leader election, signed and no-signed round state generation) is off-chain. | PM | §6.2.2, p. 3297; Fig. 6 legend; §3.3 stage 3; Table 3 ("Optimistic confirmation" ledger ×) | No off-chain confirmation step sends a transaction. Each on-chain protocol has a contract entry point. | GAP-57 (D) |
| DELIA-HMSC-ARCH-003 | An instance stored in SCC has exactly the fields {sid, P, G, OffList, NewLeader, Parent, ChildList}. Extra contract storage is allowed only as listed in ARCH-011 and must be labelled IMPL. | PM | §4.3.1, p. 3291 | Contract storage review: the seven fields exist with the meanings in the table above. | - |
| DELIA-HMSC-ARCH-004 | Field semantics, initial values and mutators are exactly as in the table above. Unstated cells stay unresolved. | PM | §4.3.1, §4.3.3-§4.3.5 | Contract tests TEST-15, TEST-16, TEST-32, TEST-34, TEST-37. | GAP-20 (OPEN), GAP-21 (D; residual GAP-82), GAP-36 (D), GAP-38 (D), GAP-65 (D), GAP-80 (D) |
| DELIA-HMSC-ARCH-005 | Multiple state channel instances can run simultaneously. When participants of different instances need to cooperate, their instances are federated rather than closed and re-created. | PM | §4.2, p. 3291 | Two independent instances can exist, and a parent can be formed over them without closing them. Topology from manual config (GAP-19). | GAP-19 (D) |
| DELIA-HMSC-ARCH-006 | The hierarchy is formed by federating child instances into a parent instance. The top instance is the root state channel instance. Multiple layers (Delta layers) are allowed. | PM | §4.3.1, Fig. 7, p. 3291; Table 1 (Delta); Eq. (5), p. 3295 | A two-layer hierarchy (Fig. 7) can be constructed; see TEST-16 and TEST-19. User-decided (GAP-21): a parent may later be federated again; a child has at most one parent (tree, not DAG). | GAP-21 (D; residual GAP-82) |
| DELIA-HMSC-ARCH-007 | How servers are partitioned into instances ("according to the network division"), when federation happens, and the tree shape for arbitrary n are unspecified. Balanced trees, fixed fanout and automatic policies are not allowed. | IMPL (user-decided) | §3.4 stage 1, p. 3290 | Ruling (GAP-19): the topology is a manual configuration file driving Init and Federate; no code computes partitions or federations. | GAP-19 (D) |
| DELIA-HMSC-ARCH-008 | Each instance runs Confirm in rounds. A round is: leader election, then one signed round state, then beta no-signed round states, so beta+1 states per round. | PM | §4.3.2, pp. 3291-3292; Thm. 1 proof ("states processed in a round is beta+1"), p. 3294 | TEST-20: in a round with beta = 2, exactly one signed and at most two no-signed states are produced. User-decided (GAP-26): the round ends after beta accepted no-signed states. Only a new signed-round anchor advances leader rotation; no-signed states do not (GAP-60). | GAP-26 (D), GAP-60 (CP: GAP-81) |
| DELIA-HMSC-ARCH-009 | beta is per instance: "beta is not fixed in different state channel instances"; "Every state channel instance could have different value of beta". | PP | §4.3.2, pp. 3291-3292 | beta is configurable per instance. | - |
| DELIA-HMSC-ARCH-010 | The beta value for each instance is not given by the protocol. | IMPL (user-decided) | Not stated (experiments: beta in {5, 10}, Fig. 12(a)) | Ruling (GAP-55, GAP-67): beta is required per-instance configuration with no protocol default. beta = 5 is an EXPERIMENT-ONLY value, used only where the paper gives no beta (GAP-56); it is not a DELIA default. | GAP-55 (D), GAP-56 (D), GAP-67 (D) |
| DELIA-HMSC-ARCH-011 | The contract needs state that Γ does not list: a history of accepted signed states (for Alg. 3 and Alg. 4), a closed marker, pending-offline requests and their start block, the current leader identity (Rule 3), vote tallies (if on-chain), and the child-state serials used by Rule 4. | IMPL (user-decided) | Implied by §4.3.3-§4.3.6; not specified | User-approved contract state outside Γ:<br>- closed flag (GAP-45)<br>- startBlock stored at OfflineMsg acceptance, and whether a valid cancellation arrived (GAP-43, GAP-63)<br>- the leader recorded for the latest accepted signed-round state, from which SCC derives the next expected leader; no global round counter (GAP-60, replacing the revision 2 round/leader index)<br>- per-child federation-submission tracking (GAP-21)<br>- whatever the parent-sid derivation needs, e.g. a federation counter (GAP-62)<br>- the P order frozen at Init (slot positions) and the active membership after an exclusion (GAP-08, GAP-65)<br>No history is stored on-chain: servers retain it off-chain, and accepted signed anchors are also emitted as events (GAP-15, GAP-61). Votes are exchanged off-chain and submitted as dispute evidence; there is no on-chain tally storage (GAP-36, GAP-64). No other extra state. Whether the recorded leader is kept separately from G is open (GAP-82). | GAP-15 (D), GAP-21 (D; residual GAP-82), GAP-30 (CP: GAP-87), GAP-31 (D), GAP-36 (D), GAP-43 (D; residual GAP-85), GAP-45 (D; residual GAP-89), GAP-60 (CP: GAP-81; residual GAP-82), GAP-62 (D), GAP-65 (D) |

| DELIA-HMSC-INIT-003 | Each participant pi sends its own InitMsg containing all participants' public keys; every server submits its own join request. | PM | Thm. 4 proof, p. 3295; §6.2.2 ("every server in a state channel instance has to submit its request"), p. 3297 | n transactions per Init for an n-participant instance. | GAP-20 (OPEN) |

## 12. State confirmation protocol

| ID | Requirement | Class | Source | Acceptance criterion | Gap |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| DELIA-HMSC-CONFIRM-001 | Every instance runs state confirmation in rounds. In each round one leader manages the instance, to avoid state conflict. | PM | §4.3.2, p. 3291; §4.1, p. 3290 | At any time each instance has at most one leader. | - |
| DELIA-HMSC-CONFIRM-002 | A leader is elected at the beginning of each round in every state channel instance, and every participant has the same opportunity to be leader. | PM | §4.3.2 stage 1, p. 3291 | Over abs(P) consecutive rounds with no OffList members, each participant leads exactly once (TEST-17). | GAP-22 (D), GAP-79 (D) |
| DELIA-HMSC-CONFIRM-003 | Election rule: the first leader is the participant with the minimum PK_pi in P. The leader does not join the next round's election; the next leader is the participant with the next smallest PK in P. If none exists, the rotation restarts at the minimum of P. | PM | §4.3.2 stage 1, pp. 3291-3292 | TEST-17: for P = {K1 < K2 < K3}, rounds 1-4 have leaders K1, K2, K3, K1. User-decided: keys are compared as fixed-width unsigned big-endian values; ineligible participants are skipped: members of the finalized on-chain OffList (GAP-79) and excluded participants (GAP-65) (DEV-11, TEST-17b). Key order uses the one canonical public-key encoding (GAP-73). | GAP-22 (D), GAP-60 (CP: GAP-81), GAP-79 (D) |
| DELIA-HMSC-CONFIRM-004 | Unspecified: the ordering of public keys (encoding and comparison); whether OffList or excluded participants are skipped; who computes the election ("the system"); how rotation resumes after a NewLeader is installed; how a parent leader relates to child leaders (INC-19). | IMPL (user-decided) | Not stated | Ruling (GAP-22, GAP-60, GAP-79):<br>- PKs compared as fixed-width unsigned big-endian integers of the one canonical key encoding (GAP-73)<br>- eligible = in active P (not excluded, GAP-65) and not in the finalized on-chain OffList; local suspicion and pending OfflineMsgs do not affect eligibility until the challenge window finishes (GAP-79, DEV-11)<br>- participants and SCC derive the leader deterministically with no global round counter: the first signed round's leader is the minimum eligible key in P; afterwards it is the next eligible key after the leader recorded in the latest accepted signed-round state, wrapping at the end (GAP-60)<br>- only a new signed-round anchor advances rotation; no-signed states do not<br>- NewLeader overrides rotation during dispute recovery; rotation then continues after that leader<br>Ruling (GAP-23 (a), GAP-68 (c)): a parent's candidate set is all of P_a, and the parent leader need not be a child leader. | GAP-22 (D), GAP-23 (D), GAP-60 (CP: GAP-81; residual GAP-82), GAP-68 (CP: GAP-87), GAP-79 (D) |
| DELIA-HMSC-CONFIRM-005 | A signed round state needs the signatures of all participants of the instance (or of the leaders of its child instances). A no-signed round state needs only the leader's signature. The signed state is the "secure anchor"; no-signed states are "temporary memory points". | PM | §4.3.2, p. 3291 | Type checks: a signed round state with a missing signature is not a signed round state (TEST-18). User-decided (GAP-26, corrected in revision 3): a no-signed state carries exactly one signature, the root leader's. | GAP-26 (D) |
| DELIA-HMSC-CONFIRM-006 | In a round the leader coordinates one signed round state first, then may conduct beta no-signed round states "in the rest of this round". | PM | §4.3.2 stages 2-3, p. 3292 | No no-signed state is produced in a round before its signed state completes. User-decided: a round starts at signed anchor serial nu; at most beta sequential no-signed states nu+1..nu+beta follow; the next state after that must be a signed anchor. | GAP-26 (D) |
| DELIA-HMSC-CONFIRM-007 | Signed round state, Step 1: a participant generates G_nu and sends it to its leader. (Fig. 8: p6 in Γ2 sends to leader p4.) | PM | §4.3.2 stage 2 Step 1, Fig. 8, p. 3292 | TEST-18. | - |
| DELIA-HMSC-CONFIRM-008 | Step 2: the leader signs `(G_nu \| leader)` and broadcasts `(G_nu, S_leader)` to all its participants, or to the leaders of the child instances in its ChildList. | PM | §4.3.2 Step 2, p. 3292 | TEST-18: every direct participant (or child leader) receives (G_nu, S_leader). S_leader is over purpose \|\| signer_role \|\| encoded state, with purpose STATE and the leader's role (GAP-72). The complete role set is open (GAP-86). | GAP-27 (D), GAP-72 (D; residual GAP-86) |
| DELIA-HMSC-CONFIRM-009 | Step 2 (cont.): every recipient verifies the leader's signature and generates its own signature on the state. | PM | §4.3.2 Step 2, p. 3292 | TEST-18: a recipient given an invalid S_leader produces no signature. The recipient's own signature carries purpose STATE and its signer role (GAP-72). | GAP-27 (D), GAP-72 (D; residual GAP-86) |
| DELIA-HMSC-CONFIRM-010 | Step 3: the leader collects the signatures from all participants (or child leaders), forming G_nu^{Gx}, and sends it to its parent leader. This repeats upward until the state reaches the root instance. | PM | §4.3.2 Step 3, Fig. 8, p. 3292 | TEST-19: in the Fig. 7/8 topology, G_nu^{G2} arrives at the root leader. | GAP-24 (OPEN) |
| DELIA-HMSC-CONFIRM-011 | Step 4: the root leader distributes the signed round state to the participants of all subordinate instances, recursively. | PM | §4.3.2 Step 4, p. 3292 | TEST-19: all six participants hold the signed round state. | GAP-24 (OPEN) |
| DELIA-HMSC-CONFIRM-012 | Unspecified: what a parent leader does on receiving a child's G_nu^{Gx} (whether it re-runs Step 2 with its child leaders to form G_nu^{Gparent}), and which object the root distributes (the text says G_nu^{G2}). | IMPL (partially user-decided) | §4.3.2 Steps 3-4 | Decided: any participant (not only the leader) may submit a valid signed-round state via Update. Still OPEN: forward unchanged vs recursive re-signing at each parent, and what the root distributes. Blocked until GAP-24 core is decided. | GAP-24 (OPEN) |
| DELIA-HMSC-CONFIRM-013 | Generating a signed round state takes three leader-participant interactions (Fig. 8 steps 2-4); generating a no-signed state takes one. | PM | Thm. 1 proof, p. 3294 | TEST-18 and TEST-20 count leader interaction phases: 3 and 1. | - |
| DELIA-HMSC-CONFIRM-014 | Every leader that receives a no-signed round state sends it on to the root instance's leader, recursively. | PM | §4.3.2 stage 3, p. 3292 | TEST-20: a no-signed state from a leaf participant reaches the root leader through each intermediate leader. User-decided (GAP-26 correction): intermediate leaders relay upward without signing. | GAP-26 (D) |
| DELIA-HMSC-CONFIRM-015 | Root approval: let G_nu* be the latest state conducted by the root leader and G_nu' the new state. If `nu* < nu + beta` and `nu' == nu* + 1`, the root leader signs G_nu' with its SK and broadcasts (G_nu', S_root) to all participants, through its descendant leaders. | PM | §4.3.2 stage 3, p. 3292 | TEST-20: with both conditions true the state is approved and broadcast; with nu' != nu*+1 it is not approved. User-decided (GAP-26, corrected in revision 3): nu in the condition = the round's signed anchor serial; beta = the originating instance's beta; the root leader approves, signs the no-signed state (the only signature it carries) and distributes it downward through descendant leaders. This matches the paper's "p1 signs G_nu'", so DEV-05 is withdrawn. The purpose and role codes of this signature are open (GAP-86). | GAP-26 (D), GAP-72 (D; residual GAP-86) |
| DELIA-HMSC-CONFIRM-016 | A receiver of (G_nu', S_root) checks whether its local latest state's serial equals nu' - 1. User-decided (GAP-46): the receiver sends (fromSerial, toSerial) to its direct leader, verifies the returned signatures and serial continuity, and stores the states; no recursion to higher leaders. | GAP-46 (D) |

| DELIA-HMSC-CONFIRM-019 | Unspecified: behavior when the root-approval condition fails; how the end of a round is signalled and the next election triggered; the exact definition of nu in `nu* < nu + beta`; whose beta applies at the root. | IMPL (user-decided) | Not stated | Ruling (GAP-26):<br>- a round begins at signed anchor serial nu, and up to beta sequential no-signed states nu+1..nu+beta may follow<br>- beta is the originating instance's<br>- the round ends after beta accepted no-signed states, and the next anchor must be a signed-round state<br>- a rejected no-signed state is not adopted<br>There is no on-chain round counter; SCC derives the expected leader from the leader recorded in the latest accepted signed-round state (GAP-60). | GAP-26 (D), GAP-60 (CP: GAP-81) |
