User-decided (GAP-40 (b)): starting a dispute is an explicit, manual protocol action; there are no automatic detection thresholds. | GAP-40 (D) |

## 13. State update protocol

Definitions (§4.3.3, p. 3292):
- Γx is the target instance and G_nu^{Gx} the submitted signed state.
- numoff is the number of addresses stored in OffList.
- num is the number of participants of Γx if ChildList is null, otherwise the number of entries in ChildList.

| ID | Requirement | Class | Source | Acceptance criterion | Gap |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| DELIA-HMSC-UPDATE-001 | The distributed ledger accepts only signed round states. No-signed round states stay off-chain as a temporary cache. | PM | §4.3.3, p. 3292 | TEST-29: submitting a state carrying only the leader's signature is rejected. | - |
| DELIA-HMSC-UPDATE-002 | A participant may submit a signed round state G_nu^{Gx} to its instance Γx. | PM | §4.3.3, p. 3292 | A participant of Γx can call Update. User-decided: any participant, not only the leader, may submit a valid signed-round state (GAP-24 partial ruling, GAP-32). | GAP-32 (CP: GAP-81) |
| DELIA-HMSC-UPDATE-003 | Compute num and numoff exactly as defined above. | PM | §4.3.3, p. 3292 | Unit test on contract helper: leaf instance num = abs(active P); parent instance num = abs(ChildList). User-decided (GAP-30, GAP-65, GAP-68): excluded participants are not in active P; for a parent signed-round state, numoff counts offline members of the expected child-leader signer set, and the parent leader's signature is counted separately when the parent leader is not one of those child leaders (DEV-18). | GAP-30 (CP: GAP-87), GAP-65 (D), GAP-68 (CP: GAP-87) |
| DELIA-HMSC-UPDATE-004 | Rule 1: nu > nu*, where nu* is the serial number of the latest state conducted by the root channel's leader. | PM | §4.3.3 Rule 1, p. 3292 | TEST-23: a submission with nu <= nu* is rejected. User-decided (GAP-66, DEV-20): nu* = the serial of the latest signed state stored on-chain in the root instance. | GAP-29 (D), GAP-66 (D) |
| DELIA-HMSC-UPDATE-005 | Rule 2: for every PK_pi in OffList, pi's LocalState omega_{pi,li} in G_nu must equal omega_{pi,li*} in G_nu*. | PM | §4.3.3 Rule 2, p. 3292 | TEST-24: a submission changing an offline server's entry is rejected. G_nu* = the latest signed state stored on-chain in the root instance (GAP-66). The position of PK_pi comes from the P order frozen at Init (GAP-08). An excluded participant's slot is frozen the same way: no later LocalState update for it is accepted unless membership is explicitly re-established (GAP-65, LIM-05). | GAP-08 (D), GAP-29 (D), GAP-65 (D), GAP-66 (D) |
| DELIA-HMSC-UPDATE-006 | Rule 3 (count): G_nu^{Gx} contains num - numoff signatures. | PM | §4.3.3 Rule 3, p. 3292 | TEST-25: a submission with fewer signatures is rejected. User-decided (GAP-30): exactly num - numoff distinct valid signers from the expected online signer set; more, fewer, duplicate or unexpected signers are rejected. OfflineMsg excludes the pending-offline server (DEV-03). For a parent instance, the parent leader's signature is also required when the parent leader is not one of the expected child leaders; if it is, one signature satisfies both (GAP-68, DEV-18). How a single signature can satisfy both roles when roles are bound into the signed bytes is open (GAP-87). | GAP-30 (CP: GAP-87), GAP-68 (CP: GAP-87) |
| DELIA-HMSC-UPDATE-007 | Rule 3 (validity): for every signature S_pi in G_nu^{Gx}, Vrfy(PK_pi, S_pi) accepts. | PM | §4.3.3 Rule 3, p. 3292 | TEST-26: one invalid signature causes rejection. User-decided: secp256k1 ECDSA over the SHA-256 digest of purpose \|\| signer_role \|\| encoded state, checked on-chain against addresses derived from P (GAP-27, GAP-28, GAP-72, GAP-73). | GAP-27 (D; residual GAP-86), GAP-28 (D), GAP-72 (D; residual GAP-86) |
| DELIA-HMSC-UPDATE-008 | Rule 3 (leader): one of the signatures must be the leader's ("contains the leader's public key"). | PM | §4.3.3 Rule 3, p. 3292 | TEST-27: a full set of valid signatures without the leader's is rejected. User-decided (GAP-27, GAP-60): one valid signature must verify under the expected leader's PK. SCC derives the expected leader with no round counter: the minimum eligible key in P for the first signed round, otherwise the next eligible key after the leader recorded in the latest accepted signed-round state, wrapping; NewLeader overrides during recovery. A leader field supplied in the message is not trusted. If signed anchors are produced off-chain but not submitted, SCC's rotation lags the off-chain rotation (GAP-81). | GAP-30 (CP: GAP-87), GAP-60 (CP: GAP-81; residual GAP-82), GAP-79 (D) |
| DELIA-HMSC-UPDATE-009 | Rule 4: if ChildList of Γx is not null, the minimum serial number of the states stored in its child instances must be less than nu, or null. | PM | §4.3.3 Rule 4, p. 3292 | User-decided (GAP-31 (b), DEV-06): Rule 4 is not used as a rejection criterion; its effect is applied through UPDATE-010. TEST-28. | GAP-31 (D) |
| DELIA-HMSC-UPDATE-010 | Rule 4 explanation: states stored in Γx's child channels that have a bigger serial number "should be set null", because they are old and meaningless. | PM | §4.3.3 paragraph after rules, p. 3292 | User-decided (GAP-31 (b), DEV-06): a valid parent update at serial nu is accepted, and each direct child whose stored G has serial > nu has its G set null; grandchildren are not touched. TEST-28. | GAP-31 (D) |
| DELIA-HMSC-UPDATE-011 | If all four State Update Rules hold, the ledger stores G_nu^{Gx} as Γx's latest state G. Otherwise it does not store it. | PM | §4.3.3, p. 3292; §4.3.1 (G = latest state) | TEST-22: a valid submission makes Γx.G equal to the submitted state and emits one event carrying the full signed state; the auditor uses these events to cross-check anchors (GAP-15, GAP-61). | GAP-15 (D), GAP-61 (CP: GAP-81) |
| DELIA-HMSC-UPDATE-012 | Unspecified: when updates are submitted ("we could periodically update", §3.3 stage 4) and by whom; how a rejection is signalled (revert vs event). | IMPL (user-decided) | §3.3 stage 4, p. 3289 | Ruling (GAP-32 (b)): a participant submits signed-round states periodically. The period is configuration, not a DELIA constant. A rejected transaction reverts and has no state effect. Leader rotation on-chain (GAP-60) and the auditor's cross-check of every anchor (GAP-61) both assume every signed anchor reaches the chain, which periodic submission does not guarantee (GAP-81). The first recovery state after a dispute rollback is submitted immediately (GAP-70). | GAP-32 (CP: GAP-81), GAP-70 (D) |

## 14. Dispute resolving protocol

Listing as printed (Alg. 4, p. 3293). Line numbers are the paper's.
```
1:  procedure CheckpointSelection(G_1, ..., G_nu, Gx)
2:    Parse Gx as (sid_x, ..., ChildList_x);
3:    if ChildList_x == null then
4:      k <- nu;
5:      for k >= 0 do
6:        k--;
7:        if G_k^{Gx} exists then
8:          return k;
9:    else
10:     k1 <- nu, k2 <- nu, count <- 0;
11:     for k1 >= 0 do
12:       k1--;
13:       if G_{k1}^{Gx} exists then
14:         count++;
15:         if count == 2 then
16:           break;
17:     for Gb in ChildList_x do
18:       k3 <- nu
19:       for k3 >= 0 then
20:         k3--;
21:         if G_{k3}^{Gb} exists then
22:           if k3 <= k2 then
23:             k2 <- k3
24:     return max(k1, k2);
```

| ID | Requirement | Class | Source | Acceptance criterion | Gap |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| DELIA-HMSC-DISPUTE-001 | The protocol prevents malicious participants from updating or generating old or invalid states. It covers (a) malicious non-leader participants, which "the leader could detect", and (b) a malicious leader, possibly colluding with a small number of participants. The protocol design addresses (b). | PM | §4.3.4, p. 3292 | User-decided (GAP-39 (a), DEV-14): malicious non-leader submissions are detected and rejected by normal verification; no separate exclusion protocol. | GAP-39 (D) |
| DELIA-HMSC-DISPUTE-002 | There are two kinds of dispute: against the leader of an instance whose ChildList is null, and against the leader of an instance whose ChildList is not null. | PM | §4.3.4, pp. 3292-3293; Fig. 9 | TEST-30 and TEST-31 cover both. | - |
| DELIA-HMSC-DISPUTE-003 | Checkpoints are chosen only among anchor points, that is, signed round states. | PM | §4.3.4, p. 3293 | Alg. 4 only returns serials k for which some G_k^{G*} signed state exists. User-decided (GAP-34, GAP-64): "exists" is looked up off-chain in the retained signed-state history. | GAP-34 (D), GAP-64 (D) |
| DELIA-HMSC-DISPUTE-004 | Alg. 4 takes all states G_1..G_nu and the instance Γx as input, and outputs the serial number of the checkpoint state. | PM | §4.3.4, p. 3293; Alg. 4 line 1 | Function signature matches. User-decided (GAP-35 (a), GAP-64): participants run checkpoint selection off-chain over the retained signed-state history; SCC does not rerun Alg. 4 and keeps no historical state arrays for it. | GAP-34 (D), GAP-35 (D), GAP-64 (D) |
| DELIA-HMSC-DISPUTE-005 | Lines 3-8 (ChildList_x == null): k = nu; while k >= 0: decrement k, and if G_k^{Gx} exists return k. As printed, the decrement precedes the test (INC-07). | PM | Alg. 4 lines 3-8 | Kept for traceability only. User-decided (GAP-34 (b), DEV-02): the implementation follows the prose and Fig. 9 semantics in DISPUTE-009, not these literal lines. TEST-30 asserts the prose results. | GAP-34 (D) |
| DELIA-HMSC-DISPUTE-006 | Lines 10-16 (own state): k1 = nu, k2 = nu, count = 0; while k1 >= 0: decrement k1; if G_{k1}^{Gx} exists, increment count; if count == 2, break. This selects the second-last signed round state of Γx. | PM | Alg. 4 lines 10-16; §4.3.4 "Using Own State", p. 3293 | TEST-31(c). User-decided (DEV-02): the second-last signed state of Γx is selected per the prose; the literal decrement-before-test off-by-one is not reproduced. | GAP-34 (D) |
| DELIA-HMSC-DISPUTE-007 | Lines 17-23 (child channel state): for each Γb in ChildList_x: k3 = nu; while k3 >= 0: decrement k3; if G_{k3}^{Gb} exists and k3 <= k2, set k2 = k3. | PM | Alg. 4 lines 17-23 | TEST-31(d). The literal listing and the prose ("latest signed state of each child, then earliest among these") give different k2 when a child has more than one signed state (INC-07). User-decided (DEV-02): implement the prose; take each child's latest signed state, then the earliest among them. | GAP-34 (D) |
| DELIA-HMSC-DISPUTE-008 | Line 24: return max(k1, k2). The "better" of the two candidates is the later one. | PM | Alg. 4 line 24; §4.3.4 ("we use the better one") | TEST-31: the result equals the maximum of the two candidates. | - |
| DELIA-HMSC-DISPUTE-009 | Intended semantics per the prose and Fig. 9: (1) with no child channels, the checkpoint is Γx's latest signed round state (Fig. 9(a), 9(b)); (2) with children, "Own State" is Γx's second-last signed round state (Fig. 9(c)), "Child Channel State" is the earliest of the children's latest signed round states (Fig. 9(d)), and the better of the two is used. | PM | §4.3.4 cases 1-2, Fig. 9, p. 3293 | TEST-30 and TEST-31 use Fig. 9 scenarios: (a) -> G_4, (b) -> G_3^{G1}, (c) -> G_2^{G3}, (d) -> G_3^{G1}. User-decided (GAP-34 (b)): this requirement governs the implementation. | GAP-34 (D) |
| DELIA-HMSC-DISPUTE-010 | To execute the protocol, a participant submits DisputeMsg, composed of the signed round state at the checkpoint, as a request to change the leader. The request is valid if the State Update Rules are satisfied. | PM | §4.3.4, p. 3293 | TEST-32. User-decided (GAP-64, GAP-71, DEV-04):<br>- the State Update Rules are not applied to the checkpoint; current P/OffList are not applied, and no historical membership snapshots are kept<br>- SCC verifies the checkpoint state's own signatures plus the dispute vote evidence<br>- the majority-certified result determines the checkpoint; SCC does not check the serial against Alg. 4<br>Open (GAP-88): exactly what the on-chain check of the checkpoint's own signatures covers, and how votes bind the checkpoint. | GAP-33 (D), GAP-35 (D), GAP-64 (D; residual GAP-88), GAP-71 (D) |
| DELIA-HMSC-DISPUTE-011 | Voting: any participant in the instance can generate a signed voting message, sent as a DisputeMsg, for either the leader or the dispute initiator. According to the vote, one of the two is excluded. | PM | §4.3.4, p. 3293; §6.2.4, p. 3298 | TEST-33, TEST-54. User-decided (GAP-36 (a), GAP-65):<br>- signed votes are exchanged off-chain; every current member of active P votes, including the leader and the initiator<br>- a strict majority (>50%) decides, with no protocol deadline (harness timeout allowed)<br>- the result and the signed votes (purpose VOTE, GAP-72) are submitted with the dispute transaction<br>- the loser is removed from active participation and signing, not added to OffList; its GlobalState slot keeps its position, frozen at the rollback checkpoint, and no later LocalState update for it is accepted unless membership is explicitly re-established (LIM-05)<br>Open (GAP-88): the effect of an exclusion on the loser's membership in ancestor or descendant instances. | GAP-36 (D), GAP-65 (D; residual GAP-88), GAP-72 (D; residual GAP-86) |
| DELIA-HMSC-DISPUTE-012 | If the leader is judged malicious, a new leader is selected randomly in this instance, "which could be decided by the hash of latest block". | PP | §4.3.4, p. 3293; §6.2.2 ("Ethereum need to select a new leader in this protocol"), p. 3297 | New-leader selection happens on-chain. User-decided (GAP-37): index = hash of the previous/latest block mod candidate count. In the EVM only a past block's hash is available, so "latest" can only mean block.number - 1. Candidates exclude the removed participant and members of the finalized on-chain OffList; pending OfflineMsgs do not exclude (GAP-79). Candidates are ordered per GAP-22. | GAP-37 (D), GAP-79 (D) |
| DELIA-HMSC-DISPUTE-013 | The new leader's address is stored in NewLeader_x. | PM | §4.3.4, p. 3293 | TEST-32: after a successful dispute against the leader, NewLeader_x is non-null and identifies a member of P (address derived from its PK, GAP-28). | GAP-28 (D) |
| DELIA-HMSC-DISPUTE-014 | All state channel instances in HMSC roll back to the checkpoint. | PM | §4.3.4, p. 3293 | TEST-32: every instance's latest state is the checkpoint state afterwards. User-decided (GAP-38): off-chain, participants discard temporary and no-signed states after the checkpoint. | GAP-38 (D) |
| DELIA-HMSC-DISPUTE-015 | After rollback, the next state Γx receives must be G_{nu+1} (nu = checkpoint serial), and the leader's signature on it must be Sign(G_{nu+1} \| NewLeader_x). | PM | §4.3.4, pp. 3293-3294 | TEST-32, TEST-51: a next state with the wrong serial or not signed by NewLeader_x is rejected. User-decided (GAP-70, GAP-72): the first state after rollback must be a signed-round state carrying the RECOVERY purpose and must be submitted on-chain immediately; SCC checks serial = checkpoint + 1 and that the required leader signature is NewLeader_x's. Which of its signatures carry RECOVERY is open (GAP-86). | GAP-27 (D), GAP-38 (D), GAP-70 (D), GAP-72 (D; residual GAP-86) |
| DELIA-HMSC-DISPUTE-016 | States between the checkpoint and the disputed latest state are lost ("state loss") and are not recovered. | PM | §4.3.4, p. 3294 | No recovery mechanism exists. | GAP-38 (D) |
| DELIA-HMSC-DISPUTE-017 | Participants learn the new leader by reading NewLeader_x and continue the state confirmation process. | PM | §4.3.4, p. 3294 | Off-chain nodes read NewLeader_x from the contract. User-decided (GAP-22): NewLeader overrides rotation during recovery; rotation then continues after that leader. SCC uses the same rule: NewLeader_x is the expected leader during recovery, and afterwards the next eligible key after it (GAP-60). | GAP-22 (D), GAP-60 (CP: GAP-81) |
| DELIA-HMSC-DISPUTE-018 | After the dispute is resolved, NewLeader_x is set to null. | PM | §4.3.4, p. 3294 | TEST-32, TEST-51: NewLeader_x returns to null. User-decided (GAP-38, GAP-70): SCC clears NewLeader_x when it accepts the recovery state, i.e. the signed-round state at checkpoint + 1 whose required leader signature is NewLeader_x's, which is submitted on-chain immediately. | GAP-38 (D), GAP-70 (D) |
| DELIA-HMSC-DISPUTE-019 | Unspecified: who executes Alg. 4 (initiator off-chain, SCC, or both) and whether SCC re-verifies the checkpoint; the source of "G_k^{Gx} exists"; vote format, eligibility, threshold, tally location and deadline; what "excluded" does to P, OffList or roles; rollback mechanics on- and off-chain; when a dispute counts as "resolved"; how malicious non-leaders are excluded; what counts as an "abnormal situation". | IMPL (user-decided) | Not stated | Rulings are recorded per row above:<br>- GAP-33: superseded by GAP-64/GAP-71 (DEV-04)<br>- GAP-34: prose Alg. 4 (DEV-02)<br>- GAP-35: off-chain selection, SCC verifies evidence<br>- GAP-36: off-chain strict-majority vote; loser removed from P<br>- GAP-37: previous block hash mod candidates<br>- GAP-38: all instances roll back; NewLeader cleared on first valid checkpoint+1 state<br>- GAP-39: detect and reject only (DEV-14)<br>- GAP-40: manual trigger<br>Revision 3:<br>- GAP-64: off-chain selection; SCC checks the checkpoint's signatures plus vote evidence<br>- GAP-65: loser's slot frozen at the checkpoint<br>- GAP-70: recovery anchor submitted immediately, clears NewLeader<br>- GAP-71: no Rules 2-4 on the checkpoint<br>- GAP-79: finalized OffList only<br>Still open: GAP-88. | GAP-33 (D), GAP-34 (D), GAP-35 (D), GAP-36 (D), GAP-37 (D), GAP-38 (D), GAP-39 (D), GAP-40 (D), GAP-64 (D), GAP-65 (D), GAP-70 (D), GAP-71 (D), GAP-79 (D), GAP-88 (OPEN) |

## 15. Offline resolving protocol

| ID | Requirement | Class | Source | Acceptance criterion | Gap |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| DELIA-HMSC-OFFLINE-001 | OffList records the offline servers of an instance. | PM | §4.3.5, p. 3294; §4.3.1 | - | - |
| DELIA-HMSC-OFFLINE-002 | Offline detection is triggered once a server loses contact with the other servers in the domain. The other servers confirm it is offline "through the inquiry mechanism". Per Thm. 5, the leader learns pi is offline when pi cannot submit signatures in state confirmation. | PM | §3.3 stage 5, pp. 3289-3290; §4.3.5; Thm. 5 proof, p. 3296 | TEST-34 setup: a server stops responding during signature collection. User-decided (GAP-41 (a)): loss of contact triggers a peer inquiry with a configurable implementation timeout; no heartbeats. Inquiry payloads (GAP-73): OfflineInquiry = (sid, targetParticipant); OfflineInquiryAck = (sid, targetParticipant); the TLS connection authenticates them, with no extra signature (NET-020, NET-035). | GAP-41 (D), GAP-73 (D) |
| DELIA-HMSC-OFFLINE-003 | Offline state confirmation, step 1: the leader broadcasts `(G_nu, H(psi_{pi,li}))` to the other participants in P. G_nu is the first GlobalState generated after pi went offline; H(psi_{pi,li}) is the hash of pi's LogCacheQueue data. | PM | §4.3.5, p. 3294 | TEST-34. User-decided (GAP-42): H(psi_{pi,li}) = SHA-256 over the raw 32-byte digests of pi's current active LogCacheQueue (including a partial batch), in record order. | GAP-42 (D) |
| DELIA-HMSC-OFFLINE-004 | The other participants also inquire pi to check that it is offline. If the check passes, each replies with its signed GlobalState data. | PM | §4.3.5, p. 3294 | TEST-34: participants that reach pi do not sign. Inquiry and acknowledgement payloads per GAP-73 (NET-020, NET-035). | GAP-41 (D), GAP-73 (D) |
| DELIA-HMSC-OFFLINE-005 | The leader collects the signatures and calls the state update protocol to submit `OfflineMsg = (G_nu, S_p1, ..., S_p(i-1), S_p(i+1), ..., S_pn, OFFLINE:(PK_pi, H(psi_{pi,li})))`. | PM | §4.3.5, p. 3294 | TEST-34 and TEST-48: an OfflineMsg carrying n-1 valid signatures (all except pi) is accepted. User-decided (GAP-42, DEV-03). | GAP-42 (D) |
| DELIA-HMSC-OFFLINE-006 | On receiving OfflineMsg, the instance first checks the State Update Rules. | PM | §4.3.5, p. 3294 | An OfflineMsg violating any rule is not processed further. User-decided (DEV-03): for OfflineMsg only, the expected signer set excludes the pending-offline server. | GAP-42 (D) |
| DELIA-HMSC-OFFLINE-007 | The instance then starts a challenge time for cancelling the request: it waits one block generation time (block height is obtainable from the smart contract) for a request from the allegedly offline server. | PM | §4.3.5, p. 3294 | TEST-34, TEST-35, TEST-50: finalization is impossible before the challenge interval has elapsed. User-decided (GAP-43, GAP-63, DEV-19): OfflineMsg records startBlock; cancellation is allowed during the one-block challenge interval; after that interval anyone may call FinalizeOffline. The exact block boundaries are open (GAP-85). | GAP-43 (D), GAP-63 (D; residual GAP-85) |
| DELIA-HMSC-OFFLINE-008 | If the participant is not actually offline, it may send its signed latest state `(G_nu, S_pi)` to the instance to request cancelling. | PM | §4.3.5, p. 3294 | TEST-35. User-decided (GAP-43): a cancel must carry pi's valid signature over the same G_nu the OfflineMsg referenced; otherwise it is rejected. The purpose code of that signature is open (GAP-86). | GAP-43 (D), GAP-63 (D; residual GAP-85), GAP-72 (D; residual GAP-86) |
| DELIA-HMSC-OFFLINE-009 | If no cancelling message is received during the waiting time, the instance adds PK_pi and H(psi_{pi,li}) to its OffList. Otherwise the offline request does not take effect. | PM | §4.3.5, p. 3294 | TEST-34, TEST-50: after the interval, a permissionless FinalizeOffline adds (PK_pi, H(psi)) to OffList if no valid cancellation was received (GAP-63, DEV-19). TEST-35: after a valid cancellation, OffList stays unchanged. | GAP-43 (D), GAP-63 (D; residual GAP-85) |
| DELIA-HMSC-OFFLINE-010 | While pi is in OffList, pi's LocalState in any later accepted state must stay unchanged (Rule 2) until pi goes online again. | PM | §4.3.3 Rule 2; Thm. 5 proof, p. 3296 | TEST-36. | GAP-29 (D), GAP-66 (D) |
| DELIA-HMSC-OFFLINE-011 | Online notification: once a server comes back, it sends notifications to the other servers in the domain. | PM | §3.3 stage 5, p. 3290 | TEST-37. Payload (GAP-73): OnlineNotification = (sid, returningParticipant); the Online State Confirmation process then starts. In the reproduction the server's log reader was paused while it was offline (EXPERIMENT-ONLY; GAP-69, LIM-01). | GAP-44 (D), GAP-69 (D), GAP-73 (D) |
| DELIA-HMSC-OFFLINE-012 | Online state confirmation: the leader broadcasts the first GlobalState generated after the offline participant comes online, and collects signatures from the other participants. | PM | §4.3.5, p. 3294 | TEST-37. | GAP-44 (D) |
| DELIA-HMSC-OFFLINE-013 | The leader calls the state update protocol to submit `OnlineMsg = (G_nu, S_p1, ..., S_pn, ONLINE: PK_pi)`. | PM | §4.3.5, p. 3294 | TEST-37. User-decided (GAP-44, DEV-12): signatures from the currently online signer set; S_pi is not required while pi is in OffList. | GAP-44 (D) |
| DELIA-HMSC-OFFLINE-014 | On OnlineMsg, the instance checks the State Update Rules. If they pass, it removes the participant's address from OffList. Otherwise the online request fails. | PM | §4.3.5, p. 3294 | TEST-37: OffList no longer contains pi. A rule-violating OnlineMsg leaves OffList unchanged. | GAP-28 (D), GAP-44 (D) |
| DELIA-HMSC-OFFLINE-015 | After coming back online, the server synchronizes all GlobalState data and LogCacheQueue data from the other servers in the domain. | PM | §3.3 stage 5, p. 3290 | TEST-37: the rejoined server's GlobalState and LogCacheQueue sets equal the others'. User-decided (GAP-44, DEV-12):<br>- missing states are requested from the direct leader, with serial continuity, signatures and Alg. 2 inputs verified<br>- pi's next valid batch chains from its frozen LocalState<br>- there is no ingestion protocol for records generated while offline; the reproduction pauses the server's log reader while it is offline (EXPERIMENT-ONLY; GAP-69, LIM-01) | GAP-44 (D), GAP-69 (D) |
| DELIA-HMSC-OFFLINE-016 | Unspecified: the inquiry mechanism, loss-of-contact criteria and timeouts; how the challenge is finalized on-chain (a contract cannot act without a transaction); verification of the cancel message; whether OnlineMsg includes S_pi; the rejoin sync protocol; LSG handling of records pi generated while offline. | IMPL (user-decided) | Not stated | Rulings are recorded per row above:<br>- GAP-41: inquiry with configurable timeout<br>- GAP-42: n-1 signers, H over the active LogCacheQueue (DEV-03)<br>- GAP-43: stored block height, one-block window, cancel signature over the same G_nu<br>- GAP-44: online signer set, sync from direct leader, frozen LocalState (DEV-12)<br>- GAP-63: permissionless FinalizeOffline after the one-block interval (DEV-19)<br>- GAP-69: log reader paused while offline (EXPERIMENT-ONLY, LIM-01)<br>- GAP-73: inquiry, acknowledgement and online-notification payloads<br>Still open: exact window boundaries (GAP-85). | GAP-41 (D), GAP-42 (D), GAP-43 (D), GAP-44 (D), GAP-63 (D; residual GAP-85), GAP-69 (D), GAP-73 (D) |

## 16. Close protocol

| ID | Requirement | Class | Source | Acceptance criterion | Gap |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| DELIA-HMSC-CLOSE-001 | After an instance is closed, no participant can submit a state to it. | PM | §4.3.6, p. 3294 | TEST-38: Update on a closed instance is rejected. User-decided (GAP-45): SCC keeps a closed flag; all later states are rejected; closed instance data stays readable. | GAP-45 (D; residual GAP-89) |
| DELIA-HMSC-CLOSE-002 | Closure does not use an on-chain challenge period. The paper rejects that approach as requiring "an unaffordable waiting time" and designs an off-chain closure. | PM | §4.3.6, p. 3294 | Close takes effect in the transaction that submits a valid CloseMsg, with no waiting period. | - |
| DELIA-HMSC-CLOSE-003 | Closing the root instance: generate a state with a special identifier `(G_nu \| Close)` and request a signed state via signed round state generation, with signatures from all participants of the root instance. | PM | §4.3.6, p. 3294 | TEST-38. User-decided (GAP-76): the Close state needs signatures from every member of the instance's P, including currently offline members, so an offline participant can block a graceful Close (LIM-03). Signatures carry purpose CLOSE (GAP-72). | GAP-45 (D; residual GAP-89), GAP-72 (D), GAP-76 (D) |
| DELIA-HMSC-CLOSE-004 | Closing an instance that has no parent: a participant may generate `(G_nu \| Close)` and request a signed state via signed round state generation. | PM | §4.3.6, p. 3294 | TEST-38. User-decided (GAP-45): direct closure is rejected while Parent != null; disassociate first (TEST-44). | GAP-45 (D; residual GAP-89), GAP-72 (D), GAP-76 (D) |
| DELIA-HMSC-CLOSE-005 | Finally, any participant may send `CloseMsg`, composed of the signed `(G_nu \| Close)`, to SCC. | PM | §4.3.6, p. 3294 | TEST-38, TEST-49: a CloseMsg from any single participant suffices. User-decided (GAP-45, GAP-72, GAP-76): SCC verifies the Close state's signatures under purpose CLOSE and requires one from every member of the instance's P, OffList members included; an ordinary signed round state (purpose STATE) is not accepted as a Close state. | GAP-45 (D; residual GAP-89), GAP-72 (D), GAP-76 (D) |
| DELIA-HMSC-CLOSE-006 | Disassociation (closing parent Γa): every instance in Γa's ChildList generates a signed round state on a new state with the Close identifier. Γa's leader collects all of them and sends `DisassociateMsg = (G_nu^{G1}, ..., G_nu^{Gm})` to SCC. | PM | §4.3.6, p. 3294 | TEST-39. User-decided (GAP-45, GAP-76): each child produces its normal signed-round close state (purpose CLOSE) and Γa's leader collects them; each direct child's Parent is reset to null and the children stay open. DisassociateMsg is rejected when Γa.Parent != null; direct nested disassociation is not supported (LIM-04). | GAP-45 (D; residual GAP-89), GAP-72 (D), GAP-76 (D) |
| DELIA-HMSC-CLOSE-007 | SCC verifies the DisassociateMsg signatures and closes Γa. | PM | §4.3.6, p. 3294 | TEST-39 and TEST-40: invalid child signatures leave Γa open. | GAP-27 (D), GAP-45 (D; residual GAP-89), GAP-72 (D), GAP-76 (D) |
| DELIA-HMSC-CLOSE-008 | Audit closure: when log integrity protection or mutual supervision is no longer needed, each server closes its log collector, log preprocessor and integrity verification module, and the Close protocol is invoked. | PM | §3.4 stage 3, p. 3290 | User-decided (GAP-18, DEV-08): no separate modules exist; stopping the log reader and LSG is coupled with a Close invocation. | GAP-18 (D) |
| DELIA-HMSC-CLOSE-009 | Unspecified: encoding of the Close identifier; which checks SCC applies to CloseMsg (State Update Rules? signature count?); the state of child instances after disassociation (Parent field, still open); closing a child instance that has a parent, other than by disassociating the parent; post-closure cleanup (none is described, so none is added). | IMPL (user-decided) | Not stated | Ruling (GAP-45):<br>- Close verified like a signed-round state with the complete signer set, then the instance is marked closed<br>- Disassociate verifies each child's signed close state, closes the parent, resets each direct child's Parent to null and leaves children open<br>- direct closure is rejected while Parent != null<br>- closed data stays readable; no cleanup<br>Revision 3:<br>- the Close identifier is the CLOSE purpose byte in the signed bytes (GAP-72)<br>- a Close needs signatures from all of the instance's P, offline members included (GAP-76, LIM-03)<br>- DisassociateMsg is rejected when Γa.Parent != null (GAP-76, LIM-04)<br>Open (GAP-89): whether CloseMsg is allowed on an instance whose ChildList is non-null, and whether excluded participants count as members of P for Close. | GAP-45 (D; residual GAP-89), GAP-72 (D), GAP-76 (D), GAP-89 (OPEN) |

| ID | Requirement | Class | Source | Acceptance criterion | Gap |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|

(2) (LocalState), Alg. 1-3, and for H(psi_{pi,li}) in OfflineResolving. | PM | §3.2(1), p. 3288; Alg. 1-3, p. 3289; §4.3.5, p. 3294 | All of these call sites use the same H, applied to raw 32-byte digests or the decided encodings (GAP-02, GAP-03). | GAP-02 (D), GAP-03 (D) |

| DELIA-CRYPTO-003 | Signatures use a digital signature scheme (Gen, Sign, Vrfy). Each participant's (PK_pi, SK_pi) comes from Gen. | PM | §4.3.1, p. 3291 | - | GAP-27 (D; residual GAP-86), GAP-49 (D) |
| DELIA-CRYPTO-004 | ECDSA is given as an example scheme ("(e.g., ECDSA)"). §6.1 does not name the scheme the prototype used. ECDSA is permitted, not mandated. No curve is specified. Ed25519, BLS, Schnorr and any aggregation are not supported by the text. | PP | §4.3.1, p. 3291; §6.1 (silent) | User-decided (GAP-27 (b)): secp256k1 ECDSA over a SHA-256 digest. This is an implementation decision, not paper-mandated; the paper only permits ECDSA as an example. | GAP-27 (D) |
| DELIA-CRYPTO-005 | Signature generation points, all PM: (a) leader signs (G_nu \| leader) [§4.3.2 Step 2]; (b) each participant or child leader signs G_nu after verifying (a) [Step 2]; (c) root leader signs no-signed G_nu' with SK [§4.3.2 stage 3]; (d) participants sign GlobalState in offline confirmation [§4.3.5]; (e) the allegedly offline server signs its latest state to cancel [§4.3.5]; (f) participants sign GlobalState in online confirmation [§4.3.5]; (g) signed voting message [§4.3.4]; (h) new leader signs Sign(G_{nu+1} \| NewLeader_x) [§4.3.4]; (i) signed (G_nu \| Close) [§4.3.6]; (j) each child's signed round state on (G_{nu_ci} \| Close) [§4.3.6]. | PM | as listed | TEST-41: each point produces a signature that Vrfy accepts, and fails after a one-bit change to the signed content. Signed bytes = purpose \|\| signer_role \|\| encoded object (GAP-72, GAP-73): (a) and (b) purpose STATE; (h) purpose RECOVERY; (i) and (j) purpose CLOSE; (g) purpose VOTE. The purposes for (c)-(f) and the VOTE payload are open (GAP-86). | GAP-27 (D), GAP-72 (D; residual GAP-86), GAP-73 (D) |
| DELIA-CRYPTO-006 | Signature verification points, all PM: (a) recipients verify the leader's signature [§4.3.2 Step 2]; (b) SCC Rule 3 runs Vrfy on every signature in a submitted signed state [§4.3.3], also for OfflineMsg, OnlineMsg and DisputeMsg via the rules [§4.3.4, §4.3.5]; (c) SCC verifies DisassociateMsg signatures [§4.3.6]. The paper does not state verification for: no-signed state receipt, cancel message, vote messages, CloseMsg. | PM | as listed | TEST-18, TEST-26, TEST-39, TEST-40. User-decided additions (IMPL, not paper-mandated), which fill the verification points the paper does not state:<br>- no-signed state receipt verifies the root leader's signature (GAP-26 correction)<br>- vote signatures are verified as dispute evidence, together with the checkpoint's own signatures (GAP-35/36, GAP-64)<br>- cancel verifies pi's signature over the same G_nu (GAP-43)<br>- CloseMsg is verified like a signed-round state, with purpose CLOSE and all of P (GAP-45, GAP-76)<br>- the auditor verifies each no-signed state's root-leader signature (GAP-61) | GAP-26 (D), GAP-36 (D), GAP-43 (D), GAP-45 (D), GAP-61 (CP: GAP-81), GAP-64 (D; residual GAP-88), GAP-76 (D) |
| DELIA-CRYPTO-007 | Unspecified: byte serialization of signed objects; the meaning of the `\|` operator in (G_nu \| p4), (G_nu \| Close) and Sign(G_{nu+1} \| NewLeader_x); the message argument missing from Vrfy(PK_pi, S_pi); how a signature "contains the leader's public key". | IMPL (user-decided) | Not stated | Ruling (GAP-27, GAP-72, GAP-73):<br>- fixed-size hashes/keys are concatenated as raw bytes; structured objects get one deterministic binary encoding first<br>- the signed digest = SHA-256 of purpose \|\| signer_role \|\| encoded object, each prefix a fixed one-byte enumeration; purposes STATE, CLOSE, RECOVERY, VOTE<br>- the paper's "\| p", "\| Close" and "\| NewLeader" suffixes are expressed by the purpose and signer-role bytes; sid is not included (GAP-09; cross-instance replay is LIM-02)<br>- "signature contains the leader's public key" = one valid signature verifies under the expected leader's PK<br>Open (GAP-86): the complete signer-role set, the purposes used by offline, cancel, online and no-signed signatures, and the VOTE payload. Open conflict (GAP-87): one signature filling two roles (GAP-68). | GAP-27 (D), GAP-72 (D; residual GAP-86), GAP-73 (D), GAP-87 (OPEN) |
| DELIA-CRYPTO-008 | Unspecified: how a signature public key PK_pi relates to the Ethereum "address" used in OffList, NewLeader and OnlineMsg handling (INC-22). | IMPL (user-decided) | §4.3.3-§4.3.5 | Ruling (GAP-28 (a)): one secp256k1 key per participant; its Ethereum address is derived from that key; no second identity mapping. OffList, NewLeader and OnlineMsg "addresses" are these derived addresses. | GAP-28 (D) |
| DELIA-CRYPTO-009 | Unspecified: key generation, distribution and pre-negotiation of P; storage of SK_pi. | IMPL (user-decided) | Not stated | Ruling (GAP-49): each participant's secp256k1 key is generated at provisioning; P is fixed in the experiment config; the private key is stored locally; no runtime PKI. | GAP-49 (D) |

User-decided (GAP-48): mutual TLS from a private test CA; minimum TLS 1.2; TLS 1.3 if negotiated. | GAP-48 (D) |

REST, gRPC, WebSocket, protobuf and JSON are not chosen. | IMPL (user-decided) | Not stated | Ruling (GAP-47, GAP-73): TLS over TCP; frame = uint32 frame_length (big-endian, number of bytes that follow) \|\| uint8 message_type \|\| message-specific fields; raw bytes, not hex; no REST, gRPC or queues. message_type codes are delegated to the implementer and frozen in the normative test-vector file (GAP-73). Open: record-hash payload fields (GAP-05). | GAP-47 (D), GAP-73 (D), GAP-05 (OPEN) |

## 19. Smart-contract requirements

**Conflict statement (must be preserved):**
- Original paper prototype: Java off-chain algorithms and protocols + web3js APIs for server-to-HMSC interaction + Solidity HMSC contracts on Ethereum 1.8.1 (§6.1, p. 3296).
- Required reproduction: Go off-chain algorithms and protocols + a Go-side Ethereum client interface (library not chosen; GAP-50) + Solidity HMSC contract on Ethereum.
- Replacing the Solidity/Ethereum contract with a Go implementation or simulated ledger is a deviation from the paper. It requires explicit user approval.

| ID | Requirement | Class | Source | Acceptance criterion | Gap |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| DELIA-SC-001 | HMSC's SCC is a smart contract of executable code pre-deployed on the distributed ledger, and it maintains instance state by consensus of all ledger nodes. | PM | §4.2, p. 3291; §2.1, p. 3287 | The contract is deployed before Init, and instance state is on-chain. | - |
| DELIA-SC-002 | The contract is written in Solidity and runs on Ethereum (prototype: Ethereum 1.8.1). The reproduction keeps Solidity on Ethereum. A Go replacement is a flagged deviation. | EXP | §6.1, p. 3296 | The repository contains a Solidity HMSC contract. No Go ledger simulator stands in for it without recorded approval. User-decided (GAP-50): a real private Ethereum network of 10 nodes; Geth compatible with 1.8.1 and a period-compatible Solidity compiler; tool upgrades only as labelled environment substitutions (DEV-16). | GAP-50 (D), GAP-78 (D) |
| DELIA-SC-003 | The prototype used web3js for server-to-contract interaction. In the reproduction a Go Ethereum client interface replaces it. The client library is implementation-specific and not chosen here. The contract ABI and semantics must not change because of the client choice. | EXP | §6.1, p. 3296 | Off-chain Go code reaches the contract only through a Go Ethereum client library compatible with the node (GAP-50). | GAP-50 (D), GAP-51 (NND) |
| DELIA-SC-004 | The contract provides on-chain entry points for Init (InitMsg), federation (FederateMsg), Update, DisputeResolving (DisputeMsg, random new-leader selection, NewLeader), OfflineResolving (OfflineMsg, challenge, cancel, OnlineMsg), Close (CloseMsg) and disassociation (DisassociateMsg). | PM | §4.3.1-§4.3.6; §6.2.2 ("five on-chain protocols"), p. 3297 | Each listed message has a contract function, plus a permissionless FinalizeOffline entry point (GAP-63, DEV-19). A dispute recovery state is submitted through Update (GAP-70). Confirm has none (GAP-57 (a), DEV-15). | GAP-57 (D), GAP-63 (D), GAP-70 (D) |
| DELIA-SC-005 | Block height is obtainable from the smart contract, for the offline challenge time. | PM | §4.3.5, p. 3294 | A contract read returns the current block number; OfflineMsg records startBlock (GAP-43, GAP-63). | GAP-43 (D), GAP-63 (D; residual GAP-85) |
| DELIA-SC-006 | Unspecified: Go Ethereum client library; Ethereum node software and version (1.8.1 is a 2018-era client); Solidity compiler version; private-chain consensus, block time and gas configuration. | IMPL (user-decided) | Not stated (§6.1 gives only "Ethereum 1.8.1") | Ruling (GAP-50):<br>- real private Ethereum network with 10 ledger nodes, not a Go simulation<br>- Geth compatible with 1.8.1 and a period-compatible Solidity compiler, or upgraded tools with unchanged contract semantics labelled an environment substitution (DEV-16)<br>- fixed PoA/Clique, not attributed to DELIA<br>- fixed 1 s block time for every experiment, with no per-figure tuning; a block time the paper explicitly gives for an experiment would take precedence (GAP-78)<br>- a Go Ethereum client library compatible with the node replaces web3js | GAP-50 (D), GAP-78 (D) |
| DELIA-SC-007 | Unspecified: on-chain storage of state history (needed by Alg. 3 and Alg. 4) and other storage beyond Γ (ARCH-011). | IMPL (user-decided) | Not stated | Ruling (GAP-15, revised by GAP-61; GAP-64): no on-chain history array; Γ.G is the latest state only. Servers retain the complete GlobalState history off-chain. Accepted signed anchors are also emitted as events, which the auditor uses for cross-checking. Alg. 4 runs off-chain over the retained signed-state history, and SCC does not re-execute it (GAP-64). Other extra contract state is listed in ARCH-011. | GAP-15 (D), GAP-34 (D), GAP-60 (CP: GAP-81), GAP-61 (CP: GAP-81), GAP-64 (D) |

## 21. Go implementation boundaries

| ID | Requirement | Class | Source | Acceptance criterion | Gap |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| DELIA-GO-001 | The prototype coded DELIA's algorithms and protocols in Java. Per the user brief, the reproduction codes all off-chain algorithms and protocols in Go with identical algorithms, message flows, data structures and asymptotic behavior. | EXP | §6.1, p. 3296 | No off-chain Java or JavaScript component exists. | - |
| DELIA-GO-002 | Package decomposition derived only from paper responsibilities (organizational; adds no behavior). | IMPL | Derived from §3.1 Fig. 3, §3.2-§3.4, §4.3 | Every package maps to a row of the table below. No other top-level packages exist. | - |
| DELIA-GO-003 | Alg. 1-4 and the Confirm steps execute sequentially as written. No goroutine parallelizes any step of an algorithm. Alg. 2 and Alg. 3 recompute MHTGen and hashes without caching. | PM | Alg. 1-4, pp. 3289, 3293; §6.2.3 ("cannot be performed in parallel"), p. 3298 | Code review: no `go` statements, worker pools or memo caches inside these packages. | - |
| DELIA-GO-004 | Unspecified: process model (one OS process per server vs in-process simulation for tests) and the concurrency model for receiving messages asynchronously. Concurrency needed only to model independent servers receiving messages is not an optimization, but the choice is unspecified. | IMPL (user-decided) | Not stated | Ruling (GAP-54): one OS process per DELIA server, each with its own TLS endpoint and a sequential protocol handler; an in-process multi-node harness is used for tests only; no worker pools, no parallel hashing. | GAP-54 (D) |

Recommended package layout (DELIA-GO-002). This is a proposal, not paper-mandated.

| Package | Responsibility (paper anchor) | Must not contain |
|---|---|---|
| `hash` | H(.) via crypto/sha256; concatenation per GAP-02 (CRYPTO-001/002) | alternative hashes |
| `logcache` | psi_{pi,li} per Eq. (1); per-server set of psi (§3.2(1), §3.3 stage 1) | ring buffers, eviction, DB |
| `merkle` | MHTGen per GAP-01 decision (§3.2(2)) | incremental or cached trees, proofs, multiproofs |
| `localstate` | Eq. (2) | caching of prior omega beyond what Eq. (2) needs |
| `globalstate` | Eq. (3); Alg. 1 GlobalGen; Alg. 2 StateVerify | parallel verification |
| `lsg` | stage 1 and stage 2 flows (§3.3) | batching of record-hash broadcasts |
| `auditor` | Alg. 3 LogVerify; retrieval of logs and of the GlobalState history; anchor cross-check against events (§3.4 stage 2; GAP-61) | inclusion proofs, binary search, parallel batches |
| `hmsc/confirm` | leader election, signed and no-signed round states (§4.3.2) | alternative election or consensus libraries |
| `hmsc/dispute` | Alg. 4 and dispute client flow (§4.3.4) | improved checkpoint selection |
| `hmsc/offline` | offline and online client flows (§4.3.5) | heartbeats or timeouts not decided under GAP-41 |
| `hmsc/closure` | Close and Disassociate client flows (§4.3.6) | archival or cleanup |
| `hmsc/chain` | Go-side binding to the Solidity SCC (GAP-50) | contract logic re-implemented in Go |
| `sig` | (Gen, Sign, Vrfy) per GAP-27 (§4.3.1) | aggregation |
| `messages` | Go structs for NET-003..NET-032 payloads (format per GAP-47) | fields not in the paper unless marked IMPL with GAP ref |
| `contracts/` (Solidity) | SCC (§4.2-§4.3) | - |

## 22. Functional test requirements

Rules:
- Tests check only paper-supported behavior. None asserts latency, throughput or storage thresholds.
- A test marked **BLOCKED** cannot have fixed expected values until the listed gaps are decided. Its structure is still specified.
- Test IDs are `DELIA-TEST-NN`, abbreviated TEST-NN elsewhere.

| Test | Covers | Setup | Expected result | Blocked by |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| TEST-01 LogCacheQueue formation | DATA-002/003/004/006, LSG-002/003/004 | n = 3, theta = 4; p1 generates 8 records | Every server holds psi_{p1,0} = {H(d1)..H(d4)} and psi_{p1,1} = {H(d5)..H(d8)}. p1 holds the same. Each element is SHA-256 of the UTF-8 line without line-ending bytes; duplicates kept. | GAP-05 (OPEN) |
| TEST-02 Merkle root generation | DATA-008/009 | fixed psi | MHTGen is deterministic; changing one element changes the root. Vectors per the DATA-009 ruling, e.g. theta = 3: root = H(H(L1\|\|L2) \|\| H(L3\|\|L3)). theta = 1: root = the single leaf (GAP-74). | - |
| TEST-03 Initial LocalState | DATA-010 | batch 0 complete | omega_{p,0} = SHA-256(raw 32-byte root_{p,0}) | - |
| TEST-04 Chained LocalState | DATA-011/012 | batches 0, 1, 2 | omega_{p,1} = H(root_{p,1} \|\| omega_{p,0}); omega_{p,2} = H(root_{p,2} \|\| omega_{p,1}); altering batch 0 changes omega_{p,1} and omega_{p,2}; inputs are raw 64-byte concatenations | - |
| TEST-05 GlobalState generation (Alg. 1) | DATA-013/014/015, ALG1-001..004 | G_{nu-1} with n = 3; p2 completes batch l > 0 | Output G_nu: entries 1 and 3 identical to input; entry 2 = H(root \|\| old entry 2); serial = nu-1 + 1; positions follow the P order frozen at Init Encoded-form vectors use the GAP-73 layout. | - |
| TEST-06 StateVerify success | ALG2-001..006 | honest (psi, root, G_{nu-1}, G_nu) | returns 1 | - |
| TEST-07 StateVerify invalid root | ALG2-003 | root replaced by a different value | returns 0; GlobalGen not invoked (spy) | - |
| TEST-08 StateVerify invalid GlobalState | ALG2-005 | (a) G_nu entry i altered; (b) another server's entry altered | returns 0 in both cases; (c) same entries with a different nu also returns 0 (GAP-09 ruling) | - |
| TEST-09 LSG stage 2 end-to-end | LSG-006..010 | honest pi; then pi broadcasts a corrupted G_nu | honest: every pj's latest GlobalState = G_nu. Corrupted: each pj broadcasts a failure notification and keeps G_{nu-1}. The adopted state is provisional (DEV-07); history is retained; the failure notification is (sender, pi, nu, reason) and no dispute is invoked. | GAP-05 (OPEN; wire format of preceding record hashes) |
| TEST-10 Real-time record broadcast | LSG-002 | pi generates theta records | theta broadcasts, one per record, each observed before the next record's broadcast; no batched message | GAP-05 (OPEN) |
| TEST-11 Alg. 3 valid logs | AUDIT-001..009 | untampered logs, G_1..G_nu | ErrList empty; audit passes. States = the complete history from a server, with no-signed states verified by root-leader signature and serial continuity and anchors cross-checked against events (DEV-10). Comparisons are skipped while pi's slot is the zero sentinel (GAP-75). | GAP-81, GAP-83 (end-to-end retrieval and cross-check only) |
| TEST-12 Alg. 3 tampered batch | AUDIT-006/007 | modify one record in batch b of pi | b is in ErrList. Under the literal listing (omega reset to the ledger value after each comparison), batches after b are not added. Expectation fixed by DEV-01: single linear pass, so b is added once and later batches are not added. | GAP-81, GAP-83 (end-to-end retrieval and cross-check only) |
| TEST-13 Alg. 3 deleted and forged records | AUDIT-013 | delete one record; separately insert one record | The batch containing the change is reported. Per GAP-16: the affected batch is added to ErrList and the audit continues; a trailing partial batch is ignored. | GAP-81, GAP-83 (end-to-end retrieval and cross-check only) |
| TEST-14 Alg. 3 no optimization | AUDIT-010, GO-003 | instrumented H and MHTGen | H called once per record per rebuilt batch; MHTGen once per inner iteration; no goroutines spawned; l never reset (DEV-01) li is derived by replay (GAP-61). | - |
| TEST-15 HMSC Init | INIT-002..004 | 3 participants submit InitMsg | Γ exists with P = submitted keys; OffList, NewLeader, Parent, ChildList null; sid unique across two Inits; G = current domain state at creation | GAP-20 (OPEN) |
| TEST-16 Federation | INIT-007/008, ARCH-005/006 | Γ1(p1..p3), Γ2(p4..p6); leaders send FederateMsg | Γ3 exists with P = {PK_p1..PK_p6}, Parent = null, OffList empty, NewLeader null, ChildList = {sid_1, sid_2} and G = the current accepted domain state; sid_3 is a 256-bit value generated by SCC and differs from sid_1 and sid_2; Γ1 and Γ2 stay open with Parent = sid_3; Γ3 activates only after both child leaders submit; a sender that is not the child's current leader (GAP-60 rule) is rejected | GAP-82 |
| TEST-17 Leader election rotation | CONFIRM-002/003 | P = {K1 < K2 < K3}; 4 rounds | leaders K1, K2, K3, K1 (keys compared as fixed-width unsigned big-endian) | - (see TEST-17b) |
| TEST-18 Signed round state (single instance) | CONFIRM-005..010, CONFIRM-013 | 4 participants, no children | participant -> leader; leader signs and broadcasts; all verify and sign; leader holds 4 valid signatures; 3 interaction phases. A participant given a bad S_leader does not sign. Signatures are over purpose STATE and the signer's role (GAP-72). | GAP-86 (role set) |
| TEST-19 Signed round state (hierarchy) | CONFIRM-010/011/012 | Fig. 7/8 topology | G_nu^{G2} from p6 reaches the root leader, then all six participants hold it | GAP-24 (OPEN), GAP-87 |
| TEST-20 No-signed round state | CONFIRM-006, 014, 015, 018, ARCH-008 | beta = 2 after a signed state at serial s | states s+1 and s+2 are relayed unsigned by intermediate leaders, approved and signed by the root leader, and distributed downward; state s+3 is not approved as no-signed in this round; the state with nu' != nu*+1 is not approved; every approved state carries exactly one signature, the root leader's (GAP-26 correction); the round ends after 2 accepted no-signed states. (b) A recipient given a bad root-leader signature or a serial gap does not adopt the state and keeps its latest. | GAP-86 (purpose of the no-signed signature) |
| TEST-21 No-signed desynchronization | CONFIRM-016 | receiver's latest serial is nu'-2 | receiver initiates synchronization with its leader before accepting: it sends (fromSerial, toSerial) to its direct leader and verifies signatures and continuity | - |
| TEST-22 Valid Update | UPDATE-002..011 | signed state with all rules satisfied | Γx.G = submitted state; one event with the full signed state emitted | GAP-81 |
| TEST-23 Rule 1 violation | UPDATE-004 | nu <= nu* | rejected; Γx.G unchanged nu* is the root instance's stored serial, so a child update with nu <= that serial is also rejected (GAP-66, DEV-20) | - |
| TEST-24 Rule 2 violation | UPDATE-005, OFFLINE-010 | pk in OffList; entry changed | rejected The same holds for an excluded participant's slot (GAP-65). | - |
| TEST-25 Rule 3 count violation | UPDATE-006 | one signature short of num - numoff | rejected (exactly num - numoff distinct expected signers required) | GAP-87 (parent-instance case only) |
| TEST-26 Rule 3 invalid signature | UPDATE-007 | one signature corrupted | rejected | - |
| TEST-27 Rule 3 missing leader signature | UPDATE-008 | leader's signature absent; the expected leader is derived on-chain from the leader recorded in the latest accepted signed-round state (GAP-60); a leader field in the message is ignored | rejected | GAP-81 |
| TEST-28 Rule 4 | UPDATE-009/010 | parent Γx; a child's stored serial >= nu | parent update accepted; each direct child with stored serial > nu has its G set null; grandchildren untouched (DEV-06) | GAP-82 (leader derivation for a nulled child G) |
| TEST-29 No-signed state on-chain | UPDATE-001, CONFIRM-018 | submit a state carrying only the leader's signature | rejected | - |
| TEST-30 Alg. 4, no children | DISPUTE-005/009 | Fig. 9(a) and 9(b) sequences | prose expectation: G_4 (9a), G_3^{G1} (9b); prose expectation only (DEV-02); history = the retained signed-state history (GAP-64) | - |
| TEST-31 Alg. 4, with children | DISPUTE-006/007/008/009 | Fig. 9(c) and 9(d) | prose: own state G_2^{G3} (9c); child state G_3^{G1} (9d); result = max. Prose semantics only (DEV-02); the literal-listing result is not asserted. | GAP-24 (OPEN; what signed states parents hold) |
| TEST-32 Malicious leader replacement | DISPUTE-010, 012..018 | dispute against the leader; a strict-majority vote certifies the checkpoint and goes against the leader | SCC verifies the checkpoint's own signatures and the vote evidence, applying no State Update Rules (DEV-04); NewLeader_x set to an eligible member of P (the removed leader and finalized OffList members excluded); all instances at the checkpoint; the removed leader's slot stays frozen at the checkpoint; the next accepted state is the recovery anchor G_{nu+1}, submitted immediately with NewLeader_x's required leader signature (purpose RECOVERY); wrong serial or signer rejected; NewLeader_x null once the recovery anchor is accepted; rotation continues after NewLeader_x; off-chain no-signed states after the checkpoint discarded | GAP-88 |
| TEST-33 Voting exclusion | DISPUTE-011 | vote favoring initiator / favoring leader | all current P members vote (including leader and initiator); a strict majority decides; the loser is removed from active P, not placed in OffList The loser's slot keeps its position, frozen at the checkpoint, and later changes to it are rejected (GAP-65). | GAP-88 (hierarchy effects only) |
| TEST-34 Offline transition | OFFLINE-002..007, 009 | pi unreachable | OfflineMsg accepted and startBlock stored; FinalizeOffline rejected before the one-block interval ends; after it, FinalizeOffline from any account adds (PK_pi, H(psi_{pi,li})) to OffList, with H over the active LogCacheQueue including a partial batch | GAP-85 |
| TEST-35 Offline cancellation | OFFLINE-008/009 | pi online, sends (G_nu, S_pi) within the window | OffList unchanged; a cancel signed over a different G_nu is rejected A later FinalizeOffline adds nothing. | GAP-85 |
| TEST-36 Offline LocalState frozen | OFFLINE-010 | after TEST-34, submit a state changing pi's entry | rejected | - |
| TEST-37 Online transition | OFFLINE-011..015 | pi returns | OnlineMsg accepted; pi not in OffList; pi's GlobalState and current-batch LogCacheQueue data equal its direct leader's; OnlineMsg accepted without S_pi; pi's next batch chains from its frozen LocalState pi's log reader was paused while pi was offline (EXPERIMENT-ONLY, GAP-69), so pi's records stay batch-aligned; the OnlineNotification carries (sid, returningParticipant). | - |
| TEST-38 Instance closure | CLOSE-001..005 | signed (G_nu \| Close) via CloseMsg | instance closed only when the Close state carries purpose CLOSE and signatures from every member of P, OffList members included (a Close missing an offline member's signature is rejected); subsequent Update rejected; instance data still readable | - |
| TEST-39 Disassociation | CLOSE-006/007 | Γ3 over Γ1 and Γ2; both children sign close states | Γ3 closed; Γ1 and Γ2 have Parent = null and stay open A DisassociateMsg for a parent whose Parent != null is rejected (GAP-76). | - |
| TEST-40 Disassociation invalid signature | CLOSE-007 | one child signature corrupted | Γ3 remains open | - |
| TEST-41 Signature points | CRYPTO-005/006 | every signing point in CRYPTO-005 | Vrfy (secp256k1 ECDSA over SHA-256) accepts; a one-bit modification is rejected | GAP-86 |
| TEST-42 Faithfulness review | Section 26, GO-003 | code review checklist | none of the Section 26 items is present | - |

## 24. Requirement traceability matrix

Abbreviations:
- **Class:** PM = PAPER-MANDATED; PP = PAPER-PERMITS; IMPL = IMPLEMENTATION-NECESSARY-BUT-UNSPECIFIED; EXP = EXPERIMENT-ONLY.
- **Component:** Go package or `contracts/` from Section 21; "deploy" = deployment/configuration.
- **Class "IMPL (u-d)":** IMPLEMENTATION-NECESSARY-BUT-UNSPECIFIED, user-decided (revision 2).
- **Open gap? (revision 3):**
  - "N" = every referenced gap is DECIDED with no pending conflict or residual.
  - "Y (...)" lists the blocking gaps: "OPEN" = unanswered; "CP: x" = decided, conflict pending in GAP-x; "res x" = residual open in GAP-x.
  - Status legend in Section 2.6.

| ID | Requirement | Class | Paper source | Implementation component | Test/acceptance criterion | Open gap? |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| DELIA-SYS-001 | Three entity kinds only | PM | §2.1, Fig. 1 | deploy | Section 3 criterion | N |
| DELIA-SYS-002 | Authenticated, authorized alliance | PM | §2.1 | deploy, sig | Section 3 criterion | N |
| DELIA-SYS-003 | Server functions LSG + HMSC | PM | §2.1, Fig. 1, §3.3 | lsg, hmsc/* | Section 3 criterion | N |
| DELIA-SYS-004 | Auditor reads logs, states, instance | PM | §2.1, §3.4 | auditor | TEST-11, TEST-45 | Y (61 CP: 81; res 83) |
