### GAP-01
- **Status:** DECIDED
- **Revision 3:** GAP-74 (a): with a single leaf, root = the leaf; H(leaf \|\| leaf) is computed only when a node really has two children.
- **User ruling:** (a). Leaves = psi hashes in record order. Parent = SHA-256(left \|\| right). Odd node duplicated. (The user's row was truncated; (a) as written in this spec.)
- **Ruling classification:** IMPLEMENTATION-NECESSARY-BUT-UNSPECIFIED (user-decided)
- **Residual (closed in revision 3):** theta = 1 is covered by GAP-74.
- **Topic:** Merkle hash tree construction in MHTGen.
- **Why a decision is needed:** Roots must be bit-exact across all servers, the auditor and any test vectors. Alg. 2 compares roots for equality.
- **Paper says:** root_{pi,li} is "the root of the Merkle hash tree constructed from a LogCacheQueue" (§3.2(2), p. 3288). Fig. 2 shows a 4-leaf binary tree (Hash 0-0, 0-1, 1-0, 1-1 -> Hash 0, Hash 1 -> root).
- **Paper does NOT say:**
  - whether leaves are the psi elements as-is or re-hashed
  - leaf order
  - odd-count or non-power-of-two handling (duplicate last, promote, pad)
  - internal-node input (H(left \|\| right)?)
  - domain separation between leaves and nodes
- **Classification:** IMPL.
- **Possible choices:**
  - (a) leaves = psi elements in xi order; parent = H(left \|\| right); odd node duplicated
  - (b) same as (a) but odd node promoted unchanged
  - (c) leaves re-hashed with domain-separation prefixes
- **Effect on faithfulness / benchmark:** Any choice is an unverifiable interpretation. Hashing cost per batch varies slightly (affects Table 2 and Fig. 14 comparisons). Detection semantics are unaffected.
- **User decision required:** YES

### GAP-02
- **Status:** DECIDED
- **User ruling:** Order per Eq. (2): H(root \|\| omega_prev), and H(root) for l = 0. Representation: raw 32-byte SHA-256 digests, not hex.
- **Ruling classification:** IMPLEMENTATION-NECESSARY-BUT-UNSPECIFIED (user-decided)
- **Topic:** Concatenation `||`, operand representation, and the Fig. 2 operand order.
- **Why a decision is needed:** H(root \|\| omega) must be byte-exact.
- **Paper says:** Eq. (2), Alg. 1 and Alg. 3 all use H(root \|\| omega_{prev}) and H(root) for li = 0. Fig. 2 draws omega_{p,l-1} before root_{p,l}, and "null" in the l = 0 row (INC-01).
- **Paper does NOT say:** whether hash values are concatenated as raw digest bytes or as encoded strings (hex or base64, as Java prototypes often use); what "null" in Fig. 2 would encode.
- **Classification:** IMPL.
- **Possible choices:**
  - Order: follow Eq. (2) (three textual sources agree) or Fig. 2.
  - Representation: raw 32-byte digests or hex strings.
- **Effect on faithfulness / benchmark:** The order question is resolvable in favor of Eq. (2) by majority of the paper's own statements; the user should confirm. The representation choice changes every hash value but not the algorithm.
- **User decision required:** YES (representation). The order is proposed as Eq. (2) pending user confirmation.

### GAP-03
- **Status:** DECIDED
- **User ruling:** (a) one UTF-8 log line, excluding line-ending bytes; no parsing. Dataset: the [22] (Zhu et al.) corpus, with the exact file manifest frozen in the experiment config; the file selection is not paper-specified.
- **Ruling classification:** IMPLEMENTATION-NECESSARY-BUT-UNSPECIFIED (user-decided)
- **Topic:** Log record encoding and log format.
- **Why a decision is needed:** H(delta_{pi,xi}) needs a byte string per record, and the log reader must delimit records.
- **Paper says:** Records are "security-relevant chronological records" (§1). The prototype uses "a log reader program and public audit log dataset [22]" (§6.1).
- **Paper does NOT say:** record boundaries, character encoding, whether line terminators or metadata are hashed, or which files of [22] are used.
- **Classification:** IMPL.
- **Possible choices:** (a) one text line = one record, UTF-8 bytes excluding the newline; (b) including the newline; (c) a parsed structured form.
- **Effect on faithfulness / benchmark:** Affects hash values and the per-record hashing cost (Fig. 14). The protocol is otherwise unaffected.
- **User decision required:** YES

### GAP-04
- **Status:** DECIDED
- **User ruling:** (a) keep record order and duplicates; a batch is exactly theta hashes; no deduplication.
- **Ruling classification:** IMPLEMENTATION-NECESSARY-BUT-UNSPECIFIED (user-decided)
- **Topic:** LogCacheQueue "hash set" semantics.
- **Why a decision is needed:** The Merkle input must be an ordered sequence. Equal hashes (for example, identical log lines) may or may not collapse.
- **Paper says:** "LogCacheQueue is a local hash set, which stores different hash values of each server's log records in a batch" (§3.2(1)). Eq. (1) uses set braces with an index-ordered enumeration. The name contains "Queue".
- **Paper does NOT say:** whether order is by xi; whether duplicates are dropped (which would make a batch contain fewer than theta leaves).
- **Classification:** IMPL.
- **Possible choices:** (a) ordered by xi, duplicates kept (sequence of exactly theta); (b) set semantics with duplicates removed.
- **Effect on faithfulness / benchmark:** Choice (b) is a form of deduplication that the brief prohibits unless stated. The paper's wording arguably states it, so the user must rule.
- **User decision required:** YES

### GAP-05
- **Status:** OPEN (not answered by the user)
- **User ruling:** none. Framing is decided separately (GAP-47); payload fields and gap/out-of-order handling are not.
- **Topic:** Record-hash broadcast payload and delivery.
- **Why a decision is needed:** pj must know which server and which position (xi, li) a received hash belongs to, and what to do when a hash is missing or out of order.
- **Paper says:** "The server pi broadcasts H(delta_{pi,xi}) ... to other servers ... once the xi-th log record is generated. When the server pj receives H(delta_{pi,xi}), it stores this item in its local LogCacheQueue" (§3.3 stage 1).
- **Paper does NOT say:** message fields beyond the hash; reliable-delivery assumptions; how gaps are handled.
- **Classification:** IMPL.
- **Possible choices:** (a) payload (sender id, xi, H) over an ordered reliable stream (TLS over TCP); (b) payload H only, with ordering inferred from the per-sender stream.
- **Effect on faithfulness / benchmark:** Adds identifier fields the paper does not define; they must be labelled IMPL. Negligible benchmark effect.
- **User decision required:** YES

### GAP-06
- **Status:** DECIDED
- **Revision 3:** GAP-75 (a): skip the Alg. 3 comparison for a server until it has completed its first batch. G_0[i] = 0^256 is only an initialization sentinel and is never treated as a LocalState produced from logs.
- **User ruling:**
  - G_0 = n fixed positions, each the all-zero 32-byte digest ("no LocalState yet").
  - nu = 0 is initialization; the first generated state is G_1.
  - Before a server's first batch its slot stays the zero digest.
  - G_0 is not put on-chain.
- **Ruling classification:** IMPLEMENTATION-NECESSARY-BUT-UNSPECIFIED (user-decided)
- **Residual (closed in revision 3):** see GAP-75.
- **Topic:** Initial GlobalState G_0, initial serial number, and the entry for a server before its first batch.
- **Why a decision is needed:** Alg. 1 needs G_{nu-1} for the very first batch in the domain. Alg. 3 starts at G_1.
- **Paper says:** Nothing about initial values. Alg. 3 iterates k from 1.
- **Paper does NOT say:** G_0 contents, whether nu starts at 0 or 1, or the placeholder value of an unset omega.
- **Classification:** IMPL.
- **Possible choices:**
  - G_0 = n empty or zero-length entries with nu = 0, or n all-zero digests with nu = 0.
  - Whether G_0 is submitted on-chain.
- **Effect on faithfulness / benchmark:** Affects Alg. 3 boundary behavior and TEST-05 and TEST-11 vectors.
- **User decision required:** YES

### GAP-07
- **Status:** DECIDED
- **User ruling:** (a) Eq. (2) governs; Alg. 1's missing l = 0 branch is treated as an omission.
- **Ruling classification:** IMPLEMENTATION-NECESSARY-BUT-UNSPECIFIED (user-decided)
- **Documented deviation:** DEV-13. The implementation departs from the literal Alg. 1 listing (p. 3289).
- **Topic:** Alg. 1 behavior when li = 0.
- **Why a decision is needed:** Alg. 1 always computes H(root \|\| omega_{pi,li-1}), but Eq. (2) requires H(root) for li = 0.
- **Paper says:** Eq. (2) defines both branches. Alg. 1 shows only the chained branch.
- **Paper does NOT say:** whether GlobalGen branches on li = 0.
- **Classification:** IMPL.
- **Possible choices:** (a) GlobalGen applies Eq. (2) in full, branching on li; (b) apply Alg. 1 literally, chaining with the GAP-06 placeholder (this contradicts Eq. (2)).
- **Effect on faithfulness / benchmark:** (b) makes LSG-produced omega_{pi,0} differ from the auditor's H(root) in Alg. 3, so every audit would flag batch 0. (a) matches Alg. 3 but departs from the literal Alg. 1 listing.
- **User decision required:** YES

### GAP-08
- **Status:** DECIDED
- **Revision 3:** GAP-61: li is derived by replaying the complete sequential GlobalState history obtained from a server; whenever slot i changes, its completed-batch counter advances. GAP-65: an excluded participant's slot is kept, frozen at the rollback checkpoint; positions are never deleted or re-indexed.
- **User ruling:** Positions follow the P ordering frozen at Init. Do not add li to GlobalState; the auditor derives li from the retained ordered state history and completed-batch counts.
- **Ruling classification:** IMPLEMENTATION-NECESSARY-BUT-UNSPECIFIED (user-decided)
- **Topic:** Server index i and batch number li inside GlobalState.
- **Why a decision is needed:** Alg. 1 must know which position to replace. Rule 2 must map PK_pi to a position. Alg. 3 must know li per G_k, but Eq. (3) stores only omega values.
- **Paper says:** G_nu = (omega_{p1,l1}, ..., omega_{pn,ln}) (Eq. (3)). Alg. 3 "Parse G_k as (... omega_{pi,li} ...)" and then uses li.
- **Paper does NOT say:** how the server order is fixed (by P order? by PK?), or whether li is carried in the state.
- **Classification:** IMPL.
- **Possible choices:**
  - Order: positions by P order at Init, or by ascending PK.
  - li: carried alongside each omega (a field not in the paper), or derived by the auditor (counting how often position i changed since G_1, which is valid only if the auditor holds every state).
- **Effect on faithfulness / benchmark:** Carrying li adds a data field the paper does not define. Deriving it needs a contiguous state history (links to GAP-15).
- **User decision required:** YES

### GAP-09
- **Status:** DECIDED
- **Revision 3:** GAP-60 needs no round number in the payload. GAP-72 (a): signed bytes = purpose \|\| signer_role \|\| encoded state, still without sid. GAP-73 (a): encoded state = uint64 nu (big-endian) \|\| uint32 entry_count (big-endian) \|\| entry_count x 32-byte LocalState, in the P order frozen at Init.
- **User ruling:** (a) canonical encoding of (nu, ordered entries); nu is bound into the compared and signed object. sid is NOT added to the signed payload; the instance supplies context externally.
- **Ruling classification:** IMPLEMENTATION-NECESSARY-BUT-UNSPECIFIED (user-decided)
- **Residual (closed in revision 3):** the byte layout is fixed by GAP-73.
- **Topic:** GlobalState serialization; whether nu is part of the compared, transmitted and signed object.
- **Why a decision is needed:** Needed for Alg. 2 equality, for signing G_nu, for Rules 1 and 2 on-chain, and for transmitting (root, G_nu).
- **Paper says:** G_nu is an array with serial number nu (Eq. (3)). Signatures are "on the state" (§4.3.2).
- **Paper does NOT say:** the encoding; whether nu is embedded in G or accompanies it; whether a signed object includes the instance sid.
- **Classification:** IMPL.
- **Possible choices:** (a) canonical encoding of (nu, entries); (b) entries only, with nu sent separately; (c) Solidity ABI encoding for on-chain compatibility.
- **Effect on faithfulness / benchmark:** Affects gas (Fig. 13) and signature sizes. If nu is left out of the signed object, Rule 1 can be bypassed; that is a security consequence the user must weigh without the paper's guidance.
- **User decision required:** YES

### GAP-10
- **Status:** DECIDED
- **Revision 3:** GAP-65: the domain-wide layout stays stable after an exclusion; the excluded participant's slot is frozen, not removed.
- **User ruling:** (a) one domain-wide G_nu containing every server's LocalState; instances confirm and propagate that same state.
- **Ruling classification:** IMPLEMENTATION-NECESSARY-BUT-UNSPECIFIED (user-decided)
- **Topic:** Scope of GlobalState and LogCacheQueue sharing: domain-wide vs per state channel instance.
- **Why a decision is needed:** Determines who broadcasts to whom in LSG and what G an instance confirms and stores in a hierarchy.
- **Paper says:** "GlobalState is an array recording the LocalState of all servers in a domain" (§3.2(3)); "server pj maintains {psi_{p1,l1}, ..., psi_{pn,ln}}" (§3.3); "GlobalState data which consists of all LocalState data from the servers in a state channel instance" (§6.2.1, INC-04).
- **Paper does NOT say:** how a child instance's G relates to the root's G.
- **Classification:** IMPL.
- **Possible choices:** (a) one domain-wide G (every instance confirms the same array); (b) one G per instance, covering that instance's P.
- **Effect on faithfulness / benchmark:** Changes Fig. 10(b) storage scaling and the signature content in every protocol.
- **User decision required:** YES

### GAP-11
- **Status:** DECIDED
- **User ruling:** (b) LSG proposals are provisional until HMSC confirmation orders/accepts one. A losing proposal is regenerated from the newly accepted latest G. No merge.
- **Ruling classification:** IMPLEMENTATION-NECESSARY-BUT-UNSPECIFIED (user-decided)
- **Documented deviation:** DEV-07. §3.3 stage 2 (p. 3289) says pj's "local G_{nu-1} is replaced by G_nu"; the ruling makes that replacement provisional.
- **Topic:** Concurrent batch completion (state conflict) in LSG.
- **Why a decision is needed:** Two servers finishing batches at the same time both compute G_nu from the same G_{nu-1}. Receivers' Alg. 2 then accepts whichever arrives first and rejects the other.
- **Paper says:** §4.1 and Fig. 5 describe "state conflict" and say "a rotating leader is elected to organize the state channel".
- **Paper does NOT say:** how the LSG broadcast or Alg. 2 interacts with the leader's ordering; whether the losing server regenerates against the new G.
- **Classification:** IMPL.
- **Possible choices:** (a) the losing server recomputes Alg. 1 against the newly accepted G and re-broadcasts; (b) generated states are only ordered by the leader in Confirm Step 1, and LSG's G_nu is provisional.
- **Effect on faithfulness / benchmark:** Affects Fig. 11(a) interval behavior at small theta and large n.
- **User decision required:** YES

### GAP-12
- **Status:** DECIDED
- **User ruling:** (a) a failed Alg. 2 emits a failure notification and aborts that state-generation attempt. It does NOT invoke dispute. Processing may continue after resync. Option (a) as written above also fixes the payload (sender, pi, nu, reason) and says recipients only record it.
- **Ruling classification:** IMPLEMENTATION-NECESSARY-BUT-UNSPECIFIED (user-decided)
- **Topic:** LSG failure notification and "terminate this stage".
- **Why a decision is needed:** Receivers of the notification need defined behavior, and the sender needs a defined next state.
- **Paper says:** "If they are invalid, pj broadcasts a failure notification and terminate this stage" (§3.3 stage 2).
- **Paper does NOT say:** payload (reason code? root? G_nu?); recipients' reaction; whether termination affects later batches; the link to DisputeResolving.
- **Classification:** IMPL.
- **Possible choices:** (a) notification carries (sender, pi, nu, reason), and recipients only log it; (b) notification triggers the dispute path.
- **Effect on faithfulness / benchmark:** (b) adds protocol coupling the paper does not state.
- **User decision required:** YES

### GAP-13
- **Status:** DECIDED
- **User ruling:** (a) retain all LocalState and GlobalState history; LogCacheQueue is bounded to current-batch state.
- **Ruling classification:** IMPLEMENTATION-NECESSARY-BUT-UNSPECIFIED (user-decided)
- **Topic:** Retention: "replaced" vs "store all"; LogCacheQueue horizon.
- **Why a decision is needed:** Determines memory and disk use and what rejoin sync can supply.
- **Paper says:** "its local G_{nu-1} is replaced by G_nu" (§3.3); "every server needs to store all LocalState and GlobalState data for further verifications"; "the length of LogCacheQueue is always constant" (§6.2.1); a rejoining server syncs "all the GlobalState data and LogCacheQueue data" (§3.3 stage 5).
- **Paper does NOT say:** whether historical G is kept after replacement, or how many past LogCacheQueues are kept.
- **Classification:** IMPL.
- **Possible choices:**
  - (a) keep all G and all omega; keep only current-batch psi per server
  - (b) keep all G, omega and psi
  - (c) keep only the latest G
- **Effect on faithfulness / benchmark:** Directly determines Fig. 10 reproduction. Fig. 10(b) (linear growth) supports keeping all G.
- **User decision required:** YES

### GAP-14
- **Status:** DECIDED
- **Revision 3:** GAP-75 (a) covers the zero-digest slot: no comparison until the server's first completed batch.
- **User ruling:** (a) k++ after each G_k; l++ per reconstructed batch; l is NOT reset per k (single linear pass). Follow the printed indentation: compare with omega_{pi,li} after reconstructing up to the current li.
- **Ruling classification:** IMPLEMENTATION-NECESSARY-BUT-UNSPECIFIED (user-decided)
- **Documented deviation:** DEV-01. Alg. 3 (p. 3289) prints no increments.
- **Topic:** Alg. 3 loop counters and indentation.
- **Why a decision is needed:** The listing never increments k or l and initializes l once. Whether l resets for each k, and whether the comparison sits inside or after the inner loop, changes both the output and the computation.
- **Paper says:** the listing reproduced in Section 9 (p. 3289).
- **Paper does NOT say:** k++ / l++ placement; whether l resets.
- **Classification:** IMPL.
- **Possible choices:**
  - (a) k increments per outer iteration; l increments per inner iteration and persists across k. Each batch is rebuilt once, and omega is reset to the ledger value after each G_k check.
  - (b) l resets to 0 for each k, so all batches 0..li are rebuilt for every G_k. This is O(nu * li) and would re-flag later batches.
  - Also: confirm the indentation of lines 11-13 against the print.
- **Effect on faithfulness / benchmark:** (b) makes audit time roughly quadratic, while §6.2.3 reports time growing "linearly with the size of audit logs", which is consistent with (a). Detection output differs between the two.
- **User decision required:** YES

### GAP-15
- **Status:** DECIDED (revised by GAP-61 in revision 3)
- **Revision 3:** GAP-61 revises this ruling. The auditor obtains the complete sequential GlobalState history off-chain from a server, verifies no-signed states by their root-leader signatures and serial continuity, and cross-checks every signed-round anchor against Ethereum event logs (DEV-10 rewritten). Event logs remain the ledger record of accepted anchors; there is still no on-chain history array. GAP-64: Alg. 4 runs off-chain over retained signed-state history.
- **User ruling:** (c) historical signed-round states are recovered from Ethereum event logs. The contract's G field stays "latest state" only, with no on-chain history array. Only signed states are audit anchors; no-signed states stay off-chain.
- **Ruling classification:** IMPLEMENTATION-NECESSARY-BUT-UNSPECIFIED (user-decided)
- **Documented deviation:** DEV-10. §3.4 stage 2 and Alg. 3 (pp. 3289-3290) take the full sequence {G_1, ..., G_nu}.
- **Topic:** Source of the contiguous state history {G_1..G_nu} for the auditor (and for Alg. 4's "exists" tests).
- **Why a decision is needed:** Γ stores only "the latest state" G, and only signed round states reach the ledger.
- **Paper says:** the auditor obtains "the states {G_1, ..., G_nu} from the distributed ledger" (§3.4 stage 2); "Only signed round states can be accepted by the distributed ledger, and the no-signed round states are stored off-chain as temporary cache" (§4.3.3).
- **Paper does NOT say:** how intermediate (no-signed) states reach the auditor; whether the contract keeps history in storage or in events.
- **Classification:** IMPL.
- **Possible choices:**
  - (a) the contract keeps an append-only history of accepted signed states, and the auditor audits only those (Alg. 3 then iterates over a sparse sequence)
  - (b) the auditor reads signed states from the ledger and no-signed states from servers' caches (contradicts "from the distributed ledger")
  - (c) history via contract event logs
- **Effect on faithfulness / benchmark:** Affects Alg. 3 semantics, Fig. 13(b) storage and gas. It is a core faithfulness question.
- **User decision required:** YES

### GAP-16
- **Status:** DECIDED
- **Revision 3:** GAP-69 (c): in the reproduction a server's log reader is paused while it is offline (EXPERIMENT-ONLY, LIM-01).
- **User ruling:** (a) a missing, altered or inserted record fails the affected batch (into ErrList) and the audit continues; a trailing partial batch is ignored. Fig. 14(b) tampering mutates bytes in selected records of completed batches, preserving record count and order.
- **Ruling classification:** IMPLEMENTATION-NECESSARY-BUT-UNSPECIFIED (user-decided)
- **Topic:** Alg. 3 with partial, deleted or inserted records.
- **Why a decision is needed:** Eq. (1) indexing assumes records 1..(li+1)*theta exist. Deletion or insertion shifts indices, and a trailing partial batch has no omega.
- **Paper says:** ErrList collects "batches in which audit log is deleted/forged/tampered" (Alg. 3); Fig. 14(b) varies the "rate of tampered audit logs".
- **Paper does NOT say:** behavior when an index does not exist; whether a trailing partial batch is ignored; the tampering method used in Fig. 14(b).
- **Classification:** IMPL.
- **Possible choices:** (a) missing index -> that batch flagged, the rest proceed by index; (b) abort with error; trailing partial batch ignored in both.
- **Effect on faithfulness / benchmark:** Affects TEST-13 and the Fig. 14(b) reproduction.
- **User decision required:** YES

### GAP-17
- **Status:** DECIDED
- **Revision 3:** GAP-77 (a): the auditor authenticates with a mutual-TLS client certificate.
- **User ruling:** periodic audits ("in cycles") with the period as a config value. On-demand is a test/CLI trigger only. Auditor authentication is required; mechanism unspecified.
- **Ruling classification:** IMPLEMENTATION-NECESSARY-BUT-UNSPECIFIED (user-decided)
- **Documented deviation:** DEV-09. §3.4 stage 2 (p. 3290): "At any time, an external auditor could access the audit phase".
- **Residual (closed in revision 3):** see GAP-77.
- **Topic:** Audit schedule and auditor access control.
- **Why a decision is needed:** The system model says audits run "automatically in cycles" (§2.1); §3.4 says "at any time" (INC-28). Retrieving logs from pi requires an authenticated request.
- **Paper says:** as above; Fig. 1 labels "Request for Audit Logs".
- **Paper does NOT say:** the cycle period; the request protocol; authentication of the auditor.
- **Classification:** IMPL.
- **Possible choices:** (a) on-demand only; (b) configurable period plus on-demand.
- **Effect on faithfulness / benchmark:** None on protocol semantics.
- **User decision required:** YES

### GAP-18
- **Status:** DECIDED
- **User ruling:** (b) no separate collector, preprocessor or verifier components. The log reader feeds LSG; verification is Alg. 2/3 code.
- **Ruling classification:** IMPLEMENTATION-NECESSARY-BUT-UNSPECIFIED (user-decided)
- **Documented deviation:** DEV-08. §3.4 stages 1 and 3 (p. 3290) name these modules as deployed and closed.
- **Topic:** Log collector, log preprocessor and integrity verification module.
- **Why a decision is needed:** These are named as deployed at audit initialization and closed at audit closure, but never defined.
- **Paper says:** §3.4 stages 1 and 3. §6.1 uses "a log reader program" for simulation.
- **Paper does NOT say:** their inputs, outputs or behavior.
- **Classification:** IMPL.
- **Possible choices:** (a) collector = log reader feeding LSG; preprocessor = record delimiting (GAP-03); verifier = the Alg. 2 invocation; (b) omit them as separate components.
- **Effect on faithfulness / benchmark:** Low. The reader rate affects Fig. 11(a).
- **User decision required:** YES

### GAP-19
- **Status:** DECIDED
- **User ruling:** manual topology configuration; no automatic partitioning or federation algorithm.
- **Ruling classification:** IMPLEMENTATION-NECESSARY-BUT-UNSPECIFIED (user-decided)
- **Topic:** Partitioning servers into instances and building the hierarchy.
- **Why a decision is needed:** Needed to instantiate any multi-instance deployment and to run Fig. 11(b) and Fig. 13(b).
- **Paper says:** instances are initialized "according to the network division" (§3.4 stage 1); federation happens when "different participants need to cooperate" (§4.2); examples: Fig. 7 (two instances of 3), Fig. 13(b) ("three state channel instances including 15 servers").
- **Paper does NOT say:** the partitioning rule, fanout, depth or federation trigger.
- **Classification:** IMPL.
- **Possible choices:** a manual topology file supplied per experiment (no algorithm); nothing automatic.
- **Effect on faithfulness / benchmark:** The topology directly affects Fig. 11(b), 12 and 13 results.
- **User decision required:** YES

### GAP-20
- **Status:** OPEN (core not answered)
- **User ruling (partial; answered under a "GAP-19" label):** the initial G of a new instance = G_0 / the current domain state at creation. There is no join timeout in the protocol; any timeout is test-harness-only.
- **Ruling classification (partial):** IMPLEMENTATION-NECESSARY-BUT-UNSPECIFIED (user-decided)
- **Still OPEN:** option (a) vs (b) for InitMsg aggregation, and sid derivation for ordinary instances.
- **Topic:** InitMsg aggregation, sid generation and initial G for an ordinary instance.
- **Why a decision is needed:** Each participant sends its own InitMsg (INC-21). The contract must decide when those messages constitute one instance.
- **Paper says:** InitMsg composed of P; Γ created; sid "unique identity"; OffList, NewLeader, Parent and ChildList null (§4.3.1); "each pi should send InitMsg containing all other participants' public keys" (Thm. 4 proof); "every server ... has to submit its request for joining" (§6.2.2).
- **Paper does NOT say:**
  - the matching rule (identical P from all members?)
  - whether the instance exists after the first InitMsg or after all n
  - sid derivation
  - initial G
  - timeout for incomplete joins
- **Classification:** IMPL.
- **Possible choices:** (a) the first InitMsg creates a pending instance; it activates when every PK in P has submitted an identical P; sid = contract counter or hash of P. (b) The first InitMsg creates the instance and later InitMsgs are joins.
- **Effect on faithfulness / benchmark:** Affects Init gas (Fig. 13(a)).
- **User decision required:** YES

### GAP-21
- **Status:** DECIDED (residual: GAP-82)
- **Revision 3:** GAP-60: the current child leader is derived with the deterministic leader rule. GAP-62: sid_a is a 256-bit pseudo-random unique opaque identifier generated by SCC at activation (for example a hash of the previous block hash, a federation counter and the ordered child sids); this replaces "not hash-derived". GAP-80 (a): OffList_a empty, NewLeader_a null, Parent_a null, ChildList_a = the child sids, G_a = the current accepted domain GlobalState; each child's Parent = sid_a on activation.
- **User ruling:**
  - (b) one submission per child leader; federation activates when all listed child leaders have submitted.
  - SCC verifies each sender against that child's current leader.
  - A parent can later be federated.
  - A child has at most one parent (hierarchy, not DAG).
  - The parent sid is a unique RANDOM value, not hash-derived.
- **Ruling classification:** IMPLEMENTATION-NECESSARY-BUT-UNSPECIFIED (user-decided)
- **Additional conflict noted:** resolved in revision 3 by GAP-60.
- **Residual:** GAP-80 was decided in revision 3. Still open (GAP-82): what "current leader" means at federation time, and which accepted state G_a takes when the children's stored states differ.
- **Topic:** Federation details.
- **Why a decision is needed:** The contract must validate FederateMsg and wire up the hierarchy.
- **Paper says:** "their leaders should send a message FederateMsg composed of (sid_1..sid_m, P_1..P_m) to SCC"; sid_a "randomly generated"; P_a = participants' keys in the child channels; Parent_a null; ChildList_a includes sid_1..sid_m; "could be executed in initialization or subsequent processes" (§4.3.1).
- **Paper does NOT say:**
  - whether one transaction or one per leader
  - how SCC checks that senders are the current leaders
  - whether children's Parent is set to sid_a
  - initial G, OffList_a and NewLeader_a
  - the on-chain randomness source (Solidity has no native RNG)
  - whether a parent can itself be federated later
  - whether a child can have two parents
- **Classification:** IMPL.
- **Possible choices:**
  - (a) all child leaders co-sign a single FederateMsg; SCC sets each child's Parent = sid_a; sid_a = hash of (block hash, sids)
  - (b) one transaction per leader, activating when all have arrived
- **Effect on faithfulness / benchmark:** Setting the children's Parent field is strongly implied by the field definition but not stated. Randomness from the block hash mirrors §4.3.4's permitted source only by analogy.
- **User decision required:** YES

### GAP-22
- **Status:** DECIDED
- **Revision 3:** GAP-79 (a): "offline" = listed in the finalized on-chain OffList only; local suspicion and pending OfflineMsgs do not affect eligibility. GAP-60: there is no round counter; the next leader is the next eligible key after the leader recorded in the latest accepted signed-round state, and only a new signed-round anchor advances rotation.
- **User ruling:**
  - Keys are compared as fixed-width unsigned big-endian values.
  - Participants in OffList are skipped.
  - The election is a deterministic computation by every participant from P, the current round and NewLeader; no election service.
  - NewLeader overrides rotation during recovery; afterwards rotation continues after that leader.
  - If the elected leader is offline, advance to the next non-offline key.
- **Ruling classification:** IMPLEMENTATION-NECESSARY-BUT-UNSPECIFIED (user-decided)
- **Documented deviation:** DEV-11. §4.3.2 stage 1 (pp. 3291-3292) rotates over all of P with no skipping.
- **Additional conflict noted:** resolved in revision 3 by GAP-79.
- **Topic:** Leader election mechanics.
- **Why a decision is needed:** "Minimum value of PK_pi" requires an ordering over keys, and the election's executor and edge cases determine liveness.
- **Paper says:** "The system chooses the participant with the minimum value of PK_pi in P as the leader. This leader will not join the election of next round, and the next leader is the participant with the next smallest PK_pi in P. If no such participant exists, the system starts over from the minimum value in P" (§4.3.2).
- **Paper does NOT say:**
  - key encoding for comparison (compressed or uncompressed point bytes, address, big integer)
  - whether OffList or excluded participants are skipped
  - whether "the system" means an off-chain deterministic computation or the contract
  - how rotation continues after a NewLeader is installed by dispute
  - what happens if the elected leader is offline
- **Classification:** IMPL.
- **Possible choices:**
  - Ordering: lexicographic order of the encoded PK bytes, or numeric order of the address.
  - Offline participants: skipped, or not skipped.
  - Executor: deterministic off-chain computation from the on-chain P.
- **Effect on faithfulness / benchmark:** Affects which node leads, not complexity.
- **User decision required:** YES

### GAP-23
- **Status:** DECIDED
- **Revision 3:** GAP-68 (c) keeps this ruling; the parent leader's signature is counted separately when the parent leader is not a child leader (DEV-18).
- **User ruling:** (a) the parent's candidate set is all of P_a.
- **Ruling classification:** IMPLEMENTATION-NECESSARY-BUT-UNSPECIFIED (user-decided)
- **Additional conflict noted:** resolved in revision 3 by GAP-68. The role-binding conflict that GAP-68 creates is GAP-87.
- **Topic:** Parent-leader vs child-leader relationship.
- **Why a decision is needed:** Under the min-PK rule a parent's P (all child participants) and a child's P can elect the same key. Fig. 8 shows the root leader p1 not leading its own child Γ1 (INC-19). Signed states flow through "leaders of child state channel instances".
- **Paper says:** "the leader selected by each state channel is used to build a hierarchical state channel" (§4.2); the Fig. 8 roles.
- **Paper does NOT say:** whether the parent election runs over P_a or over the set of child leaders; whether a node may lead a parent and a child at once.
- **Classification:** IMPL.
- **Possible choices:** (a) elect over P_a (literal rule; roles may coincide); (b) elect over the current child leaders.
- **Effect on faithfulness / benchmark:** Affects Fig. 11(b) and the hierarchy's message paths.
- **User decision required:** YES

### GAP-24
- **Status:** OPEN (core not answered)
- **User ruling (partial):** any participant may submit a valid signed-round state, not only the leader (answers the "which instances submit Update" sub-point).
- **Ruling classification (partial):** IMPLEMENTATION-NECESSARY-BUT-UNSPECIFIED (user-decided)
- **Still OPEN:** (a) forward unchanged vs (b) recursive re-signing at each parent; what the root distributes.
- **Topic:** Upward propagation of a signed round state through parents.
- **Why a decision is needed:** Step 3 says the leader "sends G_nu^{G2} to its parent leader, and this step stops until the G_nu^{G2} reaches root". A parent's signed state must carry child-leader signatures (§4.3.4, INC-16).
- **Paper says:** Steps 1-4 (§4.3.2, Fig. 8); "signed round states generated by parent channel Γ3 (G_2^{G3} and G_5^{G3}) do not include signatures of non-leader participants" (§4.3.4).
- **Paper does NOT say:** whether the parent leader re-runs Step 2 over its child leaders to form G_nu^{Gparent}; whether the root distributes G_nu^{G2} or G_nu^{G3}; which instance(s) submit Update.
- **Classification:** IMPL.
- **Possible choices:**
  - (a) each parent leader forwards the child state unchanged; the root distributes it
  - (b) each parent leader runs Steps 2-3 over its child leaders, producing G_nu^{Gparent}, recursively to the root; the root distributes both
- **Effect on faithfulness / benchmark:** (b) multiplies signature rounds by depth (Fig. 11(b)).
- **User decision required:** YES

### GAP-26
- **Status:** DECIDED (corrected in revision 3)
- **Revision 3 correction (user):** exactly the root leader signs a no-signed round state. Flow:
  1. the originating participant/channel generates the proposal
  2. intermediate leaders relay it upward
  3. the root leader approves it
  4. the root leader signs the no-signed GlobalState
  5. the root leader distributes it downward through descendant leaders
- This replaces the "originating instance leader signs" bullet below. DEV-05 is withdrawn. GAP-61, GAP-66 and GAP-70 are decided.
- **User ruling:**
  - A round begins at signed anchor serial nu; up to beta sequential no-signed states nu+1..nu+beta may follow.
  - beta is the originating instance's.
  - (Superseded in revision 3.) Exactly one leader signature: the originating instance leader signs; intermediate leaders relay; the root leader approves and redistributes without adding a signature unless it originated the state.
  - Recipients verify that signature and serial continuity.
  - A rejected state is not adopted; the local latest state is unchanged; resync if needed.
  - The round ends after beta accepted no-signed states; the next anchor must be a signed-round state.
- **Ruling classification:** IMPLEMENTATION-NECESSARY-BUT-UNSPECIFIED (user-decided)
- **Documented deviation:** DEV-05, withdrawn in revision 3. Revision 2's ruling departed from two places:
  - §4.3.2 stage 3 (p. 3292): "p1 signs G_nu' with its private key SK_p1 and broadcasts (G_nu', S_p1)", where p1 is the root leader in the Fig. 8 example.
  - Thm. 3 proof (p. 3295): "only the leader of root state channel instance p_rld will sign it".
- **Label correction:** the user cited INC-18; the signer inconsistency is INC-17. INC-18 is the undefined nu, which the ruling also resolves (nu = anchor serial).
- **Topic:** No-signed round state details.
- **Why a decision is needed:** The approval condition, the signer, receiver verification and round boundaries are all under-defined (INC-17, INC-18).
- **Paper says:** the leader "can still conduct beta no-signed round states in the rest of this round"; "Every leader in HMSC receiving a no-signed round state sends it to the leader of root state channel instance recursively"; "If nu* < nu + beta and nu' is equal to nu*+1, p1 signs G_nu' with its private key SK_p1 and broadcasts (G_nu', S_p1)"; receivers check "serial number of their local latest state is equal to nu' - 1" (§4.3.2); Thm. 3: "only the leader of root state channel instance p_rld will sign it".
- **Paper does NOT say:**
  - what nu is (probably the serial of the round's signed state)
  - which instance's beta applies at the root
  - whether the instance leader also signs
  - whether receivers verify S_root
  - what happens to a rejected no-signed state
  - how a round ends and the next election is triggered (after beta no-signed states? on a timer?)
- **Classification:** IMPL.
- **Possible choices:**
  - nu = serial of the current round's signed state.
  - beta = the root instance's beta, or the originating instance's beta.
  - Receivers verify S_root, or do not.
  - Round ends after beta approved no-signed states.
- **Effect on faithfulness / benchmark:** Determines the Fig. 12(a) behavior.
- **User decision required:** YES

### GAP-27
- **Status:** DECIDED (residual: GAP-86)
- **Revision 3:** GAP-72 (a): the paper's suffixes are bound as purpose \|\| signer_role \|\| encoded state (purposes STATE, CLOSE, RECOVERY, VOTE); sid is not included. GAP-73: both prefixes are fixed one-byte enumerations; their byte values are delegated to the normative test-vector file.
- **User ruling:**
  - (b) secp256k1 ECDSA over a SHA-256 digest; an implementation decision, not paper-mandated.
  - Fixed-size hashes/keys use raw byte concatenation. Structured variable-length objects get one deterministic binary encoding first. No ambiguous variable-string concatenation.
  - "Signature contains leader PK" = one valid signature must verify under the current leader's public key.
- **Ruling classification:** IMPLEMENTATION-NECESSARY-BUT-UNSPECIFIED (user-decided). ECDSA itself remains PAPER-PERMITS (§4.3.1).
- **Additional conflict noted:** resolved in revision 3 by GAP-72.
- **Topic:** Signature scheme, curve and signed-message serialization (absorbs former GAP-25, the `|` operator).
- **Why a decision is needed:** Needed for bit-exact signing and verification on- and off-chain, including in Solidity.
- **Paper says:** "(Gen, Sign, Vrfy) be a digital signature scheme (e.g., ECDSA)" (§4.3.1); signed objects written (G_nu \| p4), (G_nu \| Close), Sign(G_{nu+1} \| NewLeader_x); Rule 3 "Vrfy(PK_pi, S_pi)" with no message argument; "there must be one signature which contains the leader's public key".
- **Paper does NOT say:**
  - which scheme or curve the prototype used
  - the meaning of `|` (concatenation? tagging?)
  - hash-then-sign convention
  - signature encoding
  - what "contains the public key" means
- **Classification:** IMPL (scheme example is PP).
- **Possible choices:**
  - (a) ECDSA secp256k1 with Keccak-256 message digest, verifiable in Solidity via `ecrecover`. This introduces a second hash function in signing only.
  - (b) ECDSA secp256k1 over a SHA-256 digest, also verifiable via `ecrecover`, keeping SHA-256 consistent.
  - (c) ECDSA P-256 (no native EVM precompile on 2018-era Ethereum).
  - Separately: define `(X | Y)` as byte concatenation of the encodings.
- **Effect on faithfulness / benchmark:** Any choice beyond "ECDSA" is interpretation. The curve and verification method dominate the gas figures (Fig. 13(a)).
- **User decision required:** YES

### GAP-28
- **Status:** DECIDED
- **User ruling:** (a) one secp256k1 key per participant; the Ethereum address is derived from it; no second identity mapping.
- **Ruling classification:** IMPLEMENTATION-NECESSARY-BUT-UNSPECIFIED (user-decided)
- **Topic:** Public key vs Ethereum address as participant identity.
- **Why a decision is needed:** P holds signature public keys, but OffList counts "addresses", NewLeader stores "its address", and OnlineMsg "removes the address" (INC-22).
- **Paper says:** as above (§4.3.1, §4.3.3-§4.3.5).
- **Paper does NOT say:** whether the signature key pair is the participant's Ethereum account key, or whether PKs and addresses are separate identities with a binding.
- **Classification:** IMPL.
- **Possible choices:** (a) one secp256k1 key per participant serving as both signing key and Ethereum account (address derived from PK); (b) separate keys with a registered PK-address mapping.
- **Effect on faithfulness / benchmark:** (a) constrains GAP-27 to secp256k1.
- **User decision required:** YES

### GAP-29
- **Status:** DECIDED
- **Revision 3:** GAP-66: on-chain nu* = the serial of the latest signed state stored on-chain in the root instance (DEV-20).
- **User ruling:** (a) nu* = serial of the latest state conducted by the root channel leader.
- **Ruling classification:** IMPLEMENTATION-NECESSARY-BUT-UNSPECIFIED (user-decided)
- **Topic:** Meaning of nu* in Rules 1 and 2 on-chain.
- **Why a decision is needed:** nu* is "the serial number of the latest state conducted by the root channel's leader". The contract only sees submitted signed states, not the root leader's off-chain no-signed states.
- **Paper says:** Rule 1 and Rule 2 (§4.3.3); Thm. 2 proof: replay of an earlier state "will be prevented by our state update protocol's first rule".
- **Paper does NOT say:** what on-chain value nu* is; whether nu* is per instance or hierarchy-wide.
- **Classification:** IMPL.
- **Possible choices:** (a) nu* = serial of the latest state stored in the root instance's G; (b) nu* = serial of Γx's own stored G; (c) the maximum across the hierarchy.
- **Effect on faithfulness / benchmark:** Determines whether child-instance updates can be rejected by root progress. Interacts with Rule 4 and with dispute (GAP-33).
- **User decision required:** YES

### GAP-30
- **Status:** DECIDED - CONFLICT PENDING (GAP-87)
- **Revision 3:** GAP-60 replaces the round/leader index: SCC derives the expected leader from the leader recorded in the latest accepted signed-round state (first signed round: minimum eligible key; NewLeader overrides). GAP-68 (c): a parent needs num - numoff child-leader signatures, plus the parent leader's counted separately when it is not a child leader; when it is, one signature satisfies both (DEV-18). GAP-76: Close requires all of P. Open: GAP-87 (one signature filling two bound roles).
- **User ruling:**
  - Exactly num - numoff distinct valid signers from the expected online signer set.
  - For a parent signed-round state, numoff counts offline members of the parent's effective signing set (the child leaders expected to sign).
  - SCC maintains a minimal round/leader index to derive the expected leader from P; NewLeader overrides it during recovery; a message-supplied leader field is not trusted.
- **Ruling classification:** IMPLEMENTATION-NECESSARY-BUT-UNSPECIFIED (user-decided). The round/leader index is user-approved contract state outside the Γ tuple (ARCH-011).
- **Topic:** Rule 3 counting and leader identification.
- **Why a decision is needed:** "There should be num - numoff signatures" is ambiguous between "exactly" and "at least". numoff counts server addresses while num may count child instances (INC-15). The contract must know the current leader's key.
- **Paper says:** Rule 3 (§4.3.3); leader election is off-chain by "the system" (§4.3.2).
- **Paper does NOT say:** exact vs minimum count; how numoff applies to a parent instance; how the leader is identified on-chain (recomputed from round number? supplied in the submission?).
- **Classification:** IMPL.
- **Possible choices:**
  - Count: exactly or at least.
  - Parent instance: numoff = offline child leaders only, or the parent's OffList size.
  - Leader: derived on-chain from the rotation rule and a round counter (adds contract state), or declared in the submission and checked for membership in P.
- **Effect on faithfulness / benchmark:** Affects security (a declared leader is weaker) and gas.
- **User decision required:** YES

### GAP-31
- **Status:** DECIDED
- **User ruling:** (b) accept the valid parent update, and null a DIRECT child's stored G when the child serial > the accepted parent serial. No recursion to grandchildren.
- **Ruling classification:** IMPLEMENTATION-NECESSARY-BUT-UNSPECIFIED (user-decided)
- **Documented deviation:** DEV-06. §4.3.3 Rule 4 (p. 3292) is stated as an acceptance condition; the ruling never rejects on it.
- **Topic:** Rule 4 accept/reject vs nulling semantics.
- **Why a decision is needed:** The rule reads as a condition. The explanation says child states with a bigger serial "should be set null" (INC-14).
- **Paper says:** Rule 4 and its explanation (§4.3.3).
- **Paper does NOT say:**
  - whether a violation rejects the update
  - whether the update is accepted and child states nulled
  - which child "state stored" field is meant (child Γ.G?)
  - whether nulling is recursive to grandchildren
- **Classification:** IMPL.
- **Possible choices:** (a) reject if the child minimum is >= nu; (b) accept and null child G where serial > nu; (c) both: condition on the minimum, then null the larger ones.
- **Effect on faithfulness / benchmark:** Changes the observable on-chain state and TEST-28.
- **User decision required:** YES

### GAP-32
- **Status:** DECIDED - CONFLICT PENDING (GAP-81)
- **Revision 3:** GAP-60 and GAP-61 assume every signed anchor reaches the chain, and GAP-70 requires the recovery state to be submitted immediately. Periodic submission may skip anchors; see GAP-81.
- **User ruling:** (b) a participant submits signed-round states periodically; the period is config, not a DELIA constant; a rejected transaction has no state effect.
- **Ruling classification:** IMPLEMENTATION-NECESSARY-BUT-UNSPECIFIED (user-decided)
- **Topic:** Update submission timing and submitter.
- **Why a decision is needed:** "we could periodically update the state information" (§3.3 stage 4); "A participant could submit a signed round state" (§4.3.3).
- **Paper says:** as above.
- **Paper does NOT say:** the period (every signed round state? every k rounds?); which participant submits; how rejections are signalled (revert vs event).
- **Classification:** IMPL.
- **Possible choices:** (a) the leader submits each signed round state after Step 4; (b) a configurable interval; rejection via transaction revert.
- **Effect on faithfulness / benchmark:** Drives gas totals and Fig. 13(b) storage growth.
- **User decision required:** YES

### GAP-33
- **Status:** DECIDED (revised in revision 3; residual: GAP-88)
- **Revision 3:** GAP-64 and GAP-71 (c) replace "Rules 2-4 where meaningful" and the on-chain Alg. 4 serial check. SCC verifies only the checkpoint state's own signatures plus the dispute vote evidence; current P/OffList are not applied (DEV-04 rewritten).
- **User ruling:** (a) for DisputeMsg, skip Rule 1's newer-than-latest test for the checkpoint. Validate signatures and Rules 2-4 "where meaningful", and require that the serial equals the Alg. 4 (prose semantics) checkpoint.
- **Ruling classification:** IMPLEMENTATION-NECESSARY-BUT-UNSPECIFIED (user-decided)
- **Documented deviation:** DEV-04. §4.3.4 (p. 3293): the request "is considered valid, if State Update Rules are satisfied".
- **Additional conflict noted:** resolved in revision 3 by GAP-71.
- **Topic:** DisputeMsg validity vs Rule 1.
- **Why a decision is needed:** DisputeMsg carries the checkpoint's signed state and is "valid if State Update Rules are satisfied", but a checkpoint is older than the latest state, so Rule 1 (nu > nu*) fails by construction (INC-08).
- **Paper says:** §4.3.4, p. 3293.
- **Paper does NOT say:** a dispute-specific variant of the rules.
- **Classification:** IMPL.
- **Possible choices:** (a) apply Rules 2-4 and signature checks only, and check the checkpoint's serial against Alg. 4 instead of Rule 1; (b) interpret nu* in dispute context as the serial of the state preceding the checkpoint.
- **Effect on faithfulness / benchmark:** Either choice modifies a rule as written. Without one, disputes can never succeed.
- **User decision required:** YES

### GAP-34
- **Status:** DECIDED
- **Revision 3:** "exists" is searched in the retained signed-state history (GAP-61, GAP-64) rather than in event logs only.
- **User ruling:** (b) implement the prose + Fig. 9 semantics, not the defective listing; documented as a necessary correction. "Exists" searches the signed-state history from ledger events (GAP-15).
- **Ruling classification:** IMPLEMENTATION-NECESSARY-BUT-UNSPECIFIED (user-decided)
- **Documented deviation:** DEV-02. Alg. 4 lines 5-8, 11-16, 19-23 (p. 3293).
- **Topic:** Alg. 4 literal defects and the "exists" oracle.
- **Why a decision is needed:** Several issues (INC-07):
  - loops decrement before testing, so G_nu is never examined
  - counters reach -1
  - no return when nothing is found in the null-ChildList branch
  - lines 19-23 have no break, so k2 becomes the oldest signed serial of any child rather than the "earliest of the children's latest" described in the prose
  - "G_k^{Gx} exists" needs a store of which serials were signed in which instance
- **Paper says:** Alg. 4 listing; prose cases 1 and 2 and Fig. 9 (p. 3293).
- **Paper does NOT say:** a corrected listing, or the data source for "exists".
- **Classification:** IMPL.
- **Possible choices:**
  - (a) implement the listing literally, document the divergent outputs, and test both
  - (b) implement the prose semantics: break at the first found state in each child loop, and examine G_nu first if the disputed state is excluded
  - Oracle options: the contract's history (GAP-15), or local stores
- **Effect on faithfulness / benchmark:** The brief forbids "improving" Alg. 4. (a) is literal but contradicts the paper's own worked examples (Fig. 9(d)). The user must choose.
- **User decision required:** YES

### GAP-35
- **Status:** DECIDED
- **Revision 3:** GAP-64: SCC checks the submitted checkpoint's signatures plus the vote evidence; the majority-certified result determines the checkpoint; no historical state arrays are added to the contract.
- **User ruling:** (a) participants run checkpoint selection off-chain; SCC verifies the submitted signed checkpoint and vote outcome; the contract does not rescan history.
- **Ruling classification:** IMPLEMENTATION-NECESSARY-BUT-UNSPECIFIED (user-decided)
- **Topic:** Executor of Alg. 4 and on-chain verification of the checkpoint.
- **Why a decision is needed:** The initiator submits the checkpoint state. It is unclear whether SCC recomputes Alg. 4 to confirm it.
- **Paper says:** "a participant submits a message DisputeMsg composed of signed round state in the checkpoint"; "Ethereum need to select a new leader in this protocol" (§6.2.2).
- **Paper does NOT say:** whether SCC runs Alg. 4.
- **Classification:** IMPL.
- **Possible choices:** (a) off-chain Alg. 4, on-chain rule checks only; (b) both, with SCC re-running Alg. 4 over its stored history.
- **Effect on faithfulness / benchmark:** (b) raises dispute gas (Fig. 13(a) shows Dispute above Update) and requires on-chain history.
- **User decision required:** YES

### GAP-36
- **Status:** DECIDED (residual: GAP-88)
- **Revision 3:** GAP-65: the loser's slot is frozen at the rollback checkpoint, not deleted or re-indexed, and no later LocalState update for it is accepted unless membership is explicitly re-established (LIM-05). GAP-64: the vote certifies the checkpoint.
- **User ruling:**
  - (a) signed votes are exchanged off-chain; a strict majority (>50%) decides; the result and evidence are submitted with the dispute transaction.
  - All current members of P vote, including the leader and the initiator.
  - No protocol deadline; resolve as soon as a strict majority is reached; a harness timeout is allowed.
  - "Excluded" = the losing party is removed from active P/membership, NOT put in OffList.
- **Ruling classification:** IMPLEMENTATION-NECESSARY-BUT-UNSPECIFIED (user-decided)
- **Topic:** Voting mechanism.
- **Why a decision is needed:** Needed to decide the leader-vs-initiator outcome.
- **Paper says:** "Any participant in the channel instance can generate a signed voting message for the leader or the participant that initiates the dispute by sending DisputeMsg. According to the vote, one participant between leader and the dispute initiator will be excluded" (§4.3.4); honest majority abs(H) > n/2 is required (Thm. 3); "HMSC just invokes the voting mechanism among the participants in the state channel while MSC needs to contact with the distributed ledger" (§6.2.2); "Each server in the same state channel broadcasts it's DisputeMsgs, collects and responses DisputeMsgs, individually" (§6.2.4); Table 3 marks DisputeResolving as ledger-interacting.
- **Paper does NOT say:**
  - vote message format
  - voter eligibility (instance P? excluding the two parties?)
  - threshold (simple majority is implied by Thm. 3 but not stated as the rule)
  - tally location (off-chain per §6.2.2 and §6.2.4 vs on-chain per Table 3)
  - deadline
  - the meaning of "excluded": removed from P, added to OffList, or barred from leading
  - how the outcome reaches SCC before NewLeader is set
- **Classification:** IMPL.
- **Possible choices:**
  - (a) off-chain broadcast votes; strict majority of the instance's P; the tally result plus the signed votes submitted with DisputeMsg; the excluded party removed from P
  - (b) on-chain vote transactions
- **Effect on faithfulness / benchmark:** Directly determines the Fig. 12(b) and Fig. 15 reproduction.
- **User decision required:** YES

### GAP-37
- **Status:** DECIDED
- **Revision 3:** GAP-79: "currently offline" = listed in the finalized on-chain OffList.
- **User ruling:** hash of the previous/latest block mod candidate count. Candidates exclude the removed participant and currently offline participants.
- **Ruling classification:** IMPLEMENTATION-NECESSARY-BUT-UNSPECIFIED (user-decided). The block-hash source itself is PAPER-PERMITS (§4.3.4).
- **Note (not a choice):** in the EVM the current block's hash is unavailable (`blockhash(block.number)` returns 0), so "previous/latest" can only be block.number - 1.
- **Additional conflict noted:** resolved in revision 3 by GAP-79.
- **Topic:** Random new-leader selection.
- **Why a decision is needed:** A deterministic mapping is needed for on-chain execution.
- **Paper says:** "a new leader is selected randomly in this state channel instance, which could be decided by the hash of latest block, and its address is stored into NewLeader_x" (§4.3.4).
- **Paper does NOT say:**
  - which block ("latest" relative to which transaction; note that in the EVM `blockhash(block.number)` is zero, so the previous block must be used)
  - the mapping (hash mod abs(P)?)
  - whether the malicious leader or offline members are excluded as candidates
- **Classification:** PP (source) + IMPL (mapping).
- **Possible choices:** index = uint(blockhash(block.number - 1)) mod abs(candidates), where candidates = P minus the excluded leader, ordered as in GAP-22.
- **Effect on faithfulness / benchmark:** Low cost impact. Miner manipulability is inherent in the paper's own choice.
- **User decision required:** YES

### GAP-38
- **Status:** DECIDED
- **Revision 3:** GAP-70 (a): the first recovery state after rollback is a signed-round state submitted on-chain immediately; SCC checks serial = checkpoint + 1 and the NewLeader signature, then clears NewLeader.
- **User ruling:**
  - All instances in the HMSC roll back to the checkpoint.
  - Off-chain: discard temporary/no-signed states after the checkpoint.
  - Resolved when the first valid state at checkpoint+1 signed by NewLeader is accepted; then NewLeader is cleared.
- **Ruling classification:** IMPLEMENTATION-NECESSARY-BUT-UNSPECIFIED (user-decided)
- **Additional conflict noted:** resolved in revision 3 by GAP-70.
- **Topic:** Rollback mechanics, state-loss notation and "dispute resolved".
- **Why a decision is needed:** "all state channel instances in HMSC roll back to this checkpoint" must change concrete on-chain and off-chain data. "After dispute resolved, NewLeader_x will be set null" needs a trigger (INC-09).
- **Paper says:** §4.3.4, pp. 3293-3294.
- **Paper does NOT say:**
  - whether every instance's G is reset on-chain (one transaction over the whole hierarchy?)
  - what participants do with off-chain cached states after the checkpoint
  - consistent definitions of nu and nu* in "the next state ... must be G_{nu+1}" and "we lose nu - nu* states"
  - the event that marks resolution (first accepted G_{nu+1}?)
- **Classification:** IMPL.
- **Possible choices:**
  - On-chain: only Γx.G reset, or every instance's G reset.
  - Off-chain: all states after the checkpoint discarded.
  - Resolution: NewLeader_x nulled when G_{nu+1} signed by NewLeader_x is accepted.
- **Effect on faithfulness / benchmark:** Affects dispute gas and state-loss measurements.
- **User decision required:** YES

### GAP-39
- **Status:** DECIDED
- **User ruling:** (a) detect/reject malicious non-leader submissions; no separate exclusion protocol.
- **Ruling classification:** IMPLEMENTATION-NECESSARY-BUT-UNSPECIFIED (user-decided)

- **Topic:** Exclusion of malicious non-leader participants.

- **Paper does NOT say:** detection criteria, the exclusion procedure, or on-chain effects.

### GAP-40
- **Status:** DECIDED
- **User ruling:** (b) dispute initiation is an explicit/manual protocol action; no automatic thresholds.
- **Ruling classification:** IMPLEMENTATION-NECESSARY-BUT-UNSPECIFIED (user-decided)
- **Topic:** "Abnormal situation" that triggers a dispute.
- **Why a decision is needed:** Participants need concrete conditions for calling DisputeResolving.
- **Paper says:** "Once any participant finds abnormal situation, it can call the dispute resolving protocol" (§4.3.2); examples: a leader that "only process[es] the state generated by that participant while ignoring others' states" (§4.3.4); inconsistency "found in the next signed round state update" (Thm. 3).
- **Paper does NOT say:** detectable predicates, or timeouts for ignored states.
- **Classification:** IMPL.

- **User decision required:** YES

### GAP-41
- **Status:** DECIDED
- **User ruling:** (a) loss of contact triggers peer inquiry with a configurable implementation timeout; no heartbeats.
- **Ruling classification:** IMPLEMENTATION-NECESSARY-BUT-UNSPECIFIED (user-decided)
- **Topic:** Offline detection and inquiry mechanism.
- **Why a decision is needed:** Needed to decide when a server is "offline" and how peers confirm it.
- **Paper says:** "Once a server loses contact with other servers in this domain, this process is triggered. Other servers confirm this server offline through the inquiry mechanism" (§3.3 stage 5); "The detection of offline server has been mentioned in Section 3.3" (§4.3.5); in Thm. 5, the leader knows pi is offline when pi "cannot submit signatures in the state confirmation stage".
- **Paper does NOT say:** the loss-of-contact criterion; inquiry message and response; timeouts; how many inquiries.
- **Classification:** IMPL.
- **Possible choices:** (a) trigger = missing signature from pi when the leader collects signatures, with a configurable timeout; inquiry = direct request with a configurable timeout; (b) a heartbeat-based trigger (this invents heartbeats, which the brief forbids unless the user approves).
- **Effect on faithfulness / benchmark:** Timeouts are invented values and affect Fig. 13(a) "Offline" only indirectly.
- **User decision required:** YES

### GAP-42
- **Status:** DECIDED
- **User ruling:** for OfflineMsg only, the pending-offline server is excluded from the expected signer set, so n-1 signatures are valid. H(psi) = SHA-256 over the current active LogCacheQueue (including the partial batch) as raw 32-byte digests in record order.
- **Ruling classification:** IMPLEMENTATION-NECESSARY-BUT-UNSPECIFIED (user-decided)
- **Documented deviations:**
  - DEV-03: §4.3.3 Rule 3 (p. 3292) and §4.3.5 (p. 3294).
  - DEV-17: hashing a partial batch departs from Eq. (1)'s definition of psi_{pi,li} as exactly theta hashes (p. 3288).
- **Topic:** OfflineMsg vs Rule 3; H(psi) content.
- **Why a decision is needed:** OfflineMsg carries n-1 signatures (S_pi omitted), but Rule 3 requires num - numoff = n while pi is not yet in OffList (INC-10). The tag prints H(psi_{pi,p1}) (INC-11). Which psi is hashed, and how a set is serialized for hashing, are unstated.
- **Paper says:** §4.3.5, p. 3294.
- **Paper does NOT say:** how Rule 3 is satisfied; which batch's psi (the current partial batch? the last full batch?); psi serialization for H.
- **Classification:** IMPL.
- **Possible choices:**
  - Rule 3: count pi as pending-offline for OfflineMsg, or relax Rule 3 for OfflineMsg only.
  - H(psi): hash of the concatenated elements in xi order, for pi's current batch li.
- **Effect on faithfulness / benchmark:** Any choice modifies or interprets a stated rule.
- **User decision required:** YES

### GAP-43
- **Status:** DECIDED (residual: GAP-85)
- **Revision 3:** GAP-63: after the one-block interval, a permissionless FinalizeOffline transaction adds the server to OffList if no valid cancellation was received (DEV-19). The lazy alternative is dropped.
- **User ruling:**
  - Store the block height at OfflineMsg acceptance.
  - Cancellation is allowed during one block interval, then the offline marking is finalized.
  - A cancel must carry the allegedly offline participant's valid signature over the same G_nu referenced by the OfflineMsg.
  - Finalization: "any caller may trigger it after the window; alternatively lazily on the next SCC interaction" (two alternatives; see GAP-63).
- **Ruling classification:** IMPLEMENTATION-NECESSARY-BUT-UNSPECIFIED (user-decided)
- **Topic:** Challenge time, cancellation and finalization.
- **Why a decision is needed:** The contract must know when the window opens and closes and whether a cancel counts (INC-12). A contract cannot act at the end of a window without a transaction.
- **Paper says:** "it waits for one block generation time (block height could be obtained from the smart contract) to receive the request from the offline server. After the waiting time, if the participant is not actually offline, it could send its signed latest state (G_nu, S_pi) to state channel to request cancelling. In the waiting time, if the state channel instance does not receive any cancelling message, it adds PK_pi and H(psi_{pi,li}) to its own OffList. Otherwise, the offline request does not take effect" (§4.3.5).
- **Paper does NOT say:**
  - whether the window is [acceptance block, acceptance block + 1] inclusive or exclusive
  - who sends the finalizing transaction
  - whether S_pi is verified and against which G_nu
  - whether the cancel must be for the same G_nu
- **Classification:** IMPL.
- **Possible choices:** A cancel is accepted if mined while block.number <= start + 1. Finalization is a separate transaction by any participant when block.number > start + 1. The cancel's S_pi is verified over the OfflineMsg's G_nu.
- **Effect on faithfulness / benchmark:** Adds a finalization transaction (gas in Fig. 13(a) "Offline"). The window length is paper-mandated.
- **User decision required:** YES

### GAP-44
- **Status:** DECIDED
- **Revision 3:** GAP-69 (c): the reproduction pauses the server's log reader while it is offline (EXPERIMENT-ONLY, LIM-01). GAP-73: OnlineNotification = (sid, returningParticipant).
- **User ruling:**
  - OnlineMsg requires signatures from the currently online signer set; pi's own signature is not required while pi is in OffList; on acceptance pi is removed from OffList.
  - Sync: request missing states from the direct leader; verify serial continuity, signatures and Alg. 2 inputs.
  - No offline backlog-ingestion protocol: records generated but not broadcast while offline are outside the baseline.
  - pi's LocalState was frozen, and its next valid batch chains from that frozen LocalState.
- **Ruling classification:** IMPLEMENTATION-NECESSARY-BUT-UNSPECIFIED (user-decided)
- **Documented deviation:** DEV-12. The OnlineMsg tuple (S_p1..S_pn) in §4.3.5 (p. 3294); "from other servers in the domain" in §3.3 stage 5 (p. 3290).
- **Additional conflict noted:** resolved in revision 3 by GAP-69.
- **Topic:** Online rejoin details.
- **Why a decision is needed:** Needed to define OnlineMsg content, synchronization and LSG catch-up.
- **Paper says:**
  - "OnlineMsg composed of GlobalState and an online tag (G_nu, S_p1, ..., ..., S_pn, ONLINE: PK_pi)" (§4.3.5).
  - "After the offline server becomes online, this server needs to synchronize all the GlobalState data and LogCacheQueue data from other servers" (§3.3 stage 5).
  - The online notification goes to other servers.
- **Paper does NOT say:**
  - whether S_pi is among the signatures
  - Rule 3's count for OnlineMsg
  - how the notification is formatted
  - which server(s) supply sync data and how pi verifies it
  - how records pi generated while offline enter LSG (broadcast late? separate batch?)
  - how pi's LocalState catches up, given Rule 2 froze it
- **Classification:** IMPL.
- **Possible choices:** OnlineMsg signatures from all P including pi; sync from the leader, verified with Alg. 2 per received state; offline-period records broadcast in order after rejoin, before new records.
- **Effect on faithfulness / benchmark:** Multiple invented sub-behaviors.
- **User decision required:** YES

### GAP-45
- **Status:** DECIDED (residual: GAP-89)
- **Revision 3:** GAP-72: the Close identifier is the CLOSE purpose byte. GAP-76 (a): an ordinary/root Close needs signatures from all of P, offline members included (LIM-03); DisassociateMsg is not supported when the parent itself has a parent (LIM-04).
- **User ruling:**
  - Close: verify the Close state like a signed-round state, require the complete signer set, then mark the instance closed so all further states are rejected.
  - Disassociate: verify each child's signed close state, close the parent, reset each direct child's Parent = null, leave the children open.
  - Reject direct closure while Parent != null (disassociate first).
  - Closed instance data remains readable.
- **Ruling classification:** IMPLEMENTATION-NECESSARY-BUT-UNSPECIFIED (user-decided)
- **Additional conflicts noted:** resolved in revision 3 by GAP-72 and GAP-76.
- **Topic:** Close and Disassociate details.
- **Why a decision is needed:** The contract's closure checks and post-closure state need definitions (INC-23).
- **Paper says:** §4.3.6, p. 3294 (see CLOSE-001..007).
- **Paper does NOT say:**
  - encoding of the "Close" identifier
  - which checks SCC applies to CloseMsg (signature count per Rule 3? Rule 1?)
  - how "signatures from all participants of root" reconciles with child-leader signatures at parent level
  - whether children remain open after DisassociateMsg, and whether their Parent is reset
  - how to close a child that has a parent without disassociating
  - whether a closed instance's data remains readable (no archival is described)
- **Classification:** IMPL.
- **Possible choices:** CloseMsg checked with Rule 3 signature verification; a closed flag added; after disassociation, children's Parent is reset to null and they stay open; closing a child with a parent is rejected.
- **Effect on faithfulness / benchmark:** Affects Close gas (Fig. 13(a)).
- **User decision required:** YES

### GAP-46
- **Status:** DECIDED
- **User ruling:** request (fromSerial, toSerial) from the direct leader; receive states and signatures; verify signatures and serial continuity; store. No recursion to higher leaders.
- **Ruling classification:** IMPLEMENTATION-NECESSARY-BUT-UNSPECIFIED (user-decided)
- **Topic:** State synchronization with the leader (no-signed desync).
- **Why a decision is needed:** "they need to interact with their leaders to synchronize all the states to their local storage" (§4.3.2) has no protocol.
- **Paper says:** as above.
- **Paper does NOT say:** request and response format; how synced states are verified; whether sync is recursive via parent leaders.
- **Classification:** IMPL.
- **Possible choices:** request (from serial, to serial) to the direct leader; the leader returns the stored states with their signatures; the receiver verifies signatures and serial continuity.
- **Effect on faithfulness / benchmark:** Low.
- **User decision required:** YES

### GAP-47
- **Status:** DECIDED
- **Revision 3:** GAP-73 framing: uint32 frame_length (big-endian) \|\| uint8 message_type \|\| message-specific fields; message_type codes are delegated to the normative test-vector file.
- **User ruling:** TLS over TCP with deterministic length-prefixed binary messages via explicit encoding/binary framing (not gob); no REST, gRPC or queues.
- **Ruling classification:** IMPLEMENTATION-NECESSARY-BUT-UNSPECIFIED (user-decided)
- **Topic:** Off-chain transport and message serialization.
- **Why a decision is needed:** Every NET-003..NET-032 message needs a wire format and transport.
- **Paper says:** server communication is protected by TLS (§6.1; Thm. 2 proof). Nothing else.
- **Paper does NOT say:** protocol, framing, serialization, ordering, retry or timeouts.
- **Classification:** IMPL.
- **Possible choices:** TLS over TCP with length-prefixed binary frames using Go `encoding/binary` or `encoding/gob`; or TLS over TCP with newline-delimited JSON. No RPC framework unless the user chooses one.
- **Effect on faithfulness / benchmark:** Affects communication delay (Figs. 11(b), 12, 15). The Java prototype's format is unknown.
- **User decision required:** YES

### GAP-48

### GAP-50
- **Status:** DECIDED
- **Revision 3:** GAP-78 (a): 1 s blocks for every experiment, with no per-figure tuning, unless the paper later turns out to give an explicit block time for an experiment.
- **User ruling:**
  - A real private Ethereum network, not a Go simulation, with 10 ledger nodes.
  - Geth compatible with 1.8.1 and a period-compatible Solidity compiler. If old tooling can't run reliably, upgrade the tool version with contract semantics unchanged, labelled an environment substitution.
  - Fixed PoA/Clique, not attributed to DELIA.
  - Fixed block time, 1 s unless a figure requires otherwise; never tuned per result.
  - A Go Ethereum client library compatible with the node replaces web3js.
- **Ruling classification:** IMPLEMENTATION-NECESSARY-BUT-UNSPECIFIED (user-decided)
- **Documented deviation:** DEV-16 (conditional environment substitution only).
- **Additional conflict noted:** resolved in revision 3 by GAP-78.
- **Topic:** Ethereum stack for the reproduction.
- **Why a decision is needed:** The contract must be compiled, deployed and called from Go.
- **Paper says:** "employs Ethereum 1.8.1 [21] ... We use solidity to realize our HMSC contracts ... communicate with HMSC instance on Ethereum by web3js APIs" (§6.1); ledger of 10 nodes (§6.1).
- **Paper does NOT say:**
  - which Ethereum client "1.8.1" refers to (the version number matches go-ethereum 1.8.x, but the paper does not name the client)
  - Solidity compiler version
  - network type (private chain?), consensus (PoW/PoA), block time, gas limit
- **Classification:** IMPL.
- **Possible choices:**
  - Node: a private multi-node chain of an Ethereum client at the paper's version, or a current client.
  - Go client: any Go Ethereum JSON-RPC client library (not selected here).
  - Solidity compiler: a period-appropriate version (for example 0.4.x) or current.
- **Effect on faithfulness / benchmark:** Block time drives the offline challenge duration. Client, EVM version and gas schedule drive Fig. 13(a) gas numbers. Replacing the chain with a Go simulation is a deviation needing approval.
- **User decision required:** YES

- **Paper does NOT say:** engine, format, crash durability.

### GAP-54
- **Status:** DECIDED
- **User ruling:** one OS process per DELIA server with its own TLS endpoint and a sequential protocol handler; an in-process multi-node harness for tests only; no worker pools or parallel hashing.
- **Ruling classification:** IMPLEMENTATION-NECESSARY-BUT-UNSPECIFIED (user-decided)
- **Topic:** Go process and concurrency model.
- **Why a decision is needed:** Servers are independent nodes receiving messages asynchronously. Tests need a harness.
- **Paper says:** Nothing. §6.2.3 says the audit "cannot be performed in parallel".
- **Paper does NOT say:** process layout or intra-node concurrency.
- **Classification:** IMPL.
- **Possible choices:** one OS process per server with a single message-handling loop (a network reader goroutine feeding a sequential handler); an in-process multi-node harness for tests only.
- **Effect on faithfulness / benchmark:** Goroutines used only for I/O do not alter algorithms. Parallel handlers would.
- **User decision required:** YES

### GAP-56
- **Status:** DECIDED
- **Revision 3:** GAP-67 (beta = 5 is EXPERIMENT-ONLY) and GAP-78 (1 s blocks everywhere) confirm this ruling.
- **User ruling:**
  - Log mapping: deterministic round-robin distribution of the source stream across 15 servers, with a frozen mapping/seed (experiment convention).
  - Hierarchy: manual topology files; reproduce given topologies exactly; Fig. 13(b) uses 3 instances / 15 servers.
  - beta outside Fig. 12: if needed, beta = 5, labelled a reproduction setting.
  - Fig. 14(b) tampering: mutate content in selected completed batches, count/order unchanged, deterministic seed.
  - Chain: the same fixed PoA chain and 1 s blocks for all results.
  - Network: no injected latency unless reproducing a delay condition.
  - Storage: measure serialized retained DELIA state / on-disk bytes, excluding Go runtime and OS overhead.
  - Delay: wall-clock the exact reported stage, with the same boundaries across runs.
  - MSC baseline: if the Fig. 12 comparison is required, implement [23] from its own paper; no simplified MSC-like baseline.
  - Repetitions: 100 rounds where the paper says 100 (Fig. 12); any other repetitions are labelled our methodology.
- **Ruling classification:** IMPLEMENTATION-NECESSARY-BUT-UNSPECIFIED (user-decided); EXPERIMENT settings only, not protocol requirements.
- **Topic:** Unspecified experiment setup.
- **Why a decision is needed:** Needed to reproduce §6 figures.
- **Paper says:** Section 23 items.
- **Paper does NOT say:**
  - which log files of [22] and how they map to 15 servers
  - how 15.6 GB relates to per-server rates
  - hierarchy per experiment (other than Fig. 13(b))
  - beta for figures other than Fig. 12
  - tampering method and positions (Fig. 14(b))
  - Ethereum consensus, block time and network latency
  - how storage and delays were measured
  - the MSC [23] baseline implementation
  - number of repetitions and statistics
- **Classification:** IMPL.
- **Possible choices:** documented per-figure experiment configs chosen by the user.
- **Effect on faithfulness / benchmark:** Numbers will not be directly comparable to the paper without these.
- **User decision required:** YES
