### 25.1 Conflicts and residuals introduced by the rulings (revision 2)

GAP-60..67 are the user's CONFLICT-1..8, recorded verbatim in substance. GAP-68..80 were found while applying the revision 2 rulings. All were decided in revision 3; each ruling is recorded at the top of its entry, and the original analysis is kept below it for history.

### GAP-60 (CONFLICT-1)
- **Status:** DECIDED - CONFLICT PENDING (GAP-81) (residual: GAP-82)
- **User ruling (revision 3):** No separate global round counter. SCC determines the next expected leader from the leader recorded in the latest accepted signed-round state. For the first signed round, leader = the minimum eligible key in P; afterwards it is the next eligible key, wrapping at the end. NewLeader overrides this during dispute recovery. Only a new signed-round anchor advances leader rotation; no-signed states do not. The same rule serves GAP-21's current-child-leader check.
- **Ruling classification:** IMPLEMENTATION-NECESSARY-BUT-UNSPECIFIED (user-decided)
- **Analyst note:** SCC's rotation advances only for anchors it accepts. Under periodic submission (GAP-32) it can fall behind the off-chain rotation; see GAP-81. Edge cases are in GAP-82.
- **Topic:** SCC cannot determine which round, and so which expected leader, a submitted signed state belongs to.
- **Rulings in conflict:** GAP-30 (SCC derives the expected leader from an on-chain round index) × GAP-32 (periodic submission) × GAP-09 (signed payload = (nu, entries), no round number) × GAP-26 (rounds contain a variable 0..beta no-signed states). Also affects GAP-21: SCC must verify a FederateMsg sender is the child's current leader.
- **Why implementation requires a decision:** Rule 3's leader-signature check and federation sender checks cannot be evaluated on-chain.
- **What the paper explicitly says:** "there must be one signature which contains the leader's public key" (§4.3.3 Rule 3, p. 3292); leader rotation per round (§4.3.2 stage 1).
- **What the paper does NOT say:** how the ledger knows the current round or leader.
- **Classification:** IMPLEMENTATION-NECESSARY-BUT-UNSPECIFIED (conflict between user rulings)
- **Possible choices:**
  - (a) bind a round index or leader identity into the signed payload (amends GAP-09)
  - (b) submit every signed anchor on-chain as soon as it is produced, so SCC's index advances exactly once per round (amends GAP-32)
  - (c) fix round length at exactly beta no-signed states, so SCC can derive the round from the serial (amends GAP-26 "up to beta")
  - (d) accept any P member's signature as the leader signature (weakens GAP-30; a message-supplied leader field was ruled untrusted)
- **Effect on faithfulness / benchmark:** each option amends a ruling; (b) increases gas and ledger interactions (Fig. 13).
- **User decision required:** YES

### GAP-61 (CONFLICT-2)
- **Status:** DECIDED - CONFLICT PENDING (GAP-81) (residual: GAP-83, GAP-84)
- **User ruling (revision 3):** No batch-number field is added to GlobalState. Servers retain the complete sequential GlobalState history off-chain (GAP-13). The auditor obtains that history from a server, verifies no-signed states by their root-leader signatures and serial continuity, and cross-checks every signed-round anchor against the distributed ledger. li is derived by replay: whenever slot i changes, its completed-batch counter advances. This reconciles the paper's claim that the auditor gets all G's from the ledger (Alg. 3 takes G_1..G_n) with only signed states being on-chain. It revises the GAP-15 event-only ruling.
- **Ruling classification:** IMPLEMENTATION-NECESSARY-BUT-UNSPECIFIED (user-decided)
- **Documented deviation:** DEV-10 (rewritten).
- **Topic:** the auditor cannot derive li for each G_k from a sparse signed-anchor history.
- **Rulings in conflict:** GAP-08 (no li field; derive li from history) × GAP-15 (only signed anchors, from events) × GAP-26 (no-signed states off-chain) × GAP-32 (periodic submission).
- **Why implementation requires a decision:** Alg. 3's inner loop bound li is unknown, so the reconstruction depth per G_k is undefined. Several batches of one server may complete between consecutive anchors.
- **What the paper explicitly says:** Alg. 3 "Parse G_k as (... omega_{pi,li} ...)" then "for l <= li" (p. 3289); Eq. (3) stores omega values only.
- **What the paper does NOT say:** the source of li.
- **Classification:** IMPLEMENTATION-NECESSARY-BUT-UNSPECIFIED (conflict between user rulings)
- **Possible choices:**
  - (a) carry li alongside each entry (reverses GAP-08)
  - (b) submit every generated state on-chain (contradicts §4.3.3's no-signed off-chain rule and GAP-15)
  - (c) the auditor also reads no-signed states from servers (contradicts DEV-10's ledger-only source)
  - (d) the auditor computes pi's full chain from the logs and matches each anchor's omega_{pi} against it (changes Alg. 3's comparison logic; would be a new deviation)
- **Effect on faithfulness / benchmark:** determines whether Alg. 3 can run at all; affects Fig. 14.
- **User decision required:** YES

### GAP-62 (CONFLICT-3)
- **Status:** DECIDED
- **User ruling (revision 3):** sid_a is a 256-bit pseudo-random unique opaque identifier that SCC generates at federation activation, e.g. a hash of the previous block hash, a federation counter and the ordered child sids. Its randomness is not security-critical; the only objective is a unique opaque identifier. This operationalizes "randomly generated" and replaces GAP-21's "not hash-derived".
- **Ruling classification:** IMPLEMENTATION-NECESSARY-BUT-UNSPECIFIED (user-decided)
- **Topic:** source of a "random, not hash-derived" parent sid on Ethereum.
- **Rulings in conflict:** GAP-21 (sid_a is a unique RANDOM value, not hash-derived) × the platform (Solidity has no native randomness).
- **Why implementation requires a decision:** the contract must obtain the value from somewhere.
- **What the paper explicitly says:** "sid_a is randomly generated" (§4.3.1, p. 3291).
- **What the paper does NOT say:** the randomness source.
- **Classification:** IMPLEMENTATION-NECESSARY-BUT-UNSPECIFIED (conflict between ruling and platform)
- **Possible choices:**
  - (a) block-hash derived (contradicts "not hash-derived")
  - (b) leader-supplied value, checked only for uniqueness
  - (c) commit-reveal among the child leaders
  - (d) contract counter (unique, not random)
- **Effect on faithfulness / benchmark:** small gas differences; (c) adds transactions.
- **User decision required:** YES

### GAP-63 (CONFLICT-4)
- **Status:** DECIDED (residual: GAP-85)
- **User ruling (revision 3):** A minimal, permissionless FinalizeOffline transaction. OfflineMsg records startBlock, and cancellation is allowed during the one-block challenge interval. After that interval anyone may call FinalizeOffline, which adds the server to OffList if no valid cancellation was received. The user classifies this as implementation-necessary, not a DELIA optimization: a contract cannot wake itself after one block.
- **Ruling classification:** IMPLEMENTATION-NECESSARY-BUT-UNSPECIFIED (user-decided)
- **Documented deviation:** DEV-19.
- **Topic:** offline-marking finalization trigger.
- **Rulings in conflict:** GAP-43 gives two alternatives: an explicit any-caller transaction after the window, or lazy finalization on the next SCC interaction.
- **Why implementation requires a decision:** contract entry points and the TEST-34 procedure differ.
- **What the paper explicitly says:** "In the waiting time, if the state channel instance does not receive any cancelling message, it adds PK_pi and H(psi_{pi,li}) to its own OffList" (§4.3.5, p. 3294).
- **What the paper does NOT say:** how a contract acts after a wait.
- **Classification:** IMPLEMENTATION-NECESSARY-BUT-UNSPECIFIED (unresolved alternative in a ruling)
- **Possible choices:** (a) explicit finalize transaction; (b) lazy on the next interaction with that instance; (c) both.
- **Effect on faithfulness / benchmark:** (a) adds a transaction to Fig. 13(a) "Offline" gas.
- **User decision required:** YES

### GAP-64 (CONFLICT-5)
- **Status:** DECIDED (residual: GAP-88)
- **User ruling (revision 3):** SCC does not rerun Alg. 4 over historical states. Participants run checkpoint selection off-chain over the retained signed-state history. SCC checks the submitted checkpoint's signatures plus the dispute vote evidence, and the majority-certified result determines the checkpoint. No complete historical state arrays are added to the contract. Pairs with GAP-71.
- **Ruling classification:** IMPLEMENTATION-NECESSARY-BUT-UNSPECIFIED (user-decided)
- **Documented deviation:** DEV-04 (rewritten).
- **Topic:** SCC cannot verify that a DisputeMsg serial equals the Alg. 4 checkpoint.
- **Rulings in conflict:** GAP-33 (serial must equal the Alg. 4 checkpoint) × GAP-35 (the contract does not rescan history) × GAP-15 (no on-chain history array; events only). Contracts cannot read event logs.
- **Why implementation requires a decision:** the checkpoint check cannot be implemented on-chain.
- **What the paper explicitly says:** DisputeMsg is "composed of signed round state in the checkpoint"; valid "if State Update Rules are satisfied" (§4.3.4, p. 3293).
- **What the paper does NOT say:** on-chain checkpoint verification.
- **Classification:** IMPLEMENTATION-NECESSARY-BUT-UNSPECIFIED (conflict between user rulings)
- **Possible choices:**
  - (a) SCC checks only signatures and vote evidence, with checkpoint correctness enforced off-chain by voters (drops the on-chain serial check)
  - (b) store a minimal per-instance list of accepted anchor serials on-chain (amends GAP-15)
  - (c) the submitter supplies the anchor states as calldata, checked against a stored commitment (adds an authenticated structure, prohibited by Section 26 unless approved)
- **Effect on faithfulness / benchmark:** affects dispute gas (Fig. 13(a)) and dispute security.
- **User decision required:** YES

### GAP-65 (CONFLICT-6)
- **Status:** DECIDED (residual: GAP-88)
- **User ruling (revision 3):** The excluded participant's slot is frozen at the rollback checkpoint, not deleted or re-indexed. Exclusion removes the participant from active HMSC participation and signing, but the domain-wide GlobalState layout stays stable. No later LocalState updates for that participant are accepted unless membership is explicitly re-established.
- **Ruling classification:** IMPLEMENTATION-NECESSARY-BUT-UNSPECIFIED (user-decided)
- **Documented limitation:** LIM-05.
- **Topic:** removing an excluded participant from P vs GlobalState positions frozen at Init.
- **Rulings in conflict:** GAP-36 (loser removed from active P) × GAP-08 (positions follow P frozen at Init) × GAP-10 (domain-wide array).
- **Why implementation requires a decision:** array length n, Rule 2/3 counts, election and audit positions all depend on it.
- **What the paper explicitly says:** "one participant between leader and the dispute initiator will be excluded" (§4.3.4, p. 3293).
- **What the paper does NOT say:** the effect on GlobalState.
- **Classification:** IMPLEMENTATION-NECESSARY-BUT-UNSPECIFIED (conflict between user rulings)
- **Possible choices:**
  - (a) the slot remains, frozen (Rule 2-like), and the participant is removed from signing and election sets
  - (b) the slot is zeroed
  - (c) the slot is removed and positions re-indexed (changes n, encodings, and audit of earlier states)
- **Effect on faithfulness / benchmark:** (c) breaks comparison with earlier states in Alg. 3.
- **User decision required:** YES

### GAP-66 (CONFLICT-7)
- **Status:** DECIDED
- **User ruling (revision 3):** For SCC checks, nu* = the serial of the latest signed state stored on-chain in the root instance. The paper's "latest state conducted by the root channel's leader" includes later off-chain no-signed states that SCC cannot know, so this is the nearest enforceable reading. Rule 1 remains nu > nu*.
- **Ruling classification:** IMPLEMENTATION-NECESSARY-BUT-UNSPECIFIED (user-decided)
- **Documented deviation:** DEV-20.
- **Topic:** on-chain representation of nu* when the root leader does not sign no-signed states and only periodic anchors are submitted.
- **Rulings in conflict:** GAP-29 (nu* = latest state conducted by the root channel leader) × GAP-26 (only the originating instance leader signs) × GAP-32 (periodic submission).
- **Why implementation requires a decision:** Rule 1 and Rule 2 need a concrete on-chain nu* / G_nu*.
- **What the paper explicitly says:** Rule 1 "nu > nu*, where nu* is the serial number of the latest state conducted by the root channel's leader" (§4.3.3, p. 3292).
- **What the paper does NOT say:** how the ledger learns that serial.
- **Classification:** IMPLEMENTATION-NECESSARY-BUT-UNSPECIFIED (conflict between user rulings)
- **Possible choices:** nu* = (a) serial of the latest accepted anchor in the root instance's G; (b) in Γx's own G; (c) the maximum over all stored G in the hierarchy.
- **Effect on faithfulness / benchmark:** determines replay protection scope (Thm. 2) and whether child updates can be rejected by root progress.
- **User decision required:** YES

### GAP-67 (CONFLICT-8)
- **Status:** DECIDED
- **User ruling (revision 3):** Both rulings coexist. beta has no protocol default and is required configuration per instance. For an experiment where the paper gives no beta, beta = 5 is an EXPERIMENT-ONLY value, not a DELIA default.
- **Ruling classification:** IMPLEMENTATION-NECESSARY-BUT-UNSPECIFIED (user-decided) for the no-default rule; EXPERIMENT-ONLY for beta = 5.
- **Topic:** "no defaults for beta" (GAP-55) vs "beta = 5 outside Fig. 12" (GAP-56).
- **Why implementation requires a decision:** to confirm that beta = 5 lives only in experiment configuration files and never in protocol code or requirements.
- **What the paper explicitly says:** beta "is not fixed in different state channel instances" (§4.3.2); Fig. 12 uses beta in {5, 10}.
- **What the paper does NOT say:** a default beta.
- **Classification:** IMPLEMENTATION-NECESSARY-BUT-UNSPECIFIED
- **Possible choices:** (a) confirm: beta = 5 is a per-experiment config value outside protocol requirements; (b) remove the beta = 5 setting.
- **Effect on faithfulness / benchmark:** none if (a).
- **User decision required:** YES (confirmation)

### GAP-68
- **Status:** DECIDED - CONFLICT PENDING (GAP-87)
- **User ruling (revision 3):** (c). A parent instance needs the expected num - numoff child-leader signatures, plus the parent leader's signature separately when the parent leader is not one of those child leaders. When it is, one signature satisfies both requirements. GAP-23 is not reversed.
- **Ruling classification:** IMPLEMENTATION-NECESSARY-BUT-UNSPECIFIED (user-decided)
- **Documented deviation:** DEV-18.
- **Analyst note:** GAP-72 binds the signer role into the signed bytes, so one signature carries one role; see GAP-87.
- **Topic:** parent leader may not be in the parent's signing set.
- **Rulings in conflict:** GAP-23 (a) (parent candidate set = all of P_a) × GAP-30 (a parent's signers are the child leaders; exactly num - numoff signers; one must be the leader's) × paper Rule 3 (num = entries in ChildList).
- **Why implementation requires a decision:** if the elected parent leader is not a child leader, a valid parent signed state needs num - numoff + 1 signatures, violating "exactly".
- **What the paper explicitly says:** Rule 3 and the num definition (§4.3.3); Fig. 8 shows the root leader p1 not leading its child Γ1 (INC-19).
- **What the paper does NOT say:** how the parent leader's signature is counted.
- **Classification:** IMPLEMENTATION-NECESSARY-BUT-UNSPECIFIED (conflict between user rulings)
- **Possible choices:**
  - (a) signing set = child leaders ∪ {parent leader}, with num adjusted (amends the paper's num)
  - (b) elect the parent leader only among child leaders (reverses GAP-23 (a))
  - (c) the leader signature is one of the child leaders' only when coincident; otherwise count it separately
- **Effect on faithfulness / benchmark:** affects Fig. 11(b).
- **User decision required:** YES

### GAP-69
- **Status:** DECIDED
- **User ruling (revision 3):** (c) for the reproduction: pause that server's log-reader generation while it is considered offline. The paper defines no backlog ingestion, and adding one would invent a protocol. For a real system the behavior stays a documented limitation.
- **Ruling classification:** EXPERIMENT-ONLY (user-decided)
- **Documented limitation:** LIM-01.
- **Topic:** records pi generates while offline vs index-based batching and the audit.
- **Rulings in conflict:** GAP-44 (offline-period records outside the baseline; the next batch chains from the frozen LocalState) × Eq. (1) index-based batches × GAP-16 (missing/inserted records fail batches) × Alg. 3 reading pi's whole log.
- **Why implementation requires a decision:** the auditor reconstructs batches from all of pi's records by index. Records created offline shift every later batch boundary relative to what peers hashed, so every post-offline batch would fail.
- **What the paper explicitly says:** after coming online pi "needs to synchronize all the GlobalState data and LogCacheQueue data from other servers" (§3.3 stage 5); nothing about offline-period records.
- **What the paper does NOT say:** whether pi logs while offline, or how those records are batched.
- **Classification:** IMPLEMENTATION-NECESSARY-BUT-UNSPECIFIED (conflict between ruling and algorithm)
- **Possible choices:**
  - (a) offline-period records are segregated and excluded from the audited sequence (new behavior)
  - (b) pi broadcasts offline-period hashes in order after rejoin (contradicts "no backlog ingestion")
  - (c) pi's log reader is paused while offline, so no such records exist (experiment convention)
  - (d) accept and document the audit failures as expected
- **Effect on faithfulness / benchmark:** affects TEST-37 and any offline experiment.
- **User decision required:** YES

### GAP-70
- **Status:** DECIDED
- **User ruling (revision 3):** (a). The first recovery state after rollback must be a signed-round state, submitted on-chain immediately. SCC verifies that its serial is checkpoint + 1 and that the required leader signature is NewLeader's; after acceptance SCC clears NewLeader. This follows the paper's statement that the next state must be G_{nu+1} with the new leader's signature.
- **Ruling classification:** IMPLEMENTATION-NECESSARY-BUT-UNSPECIFIED (user-decided)
- **Topic:** the event that clears NewLeader may never be observable on-chain.
- **Rulings in conflict:** GAP-38 (NewLeader cleared when the first valid state at checkpoint+1 signed by NewLeader is accepted) × GAP-26 (the state after an anchor is normally a no-signed, off-chain state) × GAP-32 (periodic submission).
- **Why implementation requires a decision:** SCC only sees submitted signed-round states, so it may never see checkpoint+1. NewLeader_x would stay set indefinitely, and rotation (GAP-22) would stay overridden.
- **What the paper explicitly says:** "the next state received by Γx must be G_{nu+1} and leader's signature must be Sign(G_{nu+1} \| NewLeader_x) ... After dispute resolved, NewLeader_x will be set null" (§4.3.4, pp. 3293-3294).
- **What the paper does NOT say:** what "resolved" means on-chain.
- **Classification:** IMPLEMENTATION-NECESSARY-BUT-UNSPECIFIED (conflict between user rulings)
- **Possible choices:**
  - (a) after rollback, checkpoint+1 must be a signed-round anchor submitted on-chain immediately (amends GAP-26/GAP-32)
  - (b) a dedicated transaction carrying the checkpoint+1 state signed by NewLeader clears NewLeader (new entry point)
  - (c) clear NewLeader at the next accepted Update of Γx, whatever its serial (weakens GAP-38)
- **Effect on faithfulness / benchmark:** affects dispute gas and state-loss measurement.
- **User decision required:** YES

### GAP-71
- **Status:** DECIDED
- **User ruling (revision 3):** (c). For DisputeMsg, verify the checkpoint state's own signatures plus the dispute/vote evidence. Current P/OffList are not applied to an old checkpoint, and no historical membership snapshots are created. Pairs with GAP-64.
- **Ruling classification:** IMPLEMENTATION-NECESSARY-BUT-UNSPECIFIED (user-decided)
- **Documented deviation:** DEV-04 (rewritten).
- **Topic:** "Validate signatures and Rules 2-4 where meaningful" for DisputeMsg is not operational.
- **Rulings in conflict:** GAP-33 × Rule 2 (compare OffList entries with G_nu*, the latest state) × GAP-30 (expected online signer set) × GAP-36 (P may change after exclusion) × GAP-31 (Rule 4 nulls children).
- **Why implementation requires a decision:**
  - A checkpoint older than an offline transition fails Rule 2 against the latest state.
  - Rule 3's expected signer set may differ between checkpoint time and now (OffList changes, exclusions).
  - Applying Rule 4 to an older checkpoint would null child states. That might be intended as rollback, but it is not stated.
- **What the paper explicitly says:** DisputeMsg is "valid, if State Update Rules are satisfied" (§4.3.4).
- **What the paper does NOT say:** a dispute-specific rule set.
- **Classification:** IMPLEMENTATION-NECESSARY-BUT-UNSPECIFIED (ruling underspecified)
- **Possible choices:**
  - (a) Rules 2-3 evaluated against OffList/P as they were when the checkpoint was produced (needs history, conflicting with GAP-15)
  - (b) against current OffList/P (spurious rejections possible)
  - (c) signature validity of the checkpoint plus vote evidence only; no Rules 2-4
  - (d) Rule 4 nulling treated as part of rollback (GAP-38)
- **Effect on faithfulness / benchmark:** determines whether disputes can succeed after membership changes.
- **User decision required:** YES

### GAP-72
- **Status:** DECIDED (residual: GAP-86)
- **User ruling (revision 3):** (a). Bind the paper-defined purpose and the signer role/identity into the signed bytes, without sid (GAP-09 preserved). Purposes distinguish STATE, CLOSE, RECOVERY and VOTE, following the paper's (G_nu \| p4), (G_nu \| Close) and recovery with NewLeader. Cross-instance replay remains a documented baseline risk, because the paper does not bind state signatures to sid; DELIA is not redesigned to prevent it.
- **Ruling classification:** IMPLEMENTATION-NECESSARY-BUT-UNSPECIFIED (user-decided)
- **Documented limitation:** LIM-02.
- **Topic:** composition of signed payloads, and cross-instance / cross-purpose signature replay.
- **Rulings in conflict:** GAP-09 (signed object = canonical (nu, ordered entries); no sid) × GAP-27 (deterministic encoding) × GAP-10 (domain-wide state, identical across instances) × GAP-45 (Close verified like a signed-round state; Close identifier encoding not ruled) × paper notation.
- **Why implementation requires a decision:**
  - The paper signs objects with suffixes: (G_nu \| p4) (§4.3.2 Step 2), (G_nu \| Close) (§4.3.6), Sign(G_{nu+1} \| NewLeader_x) (§4.3.4). GAP-09 defines no suffix or tag.
  - Without an instance or purpose tag, a child leader's signature on G_nu in Γ1 is byte-identical to its signature as a signer of the parent Γ3.
  - An ordinary signed state and a Close state may be indistinguishable, so an ordinary signed round state could be submitted as a CloseMsg.
- **What the paper explicitly says:** the suffix notation above; `|` is never defined.
- **What the paper does NOT say:** the bytes behind `|`; any replay protection.
- **Classification:** IMPLEMENTATION-NECESSARY-BUT-UNSPECIFIED (conflict between ruling and paper notation)
- **Possible choices:**
  - (a) signed payload = encoding(nu, entries, purpose-tag in {state, close}, signer-role identity where the paper shows one); still no sid
  - (b) add sid to the payload (reverses the GAP-09 sid exclusion)
  - (c) keep (nu, entries) only, and accept replay and Close ambiguity as documented risks
- **Effect on faithfulness / benchmark:** security-relevant; minor gas effect.
- **User decision required:** YES

### GAP-73
- **Status:** DECIDED (residual: GAP-86)
- **User ruling (revision 3):** (a), with a clarification. This is an implementation encoding, not something the paper claims.
  - GlobalState: uint64 nu (big-endian) \|\| uint32 entry_count (big-endian) \|\| LocalState[0..n-1], 32 bytes each, in the canonical participant order frozen at Init.
  - Frame: uint32 frame_length (big-endian, number of bytes that follow) \|\| uint8 message_type \|\| message-specific fields.
  - Raw bytes, not hex strings. SHA-256 hashes are exactly 32 bytes. Public keys use one fixed canonical encoding throughout the system.
  - Signed bytes: purpose \|\| signer_role \|\| encoded_state, both prefixes fixed one-byte enumerations (GAP-72).
  - OfflineInquiry = (sid, targetParticipant); OfflineInquiryAck = (sid, targetParticipant). No extra signature: TLS authenticates the connection.
  - OnlineNotification = (sid, returningParticipant). The paper's Online State Confirmation process then starts.
- **Ruling classification:** IMPLEMENTATION-NECESSARY-BUT-UNSPECIFIED (user-decided)
- **Delegated (analyst reading; needs user confirmation):** the numeric values of the purpose, signer-role and message_type enumerations; which canonical public-key encoding is used (this also fixes the GAP-22 key order); the byte encoding of sid and participant identifiers inside messages; the field layouts of the other messages; the on-disk layout. These are left to the implementer and frozen in one normative test-vector file.
- **Topic:** concrete byte layouts. Also the payload fields of two messages that no ruling defines: the offline inquiry (NET-020) and the online notification (NET-024).
- **Why implementation requires a decision:** bit-exact cross-node equality, SHA-256 signing digests verified in Solidity, message framing and on-disk files all need one layout. The rulings say "canonical", "deterministic" and "length-prefixed" without fixing widths or order.
- **What the paper explicitly says:** nothing.
- **What the paper does NOT say:** any encoding.
- **Classification:** IMPLEMENTATION-NECESSARY-BUT-UNSPECIFIED
- **Possible choices:**
  - (a) nu as uint64 big-endian, followed by n x 32-byte entries in P order (n implied by P); frames = uint32 big-endian length + type byte + fields in paper order
  - (b) a layout identical to Solidity `abi.encodePacked` of the same fields, for simpler on-chain hashing
  - (c) delegate the exact layout to the implementer, with one normative test-vector file
- **Effect on faithfulness / benchmark:** gas cost of on-chain hashing and decoding (Fig. 13(a)).
- **User decision required:** YES (or explicit delegation)

### GAP-74
- **Status:** DECIDED
- **User ruling (revision 3):** (a): root = leaf. H(leaf \|\| leaf) is not computed unless there really are two children, so no undocumented hash operation is added.
- **Ruling classification:** IMPLEMENTATION-NECESSARY-BUT-UNSPECIFIED (user-decided)
- **Topic:** Merkle root when theta = 1, or any level with a single node.
- **Why implementation requires a decision:** "odd node duplicated" could apply to the single leaf (root = H(L \|\| L)), or the lone leaf could already be the root (root = L).
- **What the paper explicitly says:** nothing (Fig. 2 shows 4 leaves).
- **What the paper does NOT say:** single-leaf handling.
- **Classification:** IMPLEMENTATION-NECESSARY-BUT-UNSPECIFIED
- **Possible choices:** (a) root = the leaf; (b) root = H(leaf \|\| leaf); (c) disallow theta = 1 by configuration.
- **Effect on faithfulness / benchmark:** none for the experiment values of theta.
- **User decision required:** YES (low impact)

### GAP-75
- **Status:** DECIDED
- **User ruling (revision 3):** (a): skip the comparison for that server until it has completed its first batch. G_0[i] = 0^256 is only an initialization sentinel and must not be treated as a genuine LocalState produced from logs.
- **Ruling classification:** IMPLEMENTATION-NECESSARY-BUT-UNSPECIFIED (user-decided)
- **Topic:** Alg. 3 when pi's slot in G_k is still the zero digest.
- **Why implementation requires a decision:** with no completed batch, the inner loop reconstructs nothing, omega is undefined, and the comparison omega != omega_{pi,li} (the zero digest) has no defined outcome. States in which other servers advanced while pi had no batch hit this case.
- **What the paper explicitly says:** Alg. 3 listing (p. 3289).
- **What the paper does NOT say:** handling of an unset LocalState.
- **Classification:** IMPLEMENTATION-NECESSARY-BUT-UNSPECIFIED
- **Possible choices:** (a) skip the comparison while pi's slot is the zero digest and pi's reconstructed batch count is 0; (b) initialize omega to the zero digest before the loop, so the comparison holds trivially; (c) flag as an error.
- **Effect on faithfulness / benchmark:** affects TEST-11 and TEST-12 at the start of the history.
- **User decision required:** YES

### GAP-76
- **Status:** DECIDED (residual: GAP-89)
- **User ruling (revision 3):** (a) for an ordinary/root Close: signatures from all members of that instance's P, including currently offline ones, because the paper says the root close state contains signatures from all participants. An offline participant can therefore prevent a graceful Close, and the paper is not weakened to make closing easier. For Disassociate, each child produces its normal signed-round close state and the parent leader collects them. Direct nested disassociation (the parent being disassociated has its own parent) is not supported; that parent must first be detached from its own parent.
- **Ruling classification:** IMPLEMENTATION-NECESSARY-BUT-UNSPECIFIED (user-decided)
- **Documented limitations:** LIM-03, LIM-04.
- **Topic:** "complete signer set" for Close, and DisassociateMsg preconditions.
- **Rulings in conflict:** GAP-45 (Close requires the complete signer set; reject direct closure while Parent != null) × GAP-30 (a parent's signers are the child leaders; numoff excludes offline members) × paper §4.3.6 ("signatures from all participants of root state channel instance") × INC-23.
- **Why implementation requires a decision:**
  - For a root that is a parent, "complete" could mean all of P_a or all child leaders.
  - If "complete" ignores numoff, an instance with any OffList member can never close.
  - It is unstated whether DisassociateMsg is allowed when Γa itself has a Parent.
- **What the paper explicitly says:** §4.3.6, p. 3294.
- **What the paper does NOT say:** the above.
- **Classification:** IMPLEMENTATION-NECESSARY-BUT-UNSPECIFIED (conflict between ruling and paper)
- **Possible choices:**
  - Signer set: (a) all of P of the root, OffList members included; (b) the Rule 3 signer set (num - numoff); (c) child leaders only.
  - Disassociation: allowed only when Γa.Parent == null, or always.
- **Effect on faithfulness / benchmark:** affects Close gas (Fig. 13(a)).
- **User decision required:** YES

### GAP-77
- **Status:** DECIDED
- **User ruling (revision 3):** (a): mutual-TLS client certificate. The paper requires authenticated and authorized actors and the prototype already uses TLS, so the same authenticated TLS channel is the smallest addition. No separate application-level auditor signing key.
- **Ruling classification:** IMPLEMENTATION-NECESSARY-BUT-UNSPECIFIED (user-decided)
- **Topic:** auditor authentication mechanism.
- **Why implementation requires a decision:** the ruling requires auditor authentication for log retrieval but leaves the mechanism unspecified.
- **What the paper explicitly says:** all participants "should be authenticated and authorized" (§2.1, p. 3287).
- **What the paper does NOT say:** how the auditor is authenticated.
- **Classification:** IMPLEMENTATION-NECESSARY-BUT-UNSPECIFIED
- **Possible choices:** (a) mutual-TLS client certificate from the same private CA (GAP-48); (b) an auditor secp256k1 key listed in config, signing requests; (c) both.
- **Effect on faithfulness / benchmark:** none on the protocol.
- **User decision required:** YES

### GAP-78
- **Status:** DECIDED
- **User ruling (revision 3):** (a): 1 s block time everywhere in the reproduction, with no per-figure tuning. If the paper is later found to give an explicit block time for a particular experiment, that value is used; otherwise 1 s.
- **Ruling classification:** EXPERIMENT-ONLY (user-decided)
- **Topic:** block-time exceptions.
- **Rulings in conflict:** GAP-50 ("fixed block time, 1 s unless a figure requires otherwise") × GAP-56 ("the same fixed PoA chain and 1 s blocks for all results").
- **Why implementation requires a decision:** the experiment configs must be fixed before runs.
- **What the paper explicitly says:** nothing about block time.
- **What the paper does NOT say:** consensus or block time.
- **Classification:** IMPLEMENTATION-NECESSARY-BUT-UNSPECIFIED (conflict between user rulings)
- **Possible choices:** (a) 1 s for every figure, no exceptions; (b) per-figure exceptions allowed, each labelled in the experiment record.
- **Effect on faithfulness / benchmark:** the offline challenge duration and all on-chain latency figures.
- **User decision required:** YES

### GAP-79
- **Status:** DECIDED
- **User ruling (revision 3):** (a): finalized on-chain OffList only. Local suspicion and pending OfflineMsgs do not affect leader eligibility until the challenge window finishes, so every node derives the same leader.
- **Ruling classification:** IMPLEMENTATION-NECESSARY-BUT-UNSPECIFIED (user-decided)
- **Topic:** the meaning of "offline" in leader election and new-leader candidacy.
- **Rulings in conflict:** GAP-22 ("Participants in OffList are skipped" and "If the elected leader is offline, advance to the next non-offline key") × GAP-37 ("candidates exclude ... currently offline participants") × GAP-41/GAP-43 (off-chain detection, then on-chain finalization after a challenge window).
- **Why implementation requires a decision:** OffList is on-chain and finalized, while "offline" by local detection can differ between participants. A deterministic election computed by every participant would then diverge. On-chain candidate selection can only see OffList.
- **What the paper explicitly says:** rotation over P (§4.3.2); "a new leader is selected randomly" (§4.3.4).
- **What the paper does NOT say:** offline handling in either.
- **Classification:** IMPLEMENTATION-NECESSARY-BUT-UNSPECIFIED (ambiguity between user rulings)
- **Possible choices:** "offline" means (a) listed in the finalized on-chain OffList only; (b) OffList plus pending OfflineMsg requests; (c) local detection (non-deterministic).
- **Effect on faithfulness / benchmark:** leader-rotation determinism.
- **User decision required:** YES

### GAP-80
- **Status:** DECIDED (residual: GAP-82)
- **User ruling (revision 3):** (a): OffList = empty, NewLeader = null, Parent = null, ChildList = [child sid_1..sid_m], G = the current accepted domain GlobalState; when federation activates, each child's Parent = parent.sid. G is not left null until a future parent Update, because the parent exists to coordinate child channels that are already running.
- **Ruling classification:** IMPLEMENTATION-NECESSARY-BUT-UNSPECIFIED (user-decided)
- **Topic:** parent-instance initial fields, and setting the children's Parent.
- **Why implementation requires a decision:** the GAP-21 ruling does not fix initial OffList_a, NewLeader_a or G_a, and does not explicitly say children's Parent becomes sid_a on activation. The GAP-20 partial ruling ("initial G of a new instance = current domain state") and the GAP-45 ruling ("reset each direct child's Parent = null") imply, but do not state, both.
- **What the paper explicitly says:** Parent_a null; ChildList_a includes sid_1..sid_m; sid_a random (§4.3.1). Null initial values are stated only for "initialization process".
- **What the paper does NOT say:** the other parent fields, or updating the children.
- **Classification:** IMPLEMENTATION-NECESSARY-BUT-UNSPECIFIED
- **Possible choices:** (a) OffList_a = null, NewLeader_a = null, G_a = current domain state, each child's Parent = sid_a on activation; (b) G_a = null until the first parent Update.
- **Effect on faithfulness / benchmark:** negligible.
- **User decision required:** YES (confirmation)

### 25.2 Conflicts and residuals introduced by the revision 3 rulings

Found while applying the revision 3 rulings. None is resolved here.

### GAP-81
- **Status:** OPEN CONFLICT (found in revision 3)
- **Topic:** every signed anchor must reach the chain, but submission is periodic.
- **Rulings in conflict:** GAP-60 (SCC's rotation advances only per accepted signed-round anchor) × GAP-61 (the auditor cross-checks every signed-round anchor against the ledger) × GAP-32 (signed-round states are submitted periodically, with a configured period).
- **Why implementation requires a decision:**
  - Off-chain, every signed round advances the rotation. If an anchor is produced but not submitted, SCC still expects the leader that follows the last anchor it accepted, so the next submitted anchor carries a different leader's signature and fails Rule 3.
  - An anchor that never reaches the chain cannot be cross-checked by the auditor.
- **What the paper explicitly says:** "we could periodically update the state information" (§3.3 stage 4); a leader is elected per round (§4.3.2).
- **What the paper does NOT say:** whether every signed round state is submitted.
- **Classification:** IMPLEMENTATION-NECESSARY-BUT-UNSPECIFIED (conflict between user rulings)
- **Possible choices:**
  - (a) the submission "period" is every signed-round anchor, as soon as it is produced (narrows GAP-32)
  - (b) keep periodic submission, but advance the off-chain rotation only on anchors accepted on-chain (couples Confirm to ledger latency)
  - (c) keep periodic submission and accept that unsubmitted anchors are not cross-checked and later anchors may be rejected (weakens GAP-60 and GAP-61)
- **Effect on faithfulness / benchmark:** (a) and (b) fix the number of Update transactions per round (Fig. 13(a), 13(b)); (b) adds ledger latency to confirmation (Fig. 11(b), Fig. 12).
- **User decision required:** YES

### GAP-82
- **Status:** OPEN (residual of GAP-60, GAP-21, GAP-80)
- **Topic:** leader-derivation edge cases.
- **Why implementation requires a decision:**
  - FederateMsg sender check (GAP-21): "current leader" could mean the leader recorded in the child's latest accepted anchor (the leader of the round in progress), or the next expected leader (the one who will sign the next anchor).
  - A parent's first signed round: G_a starts as the current accepted domain state (GAP-80), which was signed in a child and records a child leader. It is unstated whether the parent's first leader is the minimum eligible key in P_a ("first signed round") or the key after that recorded leader.
  - Which accepted state G_a takes when the children's stored states differ.
  - Rule 4 nulls a direct child's G (GAP-31). If the recorded leader is read from G, a nulled G loses it, and rotation would restart at the minimum key.
- **What the paper explicitly says:** minimum-PK rotation (§4.3.2); the parent's initial Parent and ChildList (§4.3.1).
- **What the paper does NOT say:** any of the above.
- **Classification:** IMPLEMENTATION-NECESSARY-BUT-UNSPECIFIED
- **Possible choices:**
  - "current leader" = the recorded leader of the latest accepted anchor (the minimum eligible key if there is none), or the next expected leader
  - a parent's first leader = the minimum eligible key in P_a, or the key after the leader recorded in G_a
  - G_a = the child state with the highest serial, or the state of a designated child
  - the recorded leader is stored separately from G and survives nulling, or it resets to the first-signed-round rule when G is null
- **Effect on faithfulness / benchmark:** no cost effect; determines which node leads.
- **User decision required:** YES

### GAP-83
- **Status:** OPEN (residual of GAP-61)
- **Topic:** the auditor's history retrieval.
- **Why implementation requires a decision:** GAP-61 says the auditor obtains the history "from a server" but does not say:
  - which server: the audited pi, a configured server, any server, or several compared
  - the request and response fields (NET-037)
  - what the auditor does when a no-signed signature fails, a serial is missing, or an anchor differs from the event log. ErrList holds only batch numbers (AUDIT-009), so such failures cannot be reported there.
- **What the paper explicitly says:** the auditor obtains the logs from pi and the states from the distributed ledger (§3.4 stage 2).
- **What the paper does NOT say:** any of the above.
- **Classification:** IMPLEMENTATION-NECESSARY-BUT-UNSPECIFIED
- **Possible choices:**
  - source: (a) the audited pi; (b) a server named in the audit configuration; (c) two servers, compared
  - failed check: (a) the audit aborts with an integrity error separate from ErrList; (b) the affected serial range is reported separately and Alg. 3 continues over the verified states
- **Effect on faithfulness / benchmark:** audit result semantics; retrieval time only indirectly affects Fig. 14.
- **User decision required:** YES

### GAP-84
- **Status:** OPEN (residual of GAP-38, GAP-61)
- **Topic:** auditing batches whose states were lost in a dispute rollback.
- **Why implementation requires a decision:** after a rollback, the states between the checkpoint and the disputed latest state are discarded (DISPUTE-016, GAP-38). A server whose batch completed in a lost state still has those log records, but its LocalState is back at the checkpoint value. Alg. 3 rebuilds batches by record index over the whole log. Unless the lost batch reappears in a later state, every later batch of that server is misaligned with its LocalState chain and fails.
- **What the paper explicitly says:** "we lose nu - nu* states" (§4.3.4).
- **What the paper does NOT say:** whether a server re-proposes a batch whose state was lost, or how the audit treats those records.
- **Classification:** IMPLEMENTATION-NECESSARY-BUT-UNSPECIFIED
- **Possible choices:**
  - (a) the server re-runs Alg. 1 for the lost batch against the recovered state, as a losing proposal does under GAP-11
  - (b) records of lost batches are excluded from the audited sequence
  - (c) accept and document the resulting audit failures
  - (d) experiment convention: no batch completes between the checkpoint and the dispute
- **Effect on faithfulness / benchmark:** audits after dispute experiments (Fig. 12(b)).
- **User decision required:** YES

### GAP-85
- **Status:** OPEN (residual of GAP-43, GAP-63)
- **Topic:** exact block boundaries of the offline challenge interval.
- **Why implementation requires a decision:** with OfflineMsg accepted in block s, SCC must decide whether a cancel mined in block s + 1 is still inside the interval, and whether FinalizeOffline is first allowed in block s + 1 or s + 2. A cancel and a FinalizeOffline in the same block must not both succeed.
- **What the paper explicitly says:** "it waits for one block generation time" (§4.3.5); see INC-12.
- **What the paper does NOT say:** whether the bounds are inclusive or exclusive.
- **Classification:** IMPLEMENTATION-NECESSARY-BUT-UNSPECIFIED
- **Possible choices:** (a) cancel accepted while block.number <= s + 1; FinalizeOffline only when block.number > s + 1. (b) cancel accepted while block.number < s + 1; FinalizeOffline when block.number >= s + 1.
- **Effect on faithfulness / benchmark:** with 1 s blocks (GAP-78) the window is 1-2 s; TEST-34, 35 and 50 timing.
- **User decision required:** YES

### GAP-86
- **Status:** OPEN (residual of GAP-27, GAP-72, GAP-73)
- **Topic:** semantic parts of the signed bytes and messages that the rulings do not cover.
- **Why implementation requires a decision:**
  - Signer-role set: the rulings name the purposes (STATE, CLOSE, RECOVERY, VOTE) but not the roles, for example participant, instance leader, child leader, parent leader, and the offline server that cancels.
  - Purposes not assigned: the root leader's no-signed signature (CONFIRM-015), the signatures collected for OfflineMsg and OnlineMsg, and the offline server's cancel signature (NET-023).
  - RECOVERY: whether only NewLeader's signature on G_{nu+1} carries RECOVERY, or every signature on the recovery anchor.
  - VOTE: a vote is not a GlobalState, so "encoded_state" is undefined for it. Under GAP-64 the vote must also identify the checkpoint it certifies (see GAP-88).
  - The layout has no explicit signer-identity field; identity is carried only by the signing key (recovered on-chain). Confirm that this satisfies "signer role/identity".
  - FinalizeOffline calldata fields (NET-036).
- **What the paper explicitly says:** (G_nu \| p4), (G_nu \| Close), Sign(G_{nu+1} \| NewLeader_x); "a signed voting message" (§4.3.2, §4.3.4, §4.3.6).
- **What the paper does NOT say:** any of the above.
- **Classification:** IMPLEMENTATION-NECESSARY-BUT-UNSPECIFIED
- **Possible choices:**
  - (a) roles {PARTICIPANT, LEADER, CHILD_LEADER}; purpose STATE for every state signature that is not a Close, a vote or the recovery anchor, including offline, online, cancel and no-signed signatures; RECOVERY on every signature of the recovery anchor; VOTE payload = (checkpoint serial, checkpoint state digest, voted-for participant key)
  - (b) as (a), but RECOVERY only on NewLeader's signature
  - (c) add purposes OFFLINE, ONLINE and CANCEL (extends the ruled purpose list)
- **Effect on faithfulness / benchmark:** negligible gas; (c) stops an offline-confirmation signature being reused as a state signature.
- **User decision required:** YES

### GAP-87
- **Status:** OPEN CONFLICT (found in revision 3)
- **Topic:** one signature satisfying two roles.
- **Rulings in conflict:** GAP-68 (c) ("If it is already a child leader, one signature satisfies both requirements") × GAP-72 (a) (the signer role is bound into the signed bytes).
- **Why implementation requires a decision:** a signature made with the child-leader role is not a signature with the parent-leader role, because the role byte differs. Rule 3 counting for a parent whose leader is also a child leader (UPDATE-006, UPDATE-008) is therefore undefined.
- **What the paper explicitly says:** Rule 3 (§4.3.3); the Fig. 8 roles; the (G_nu \| p4) leader suffix (§4.3.2).
- **What the paper does NOT say:** role binding.
- **Classification:** IMPLEMENTATION-NECESSARY-BUT-UNSPECIFIED (conflict between user rulings)
- **Possible choices:**
  - (a) a combined role value used only when the roles coincide
  - (b) the coinciding signer signs twice, once per role, and the pair counts as one signer toward num - numoff (amends "one signature")
  - (c) in a parent instance, signatures bind the purpose but not the role (amends GAP-72 for parents)
- **Effect on faithfulness / benchmark:** signature counts in Fig. 11(b); Update gas.
- **User decision required:** YES

### GAP-88
- **Status:** OPEN (residual of GAP-36, GAP-64, GAP-65, GAP-71)
- **Topic:** on-chain dispute verification, and exclusion in a hierarchy.
- **Why implementation requires a decision:**
  - "Verify the checkpoint state's own signatures" is ruled without current P/OffList and without membership snapshots, but no key set is named. Without one, any key could sign a "checkpoint". Whether a count or a leader-role signature is required is also unstated.
  - Under GAP-64 the majority-certified result determines the checkpoint, so each vote must identify the checkpoint as well as the leader-or-initiator choice. SCC also needs the voter key set (current active P, GAP-36) to verify the votes.
  - The loser is removed from Γx's active participation. It is unstated whether it is also removed from ancestor instances (P_a contains every child participant) and what happens when the loser leads a child instance of a parent.
- **What the paper explicitly says:** §4.3.4, p. 3293.
- **What the paper does NOT say:** any of the above.
- **Classification:** IMPLEMENTATION-NECESSARY-BUT-UNSPECIFIED
- **Possible choices:**
  - checkpoint signatures: (a) each verifies under a key in Γx's P as frozen at Init, and one carries the leader role; (b) as (a), with signers forming a strict majority of that P; (c) validity only, with membership certified by the votes
  - votes: (a) vote = (checkpoint serial, checkpoint state digest, voted-for key), verified against current active P
  - hierarchy: (a) exclusion applies to Γx and every ancestor instance; (b) to Γx only
- **Effect on faithfulness / benchmark:** dispute gas (Fig. 13(a)); Fig. 12(b); Fig. 15.
- **User decision required:** YES

### GAP-89
- **Status:** OPEN (residual of GAP-45, GAP-76)
- **Topic:** Close on a parent instance, and excluded members in Close.
- **Why implementation requires a decision:**
  - GAP-76 rules an ordinary/root Close (all of P) and Disassociate (children's close states). A root parent has Parent = null, so GAP-45 does not reject a CloseMsg for it. It is unstated whether CloseMsg may close an instance whose ChildList is non-null, leaving children whose Parent names a closed instance.
  - "All members of that instance's P, including offline ones": it is unstated whether a participant excluded by a dispute vote (GAP-65) must also sign.
- **What the paper explicitly says:** §4.3.6, p. 3294.
- **What the paper does NOT say:** either point.
- **Classification:** IMPLEMENTATION-NECESSARY-BUT-UNSPECIFIED
- **Possible choices:**
  - parent Close: (a) CloseMsg rejected while ChildList != null (disassociate instead); (b) allowed with all of P_a, and each child's Parent reset to null
  - excluded participants: (a) they do not sign a Close; (b) they must sign, so a Close may become impossible
- **Effect on faithfulness / benchmark:** Close gas (Fig. 13(a)).
- **User decision required:** YES

## 26. Explicit list of things NOT to implement

None of the following appears in the DELIA paper. Each is prohibited unless the user approves a deviation in writing (TEST-42 checks this list).

**Data and Merkle**
1. Caching of Merkle roots, LocalStates, GlobalStates or record hashes beyond what Eq. (2) and Alg. 1-3 literally carry forward.
2. Incremental Merkle trees, alternative tree layouts (k-ary, sparse, MMR), Merkle inclusion proofs, multiproofs, proof aggregation.
3. Deduplication of record hashes, unless GAP-04 is decided that way on the strength of the paper's "hash set" wording.
4. Ring buffers, eviction policies, databases, indexes or extra persistence layers for LogCacheQueue, LocalState or GlobalState.

**LSG and audit**
5. Batching of record-hash broadcasts (LSG-002 is per record, immediately).
6. Parallel or concurrent Merkle construction, parallel Alg. 2 verification, parallel Alg. 3 batches, goroutine worker pools.
7. Binary-search tamper localization, sampling, probabilistic audit, or any replacement of Alg. 3's exhaustive recomputation.
8. HMF-Audit mechanisms or components.

**HMSC**
9. Alternative state channel protocols (e.g., MSC [23], Perun, Counterfactual) inside DELIA. MSC is only a possible benchmark baseline (EXP-024).
10. Alternative leader election: Raft, PBFT, random selection for normal rounds, weighted or reputation rules, consensus libraries. Randomness is used only for NewLeader (DISPUTE-012).
11. "Improved" checkpoint selection, or a silent fix of Alg. 4 (GAP-34 decides).
12. Automatic hierarchy construction, balanced trees, fixed fanout or automatic federation policy.
13. Heartbeats, timeouts or retry policies not decided under GAP-41 and GAP-47.
14. Signature aggregation (BLS, multi-sig schemes), threshold signatures, Schnorr, Ed25519.
15. On-chain challenge periods for Close (explicitly rejected, CLOSE-002).
16. Custom state pruning, archival or cleanup after Close or dispute.
17. Extra cryptographic fields (nonces, timestamps, MACs, domain tags) presented as DELIA requirements. Anything a gap decision adds is labelled IMPL with its GAP ID.

**Platform and transport**
18. Replacing the Solidity/Ethereum SCC with Go logic, a mock ledger, or a different blockchain, without approval.
19. REST, gRPC, WebSocket, protobuf, JSON, message brokers or queues as DELIA requirements. Only as a GAP-47 decision.
20. Communication compression, asynchronous processing pipelines, microservices, repository/ORM abstraction layers, generic plug-in frameworks.
21. Cloud providers, coordinators or any entity beyond servers, auditor and ledger nodes (SYS-001).
22. Performance acceptance thresholds derived from §6 numbers. Evaluation results are reproduction targets, not pass/fail criteria.

## Developer Handoff (revision 1 - SUPERSEDED by "Developer Handoff (revision 2)" at the end of this file; kept for history)

The requirements below can be implemented **now without inventing behavior**. They are structural or control-flow requirements whose inputs can remain abstract (for example, an `MHTGen` interface, or an opaque `GlobalState` equality function). They do not fix any value blocked by a gap. Everything else waits for decisions.

**Tier A: implementable immediately**
- Data model skeleton:
  - DELIA-DATA-001, DATA-003, DATA-004 (batch indexing with theta as a required config value, li from 0, xi from 1)
  - DATA-012, DATA-015 (chain order; one position changes per new GlobalState)
- LSG flow skeleton: DELIA-LSG-001, LSG-004, LSG-006, LSG-008 (sender stores own hash; batch-full trigger; receiver verifies using only local psi and G_{nu-1}).
- Algorithm control flow over abstract primitives:
  - DELIA-ALG1-004 (copy all entries, replace position i)
  - DELIA-ALG2-001, ALG2-003, ALG2-004, ALG2-006 (root check before GlobalGen; GlobalGen as subroutine; return codes)
  - DELIA-AUDIT-003, AUDIT-008, AUDIT-009, AUDIT-010 (signature, return semantics, batch-number ErrList, sequential with no proofs or caches)
- HMSC data model and constants:
  - DELIA-HMSC-ARCH-003 (seven Γ fields), ARCH-009 (per-instance beta config)
  - DELIA-HMSC-INIT-004 (OffList, NewLeader, Parent, ChildList null at Init)
  - DELIA-HMSC-CONFIRM-001, CONFIRM-007, CONFIRM-013, CONFIRM-018 (one leader per round; Step 1; 3-phase vs 1-phase; no-signed kept off-chain)
  - DELIA-HMSC-UPDATE-001 (ledger rejects no-signed states); UPDATE-003 for leaf instances (num = abs(P))
  - DELIA-HMSC-DISPUTE-008 (max(k1, k2)), DISPUTE-016 (no state-loss recovery)
  - DELIA-HMSC-OFFLINE-001; DELIA-HMSC-CLOSE-002 (no closure challenge period)
- Platform: DELIA-CRYPTO-002 (Go `crypto/sha256`), DELIA-NET-002, DELIA-SC-001, DELIA-SC-002 (keep Solidity on Ethereum), DELIA-GO-001, DELIA-GO-003.
- Section 26 prohibitions (TEST-42).

**Tier B: implementable as soon as the "primitive" gaps are decided**
- Primitive gaps: GAP-01 Merkle, GAP-02 concatenation/representation, GAP-03 record encoding, GAP-04 set semantics, GAP-06 initial state, GAP-07 li = 0, GAP-08 index/li, GAP-09 state encoding, GAP-27 signatures, GAP-28 identity, GAP-47 transport.
- Requirements unblocked by those decisions, with no further invention:
  - DATA-002, 008, 010, 011, 013, 014; LSG-002, 003, 007, 010; ALG1-001..003; ALG2-002, 005
  - CONFIRM-003 (after GAP-22 ordering), CONFIRM-008..011 (after GAP-24 for hierarchy)
  - UPDATE-005, 007 (after GAP-29); CLOSE-006, 007
  - Tests TEST-01..TEST-10, TEST-15, TEST-17, TEST-18, TEST-26, TEST-40, TEST-41

**Tier C: blocked on protocol-semantics gaps.** Alg. 3 end-to-end (GAP-14, 15, 16), Rules 1, 3 and 4 on-chain (GAP-29, 30, 31), the whole dispute path (GAP-33..40), offline and online (GAP-41..44), closure checks (GAP-45), hierarchy (GAP-19, 21, 23, 24), no-signed rounds (GAP-26, 46). See the list below.
